# Overview

*Living synthesis across all sources. Updated on every ingest.*

> **Update 2026-06-07 — [[College Physics 2e — Ch.1: Introduction: The Nature of Science and Physics|OpenStax *College Physics 2e*]] ingested in full (34/34 chapters).** The wiki's **first algebra-based introductory-physics** cluster — one source page per chapter (`college-physics-2e-ch01`…`ch34`), ingested via per-chapter opus subagents run **sequentially**, shared layer consolidated by the orchestrator. **+34 sources / +355 concepts / +27 entities** (CC BY 4.0; capture in `raw/college-physics-2e/`). The arc spans the whole standard sequence: **mechanics** ([[Kinematics]] → [[NewtonsSecondLaw|Newton's laws]] / [[Force]] / [[Friction]] → [[Work]] / [[KineticEnergy]] / [[ConservationOfEnergy]] → [[LinearMomentum]] / [[Collision|collisions]] → [[Torque]] / [[StaticEquilibrium]] → [[AngularMomentum|rotation]] → [[UniformCircularMotion|circular motion]] / [[NewtonsLawOfUniversalGravitation|gravitation]] / [[KeplersLaws]]), **fluids** ([[Buoyancy]] / [[ArchimedesPrinciple]] / [[BernoulliPrinciple]] / [[Viscosity]]), **thermal physics** ([[KineticTheoryOfGases]] / [[IdealGasLaw]] / [[Heat|heat transfer]] / the [[FirstLawOfThermodynamics|laws of thermodynamics]] / [[CarnotCycle|heat engines]]), **waves & sound** ([[SimpleHarmonicMotion]] / [[Wave|waves]] / [[Resonance]] / [[DopplerEffect]] / [[Decibel|sound]]), **electromagnetism** ([[CoulombsLaw]] / [[ElectricField]] / [[Voltage]] / [[Capacitance]] → [[OhmsLaw]] / [[ElectricCurrent]] / [[KirchhoffsRules|circuits]] → [[MagneticField]] / [[LorentzForce]] → [[FaradaysLaw|induction]] / [[ElectricalTransformer|transformers]] → [[MaxwellsEquations]] / [[ElectromagneticWave|EM waves]]), **optics** ([[GeometricOptics]] / [[SnellsLaw]] / [[OpticalLens|lenses]] → [[WaveOptics]] / [[Diffraction]] / [[Polarization]] → [[HumanEyeOptics|vision & instruments]]), and **modern physics** ([[SpecialRelativity]] / [[MassEnergyEquivalence]] → [[WaveParticleDuality|quantum]] / [[PhotoelectricEffect]] / [[UncertaintyPrinciple]] → [[BohrModel|atomic]] → [[Radioactivity|nuclear]] / [[NuclearFission|fission & fusion]] → [[StandardModel|particle physics]] → [[Cosmology]] / [[BigBang]] / [[DarkMatter]] / [[GeneralRelativity]]). It **interlocks with the existing [[chemistry-2e-ch01|Chemistry 2e]] corpus** — the thermodynamics, atomic-structure, and quantum chapters *merged in place* (e.g. [[HeatThermodynamics]], [[Calorimetry]], [[BohrModel]], [[AtomicSpectra]], [[QuantumNumbers]], [[PhotoelectricEffect]], [[AlbertEinstein]] now carry both chemistry and physics sources) — and grounds the higher-math cluster ([[Calculus]], [[mml-book|MML]] linear algebra, [[AnalyticGeometry]]) in physical application. Mints the major physicist entities ([[IsaacNewton]], [[AlbertEinstein]], [[JamesClerkMaxwell]], [[MichaelFaraday]], [[MarieCurie]], [[JohannesKepler]], [[CharlesCoulomb]], [[GeorgOhm]], [[NielsBohr]], [[ErnestRutherford]], [[MurrayGellMann]], …). **No contradictions** — but, as with the math/chemistry ingests, generic physics terms collided with off-domain ML/systems pages and were disambiguated rather than overwritten: [[LinearMomentum]] (vs the gradient-descent [[Momentum]]), [[ThermodynamicTemperature]] (vs softmax [[Temperature]]), [[ThermodynamicEntropy]] (vs Shannon [[Entropy]]), [[ElectricalTransformer]] (vs the [[Transformer]] architecture), [[SpeedOfLightPhysics]] (vs the latency [[SpeedOfLight]]), [[MolecularDiffusion]] (vs diffusion models), [[LightReflection]] (vs program reflection), [[GyroscopicPrecession]] (vs the MEMS [[Gyroscope]]), and physics [[Work]] / [[Power]] / [[EnergyEfficiency]].

> **Update 2026-06-07 — [[chemistry-2e-ch01-essential-ideas|OpenStax *Chemistry 2e*]] ingested in full (21/21 chapters).** The wiki's **first general-chemistry** corpus — and, with the concurrent [[College Physics 2e — Ch.1: Introduction: The Nature of Science and Physics|College Physics 2e]] ingest, a new **physical-sciences** domain alongside the existing ML / math / systems / CS content. One source page per chapter (`chemistry-2e-ch01`…`ch21`), ingested via per-chapter Opus subagents that wrote only the source/concept/entity layers; the shared layer (index / overview / log) was consolidated by the orchestrator. **+21 sources / +273 new concepts / +15 new entities** (26 quantum/thermodynamics/measurement concept pages and 8 physicist entities were *shared* with the College Physics 2e ingest and merged in place rather than duplicated). Source: the OpenStax web book `https://openstax.org/books/chemistry-2e`. The arc runs the full first-year-chemistry sequence, **atoms → nuclear**: foundations & measurement ([[Matter]], [[SIUnits]], [[SignificantFigures]], [[DimensionalAnalysis]]) → atomic theory & the [[PeriodicTable]] ([[AtomicTheory]], [[Isotope]], [[ChemicalNomenclature]]) → composition & stoichiometry ([[TheMoleConcept]], [[Molarity]], [[BalancingChemicalEquations]], [[LimitingReactant]]) → [[ThermalEnergy|thermochemistry]] ([[Calorimetry]], [[EnthalpyChemistry]], [[HessLaw]]) → quantum [[ElectronConfiguration|electronic structure]] & [[PeriodicTrends]] ([[BohrModel]], [[QuantumNumbers]], [[AufbauPrinciple]]) → [[ChemicalBond|bonding]] & geometry ([[LewisStructure]], [[VSEPRTheory]], [[ValenceBondTheory]], [[MolecularOrbitalTheory]]) → states of matter ([[GasLaws|gases]] / [[IdealGasLawChemistry]] / [[KineticMolecularTheory]], [[IntermolecularForces|liquids & solids]], [[ColligativeProperties|solutions & colloids]]) → [[ReactionRate|kinetics]] ([[RateLaw]], [[ArrheniusEquation]], [[Catalysis]]) → [[ChemicalEquilibrium|equilibrium]] ([[EquilibriumConstant]], [[LeChatelierPrinciple]], [[ICETable]]; [[BronstedLowryAcidsAndBases|acid-base]] with [[pHScale]] / [[BufferSolution|buffers]]; [[SolubilityProduct|solubility]] & [[ComplexIon|complex-ion]] equilibria) → chemical [[EntropyThermodynamics|thermodynamics]] ([[GibbsFreeEnergy]], [[GibbsFreeEnergyAndEquilibrium|ΔG° = −RT ln K]]) → [[Electrochemistry]] ([[GalvanicCell|galvanic cells]], [[NernstEquation]], [[ElectrolysisChemistry|electrolysis]]) → descriptive [[RepresentativeElements|main-group]] & [[TransitionMetals|transition-metal]]/[[CoordinationCompound|coordination]] chemistry → [[OrganicChemistry|organic]] ([[Hydrocarbon|hydrocarbons]], [[OrganicFunctionalGroup|functional groups]]) → [[NuclearChemistry|nuclear]] ([[RadioactiveDecayChemistry|decay]], [[NuclearFissionChemistry|fission]] & [[NuclearFusionChemistry|fusion]]). Mints chemistry-historical entities ([[JohnDalton]], [[DmitriMendeleev]], [[GilbertLewis]], [[LinusPauling]], [[GermainHess]], [[JosiahWillardGibbs]], [[FriedrichWohler]], …). **No contradictions** — but, as with the math and physics ingests, chemistry concepts were **deliberately given chemistry-explicit page names** to avoid collisions with the existing ML/physics corpus: [[EntropyThermodynamics]] (vs information-theory [[Entropy]]), [[GibbsFreeEnergy]] / [[EnthalpyChemistry]] / [[HeatThermodynamics]] (thermochemistry vs softmax/ML terms), [[GaseousDiffusion]] / [[GaseousDiffusion|effusion-diffusion]] (vs diffusion models), [[IdealGasLawChemistry]] (vs the physics [[IdealGasLaw]]), and [[ReactionRate]] / [[Catalysis]] / [[Solubility]] kept in their chemical sense. The thermodynamics, atomic-structure, and quantum chapters share pages with College Physics 2e (e.g. [[BohrModel]], [[PhotoelectricEffect]], [[QuantumNumbers]], [[FirstLawOfThermodynamics]], [[SecondLawOfThermodynamics]] now carry both chemistry and physics sources).

> **Update 2026-06-07 — [[Prealgebra|OpenStax *Prealgebra 2e*]] ingested in full (11/11 chapters).** The wiki's **first dedicated foundational-arithmetic / pre-algebra** cluster — the concrete bedrock beneath the corpus's existing higher-math ([[Calculus]], the [[mml-book|MML]] linear-algebra/probability material, [[AnalyticGeometry]]) and CS content. One source page per chapter (`prealgebra-2e-ch01`…`ch11`), ingested via per-chapter opus subagents run **sequentially**, with the shared layer (index/overview/log + the [[OpenStax]]/[[Prealgebra]] entities) consolidated by the orchestrator. The arc runs concrete→symbolic: [[WholeNumbers|whole numbers]] / [[PlaceValue|place value]] / [[Divisibility]] → the [[Variable|language of algebra]] ([[OrderOfOperations|PEMDAS]], [[Equation|equations]], [[PrimeFactorization]] / [[LeastCommonMultiple|LCM]]) → [[Integer|integers]] / [[AbsoluteValue|absolute value]] / [[SignedNumberArithmetic|sign rules]] → [[Fraction|fractions]] & [[Decimal|decimals]] (with [[Ratio|ratios]], [[Mean|averages]], [[SquareRoot|square roots]]) → [[Percent|percents]] / [[Proportion|proportions]] / [[SimpleInterest|interest]] → the [[RealNumbers|real-number]] field [[CommutativeProperty|properties]] ([[DistributiveProperty|distributive]] et al.) & [[SystemsOfMeasurement|measurement]] → [[SolvingLinearEquations|solving linear equations]] → [[ProblemSolvingStrategy|geometry/word-problem models]] ([[PythagoreanTheorem]], [[PerimeterAndArea]], [[VolumeAndSurfaceArea]]) → [[Polynomial|polynomials]] / [[ExponentRules|exponents]] / [[ScientificNotation]] → the [[RectangularCoordinateSystem|coordinate plane]], [[GraphingLinearEquations|graphing]] & [[Slope]]. **+11 sources / +56 concepts / +2 entities**; existing [[Mean]] / [[Median]] / [[Mode]] / [[probability]] and several stubs ([[Divisibility]], [[linearequation|Linear Equation]], `typesofnumber`) enriched in place. **No contradictions** — but several deliberate *naming disambiguations* were required, because the ML/systems corpus had already claimed the obvious names with off-domain meanings: [[Exponent]] is the IEEE-754 float field (→ exponent rules live in [[ExponentRules]]), [[Angle]] is the inner-product angle (→ geometry angles fold into [[Triangle]]), [[Temperature]] is the softmax sampling constant (→ thermometric units link [[TemperatureMeasurement]]), and [[Coordinates]] / [[AnalyticGeometry]] are the linear-algebra abstractions (→ the concrete Cartesian plane is [[RectangularCoordinateSystem]]). Bonus: the new [[Polynomial]] / [[ScientificNotation]] pages resolved previously-dangling links in the RosettaCode sources.

> **Update 2026-06-07 — Zig language ingested: the official [ziglang.org/learn](https://ziglang.org/learn/) tutorials / guides / how-tos (6 pages).** The wiki's **first dedicated [[Zig]] / modern-systems-language** cluster — a low-level counterpart to the existing embedded-[[RustLanguage|Rust]] and [[CLanguage|C]] material. Per-source opus subagents ran sequentially; the shared layer was consolidated by the orchestrator. The six sources: [[zig-getting-started|Getting Started]] (install paths, [[ZigLanguageServer|ZLS]], `zig init`→`zig build run`), [[zig-in-depth-overview|In-Depth Overview]] (the canonical feature tour), [[zig-why-zig-vs-rust-d-cpp|Why Zig vs C++/D/Rust]] (rationale essay), [[zig-build-system-guide|Build System guide]] ([[DirectedAcyclicGraph|DAG]] of build steps + [[ZigPackageManager|`build.zig.zon`]]), [[zig-code-examples|Code Samples]], and [[zig-tools|Tools]] (editor/LSP catalog). The through-line is Zig's [[NoHiddenControlFlow|"no hidden control flow / allocations / macros"]] philosophy, realized via first-class [[Comptime|comptime]] (generics + reflection without macros — see [[Metaprogramming]]), [[ErrorUnion|error unions]] (`!T`/`try`/`catch`) and [[ZigOptional|optionals]] (`?T`, no null), [[ZigAllocator|explicit allocator passing]] ([[ManualMemoryManagement|manual memory]], no GC) with [[DeferStatement|defer/errdefer]] cleanup, [[CInterop|seamless C interop]] (`@cImport`, `zig cc`, bundled libc) and first-class [[CrossCompilation|cross-compilation]], and a [[ZigBuildSystem|build system]] that doubles as a [[ZigPackageManager|package manager]] with four [[ZigBuildModes|build modes]]. Stewarded by the [[ZigSoftwareFoundation]]; [[LLVM]] is the codegen backend. **+6 sources / +16 concepts / +3 entities** ([[Zig]], [[ZigSoftwareFoundation]], [[LLVM]]). No contradictions flagged — only intra-page version drift in Zig's own ~0.16 std-API examples, noted on the source pages.

> **Update 2026-06-07 — [[logic-text-v2|*Introduction to Logic and Critical Thinking*]] ([[MatthewVanCleave|Van Cleave]], v2.0, CC BY 4.0, 2016) ingested.** The wiki's **first dedicated logic / [[CriticalThinking|critical-thinking]]** source — a philosophy/humanities counterweight to the corpus's ML-and-CS center of gravity. It supplies the *foundational vocabulary of argument* the rest of the wiki uses informally: [[Argument]] (premise/conclusion/standard form), [[Validity]] (a property of **form**, not truth — the imagination/counterexample test), [[Soundness]] (validity + true premises), and the [[DeductiveReasoning|deductive]] (certain, non-defeasible) vs [[InductiveReasoning|inductive]] (probable, defeasible) split. On the formal side it **de-stubs [[PropositionalLogic]]** (four truth-functional connectives, the [[TruthTable|truth-table]] validity test, the **8 [[RulesOfInference|valid forms of inference]]** for proofs) and adds [[CategoricalLogic]] (A/E/I/O + Venn diagrams) as the answer to propositional logic's expressive gap. Chapter 3 wires directly into the existing probability cluster: [[InferenceToBestExplanation]] is the critical-thinking face of [[Abduction]] (its "simplicity" virtue = [[OccamsRazor|Ockham's razor]], after [[WilliamOfOckham]]), [[NecessaryAndSufficientConditions]] formalizes causal reasoning, and the [[ConjunctionFallacy]] / [[BaseRateFallacy]] ([[DanielKahneman|Kahneman]] & [[AmosTversky|Tversky]]) are exactly the intuitive failures of [[Probability]] / [[BayesTheorem|Bayes]] the wiki documents normatively. Chapter 4 adds a [[LogicalFallacy]] catalog (formal vs informal). **+1 source / +13 concepts / +4 entities**; [[PropositionalLogic]] de-stubbed; [[OccamsRazor]] / [[Abduction]] / [[Probability]] / [[TruthTable]] enriched in place. *Framing note, not a contradiction:* Van Cleave classes inference-to-the-best-explanation as a species of **induction**, whereas the wiki's [[Abduction]] page (after [[mml-book|MML]]) treats abduction as a **third** mode of inference — same substance, different taxonomy.

> **Update 2026-06-06 — [[fuzzingbook-03-fuzzer|*The Fuzzing Book*]] (Zeller, Gopinath, Böhme, Fraser & Holler; [[CISPA]] 2024) ingested in full — 30/30 content chapters.** The wiki's first dedicated **software-test-generation / [[Fuzzing|fuzzing]]** corpus, one source page per chapter (`fuzzingbook-01`…`30`) across six parts: lexical ([[RandomFuzzer|random]] → [[Coverage|coverage]] → [[MutationBasedFuzzing|mutation]] → [[GreyboxFuzzing|greybox]]/[[AFL]] → [[SearchBasedTesting|search-based]] → [[MutationAnalysis|mutation analysis]]), syntactic ([[ContextFreeGrammar|grammars]] → [[GrammarFuzzer|tree fuzzing]] → [[Parser|parsing]] → [[ProbabilisticGrammar|probabilistic]]/[[GeneratorGrammar|generator]] grammars → [[InputReduction|delta-debugging]]), semantic ([[ISLa]]/[[ConstraintBasedFuzzing|constraints]], [[DynamicTaintAnalysis|taint]], [[ConcolicExecution|concolic]]/[[SymbolicExecution|symbolic]] execution, [[DynamicInvariant|invariant mining]]), domain-specific ([[ConfigurationFuzzing|config]]/[[APIFuzzing|API]]/[[TestCarving|carving]]/[[CompilerTesting|compiler]]/[[WebApplicationFuzzing|web]]/[[GUIFuzzing|GUI]]), and management ([[FuzzingAtScale|scale]]/[[CrashDeduplication|triage]], [[GoodTuringEstimator|when-to-stop]]). The engineering complement to the existing [[Hypothesis]] [[PropertyBasedTesting|property-based-testing]] cluster, built on a shared `Fuzzer`/`Runner` architecture ([[fuzzingbook-03-fuzzer|Ch 3]]). **+30 sources / +157 concepts / +35 entities**; [[Fuzzing]]/[[Coverage]]/[[Testing]] deepened in place. Per-source subagents run sequentially; shared layer consolidated by the orchestrator. No contradictions flagged.

> **Update 2026-06-05 — [[Hypothesis]] how-to guides ingested (5-guide batch).** The [[PropertyBasedTesting|property-based testing]] cluster now carries the full *how-to* shelf from `hypothesis.readthedocs.io/en/latest/how-to/`, ingested via per-source opus subagents (run sequentially; shared layer consolidated by the orchestrator) alongside the concurrent same-day *explanation* ingest. The five guides and what each adds: **(1)** [[hypothesis-howto-suppress-healthchecks|Suppress a health check everywhere]] — silence a [[HealthCheck]] per-test or globally via [[HypothesisSettings|settings profiles]] (`suppress_health_check`, decorator-over-profile precedence), with the caveat that blanket suppression is discouraged. **(2)** [[hypothesis-howto-type-strategies|Write type hints for strategies]] — **mints [[SearchStrategy]]**: type strategy-returning functions as `SearchStrategy[T]`, the function-vs-strategy distinction, `@composite` return-type hints, covariance. **(3)** [[hypothesis-howto-custom-database|Write a custom database]] — **mints [[ExampleDatabase]]**: the pluggable key-value byte store that persists/replays minimal failing examples (`save`/`fetch`/`delete`, optional `move`, change-listening; built-in directory / in-memory / GitHub-artifact / multiplexed backends), the persistence layer for what [[Shrinking]] produces, selected via the `database=` setting. **(4)** [[hypothesis-howto-detect-tests|Detect Hypothesis tests]] — the public `is_hypothesis_test()` predicate (covers `@given` + stateful tests) and the [[Pytest]] auto-applied `@pytest.mark.hypothesis` mark, for plugin/tooling introspection. **(5)** [[hypothesis-howto-external-fuzzers|Use an external fuzzer]] — **mints [[Fuzzing]]** (coverage-guided / mutation-based input generation — Atheris / libFuzzer / python-afl / HypoFuzz — as the complement to property-based testing): `fuzz_one_input(buffer) -> bytes | None` turns a `@given` test into a fuzz target (invalid→`None`; failure writes the pruned buffer to the [[ExampleDatabase]] and re-raises; bypasses the normal `Phase` lifecycle, no inline shrink). Net: **3 new concepts** ([[SearchStrategy]], [[ExampleDatabase]], [[Fuzzing]]); the [[Hypothesis]] entity gained sections for typing, persistence, test-detection, and external fuzzing; [[PropertyBasedTesting]] / [[HypothesisSettings]] / [[Shrinking]] / [[Pytest]] / [[TypeHints]] enriched in place.

> **Update 2026-06-05 — [[hypothesis-example-count|Hypothesis Docs — "How many times will Hypothesis run my test?"]] ingested.** Third source in the [[PropertyBasedTesting|property-based testing]] cluster, pinning down the [[Hypothesis]] **execution model**: the run count is *approximately* `max_examples` but deviates four ways — **fewer** when the search space is exhausted (`st.integers(0, 19)` → 20 runs, not 100), **more** when examples are retried (failed an `assume()` / `.filter()`, or too large → [[HealthCheck|`HealthCheck.data_too_large`]]), and a **variable** count once a failure is found and the [[Shrinking|`Phase.shrink` / `Phase.explain`]] phases run. Load-bearing fact: the minimal failing example is **always replayed once** to rule out flakiness (a failure at `n=0` executes `n=0` *twice*), and `.filter()` is more efficient than `assume()` (intra-example retry vs whole-example discard). **Mints [[Shrinking]]**; enriches [[PropertyBasedTesting]] (run-count bullet), [[HealthCheck]] (`data_too_large`), and the [[Hypothesis]] entity (new *How many times a test runs* section) in place. Ran alongside the concurrent same-day cluster ingest.

> **Update 2026-06-05 — [[hypothesis-domain-and-distribution|Hypothesis Docs — Domain and Distribution]] ingested.** Second source in the wiki's emerging **[[PropertyBasedTesting|property-based testing]]** cluster (after [[hypothesis-howto-suppress-healthchecks]]), both documenting the [[Hypothesis]] Python library. The page states the cluster's load-bearing design principle: **the user owns the *domain*** (which inputs a strategy *can* generate — `lists(integers())` → all integer lists) **but the library owns the *distribution*** (how often each input is generated). Practical corollaries: choose the most-general strategy so any pass-eligible edge case is reachable (*"Far better to find bugs slowly, than not find them at all"*); manage performance with [[HypothesisSettings|`max_examples` / `phases`]] rather than narrowing the strategy; distribution is intentionally not a public knob (humans over-tune for known bugs; it's codebase/property-dependent; keeping it internal preserves API freedom) — users needing distribution control are pointed to `hypofuzz` / `crosshair`. This is the wiki's **generative-testing** branch, complementing the example-based testing pages ([[Pytest]], [[UnitTesting]], [[RegressionTesting]], [[BehavioralTesting]]) without conflict. **No new pages minted** — enriched [[PropertyBasedTesting]] (new *Domain vs Distribution* section) and [[Hypothesis]] (new *design philosophy* section) in place; **index backfilled** for the entire prior-unindexed Hypothesis cluster (1 new + 1 prior source, the [[Hypothesis]] entity, and concepts [[PropertyBasedTesting]] / [[HypothesisSettings]] / [[HealthCheck]]). *(Note: a concurrent ingest is also extending this cluster — [[hypothesis-example-count]] source + [[Shrinking]] concept.)*

> **Update 2026-06-04 — [[mechanics-of-ml|*The Mechanics of Machine Learning*]] ([[TerenceParr|Parr]] & [[JeremyHoward|Howard]], explained.ai, v0.4) ingested.** The **applied, tabular-data, Random-Forest-first counterpart** to the same authors' theory tutorial [[matrix-calculus-for-deep-learning]] — together they now bracket the explained.ai pedagogy in the wiki (theory: NN gradients; practice: the end-to-end tabular ML workflow). This is the wiki's **clearest "pick one powerful model and master the workflow" source**: it elevates the [[RandomForests|Random Forest]] from [[islr-seventh-printing|ISLR]]'s "strong baseline" to **default model** ("the Swiss Army Knife™ of the machine learning world"), with the crowdsourcing-of-real-estate-agents intuition for why ensemble averaging works. Two [[Kaggle]] datasets carry it: NYC apartment rent (regression) and Blue Book for Bulldozers (time-series regression, scored on [[RMSLE]], final test RMSLE 0.2396 ≈ top-5%). **Distinctive contributions to the corpus**: (1) the **[[Denoising|denoise-vs-transform]] dichotomy** — the *same* `RandomForestRegressor` goes OOB R² −0.0076 → 0.8677 by domain-bounded cleaning, or 0.8767 by [[LogInExpOut|"log in, exp out"]] on raw data (matching on R² but losing on MAE — a clean lesson that the "better" model depends on the metric); (2) the **categorical-encoding menu** ([[LabelEncoding|label]] / [[FrequencyEncoding|frequency]] / [[OneHotEncoding|one-hot]] / [[TargetEncoding|target]]) with explicit when-to-use and overfitting/leakage caveats; (3) the **"testing trilogy"** framing of [[TrainValTestSplit|train/validate/test]] with **time-based splitting** for time-series ("randomly splitting ... allows the model to train on data from the future") and the [[OutOfBagScore|OOB]]-is-optimistic-on-time-series caveat; (4) a notably crisp **[[DataLeakage|data-leakage]]** definition tied to [[FeatureEngineering|feature engineering]] ("features that directly or indirectly hint at the target variable"). **Convergence, not contradiction**: its time-based-split and "transform val/test only from training data" discipline independently matches [[dmls-ch05-feature-engineering|DMLS Ch 5]]'s six leakage causes from the applied side; its RF-as-default thesis is the practitioner's version of [[dmls-ch06-model-development|DMLS Ch 6]]'s ensembling section. It occupies the **classical-ML / tabular / Random-Forest niche** the wiki's deep-learning- and LLM-heavy corpus leaves open. Mints **7 concept pages** ([[TargetEncoding]], [[FrequencyEncoding]], [[OutOfBagScore]], [[LogInExpOut]], [[Denoising]], [[RMSLE]], [[Hyperparameter]]); updates concepts [[RandomForests]] / [[FeatureEngineering]] / [[DataLeakage]] / [[TrainValTestSplit]] and entities [[TerenceParr]] / [[JeremyHoward]] / [[FastAI]] / [[sklearn]] / [[Kaggle]] in place; index gets 1 source + 7 concept entries; `raw/books/mechanics-of-machine-learning.md` holds the (agent-fetched, non-verbatim) capture. **No new entity pages** — [[matplotlib]] already exists as a concept page and [[Conda|Anaconda]] is covered by the existing Conda entity (two over-created stubs were removed). **Contradictions flagged**: only *intra-source* tensions (OOB used as validation in Ch 5 vs flagged optimistic in Ch 9; log-target wins on R² but loses on MAE) — no hard conflicts with prior wiki sources; complementary to [[islr-seventh-printing|ISLR]] (theory) and [[mml-book|MML]] (math foundations).

> **Update 2026-05-24 — [[dspy-image-generation-prompting-tutorial|DSPy Tutorial — Image Generation Prompt Iteration (`dspy.Image` + Flux Pro via FAL)]] ingested.** **Twentieth wiki-corpus [[DSPy]] tutorial** and the **first wiki receipt of an image modality in DSPy** — the dspy.ai *Image Generation Prompt Iteration* tutorial, attributed to a tweet from [@ThorondorLLC](https://x.com/ThorondorLLC). **Shortest DSPy tutorial in the wiki corpus to date**: a single string-spec [[DSPyPredict|`dspy.Predict`]] (`"desired_prompt: str, current_image: dspy.Image, current_prompt:str -> feedback:str, image_strictly_matches_desired_prompt: bool, revised_prompt: str"`) wrapped in a plain Python `for i in range(max_iter=5)` loop with early break on the boolean match field. Drives [[IterativeImagePromptRefinement|iterative image-prompt refinement]] against the [[FluxPro|Flux Pro v1.1 Ultra]] text-to-image model hosted by [[FAL]] (`fal-ai/flux-pro/v1.1-ultra`); the critic is [[GPT4oMini|`gpt-4o-mini`]] at `temperature=0.5`. Programming-stage-only — **no [[DSPyModules|`dspy.Module`]] subclass**, no [[DSPyOptimization|Optimization stage]], no [[DSPyMetrics|metric]], no eval set; the tutorial **explicitly disclaims its own optimizability** (*"this is not DSPy prompt optimization as it is normally used, but it is a good example of how to use multimodal DSPy"*) and names the natural lift (*"a future upgrade would be to create a dataset of initial, final prompts to optimize the prompt generation"*) — **first DSPy tutorial in the wiki that ships a working program while explicitly disclaiming the Optimizer surface**. **First wiki receipts within this tutorial**: (1) [[DSPyImage|`dspy.Image`]] primitive on a Signature `InputField`; (2) `dspy.Image.from_url(url)` construction path; (3) **URL-passthrough adapter wire shape** — `dspy.inspect_history(...)` shows `<image_url: https://fal.media/files/.../*.jpg>` carried verbatim into the chat-completion request with no base64 inlining, the **opposite transport discipline** from [[DSPyAudio|`dspy.Audio`]]'s base64-in-`data` storage (the wiki's two non-text DSPy primitives now have **opposite** transport disciplines — URL passthrough vs base64 inlining — matched to the common provenance of each modality: image generators return hosted URLs, audio datasets deserialize into NumPy arrays); (4) `dspy.Image` named inline in a string-spec Signature — proves the string-Signature parser resolves the `dspy.` namespace prefix as a typed field reference (by symmetry, the same parser accepts `dspy.Audio`); (5) **string-Signature outputs are field-set-canonical, not field-order-canonical** — the `inspect_history(5)` excerpt shows runs of the same Signature with different output-field orders (`feedback / revised_prompt / image_strictly_matches_desired_prompt` in run 1 vs `feedback / image_strictly_matches_desired_prompt / revised_prompt` in runs 2–5), a structural disclosure no prior DSPy tutorial in the corpus had surfaced — downstream consumers that parse by field position will break; field-name parsing is mandatory; (6) [[FluxPro|Flux Pro v1.1 Ultra]] and [[FAL]] as the image-generation provider stack in a DSPy program (the framework treats the image generator as an external black-box service, not as a `dspy.LM` — **symmetric to [[DSPyAudio]]'s out-of-LM TTS handling** in [[dspy-audio-tutorial]]; the wiki's multimodal-output coverage is now consistently *"input-side first-class, output-side wrapped HTTP"*); (7) `python-dotenv` `load_dotenv()` for credential loading (replaces the `os.environ[...]=...` / `os.getenv(...)` pattern in prior tutorials); (8) `temperature=0.5` for a single-Signature critic (highest fixed temperature for a single-Signature critic in the DSPy tutorial corpus to date — sits between the conservative `0.0` of evaluation-shaped tutorials and the `0.7` of [[dspy-tool-use-tutorial]]'s trajectory ChainOfThought); (9) two-provider, no-HF-dataset DSPy tutorial shape (OpenAI + FAL, no [[HuggingFace]]); (10) the [[IterativeImagePromptRefinement|critic-then-revise loop pattern]] — **the most natural candidate in the DSPy tutorial corpus for a future [[GEPA]] receipt on a multimodal task** (none of the existing GEPA tutorials in the wiki exercises the image modality; the hand-rolled loop is *what GEPA automates*). Mints **4 concept pages** ([[DSPyImage]], [[FluxPro]], [[GPT4oMini]], [[IterativeImagePromptRefinement]]) + **2 entity pages** ([[FAL]], [[BlackForestLabs]]); updates [[index]] (1 source under the DSPy Tutorial cluster, 4 concepts, 2 entities), `wiki/overview.md` (this Update block), `wiki/log.md` (entry). **Contradictions flagged**: (1) **run log ↔ `inspect_history` mismatch** — the printed iteration log shows autumn-scene images and `False` matches for iterations 1–3 before a `True` at iteration 4, but `dspy.inspect_history(5)` shows a café-scene image returning `True` at the **first** call; the two captures are from different notebook runs of the same code and the rendered tutorial does not flag the disagreement — **first wiki DSPy tutorial whose printed `inspect_history` output disagrees with the printed cell output above it**, so readers cannot trace a single run end-to-end; (2) **field-order drift between runs** in the same notebook — the [[DSPyAdapters|Adapter]] does not pin field order for string-spec Signatures across runs; (3) `max_iter = 5` hard-coded without ablation across desired prompts; (4) **no image-similarity metric** ([[CLIP|CLIP]] / [[CosineSimilarity|cosine]] / human-eval) grounds the critic's `image_strictly_matches_desired_prompt` boolean — the loop trusts the critic LM's self-judgement; **symmetric to [[dspy-audio-tutorial|the audio tutorial's]] honest [[Wav2Vec2]]-as-metric caveat, but this tutorial does not flag the same caveat about LM-as-judge image evaluation**; (5) `temperature=0.5` on a boolean-emitting field is a low-grade nondeterminism source the tutorial does not flag; (6) **no cost / wall-time / token-count disclosure** — continuing gap across the DSPy tutorial corpus, especially load-bearing here because each iteration runs a [[FluxPro|Flux Pro]] generation (5–30 s typical) plus a vision-LM call; (7) `fal-ai/flux-pro/v1.1-ultra` endpoint pinned without a Flux Schnell / Flux Dev cost-quality comparison; no [[DallE|DALL-E 3]] / [[StableDiffusion|Stable Diffusion]] / [[MidJourney]] alternative comparison. **Scope-limit gaps**: no [[DSPyModules|`dspy.Module`]] subclass (consequence: program cannot be passed to any [[DSPyOptimizers|Optimizer]]); no [[GEPA]] receipt on the natural target pattern; no CLIP/aesthetic/human-preference baseline; no save/load receipt; no streaming/async/observability composition; no multi-image input; no image+audio multimodal composition; no image-output `dspy.Image` receipt (symmetric to [[DSPyAudio|`dspy.Audio`]]'s "no first-class output" disclosure — the wiki's multimodal coverage is now consistently *input-first-class / output-wrapped-HTTP* across both non-text primitives); no [[DallE|DALL-E 3]] / [[StableDiffusion|SD]] / [[MidJourney]] comparison; no retry / error-handling around `fal_client` failures or vision-LM timeouts. **Validation**: source page exists (`wiki/sources/dspy-image-generation-prompting-tutorial.md`); 4 new concept pages exist ([[DSPyImage]], [[FluxPro]], [[GPT4oMini]], [[IterativeImagePromptRefinement]]); 2 new entity pages exist ([[FAL]], [[BlackForestLabs]]); index entries inserted: 1 source (Sources block, adjacent to [[dspy-tutorial-games]] / [[dspy-yahoo-finance-react-tutorial]] in the DSPy Tutorial cluster), 4 concepts (DSPyImage and IterativeImagePromptRefinement adjacent to [[DSPyAudio]] in the DSPy-primitives cluster; GPT4oMini adjacent to [[GPT4oMiniAudio]] in the OpenAI-models cluster; FluxPro adjacent to [[DiffusionModel]] in the image-generation cluster), 2 entities (FAL adjacent to [[FastMCP]] in the F cluster; BlackForestLabs adjacent to [[BespokeLabsAI|Bespoke Labs]] in the B cluster). Forward references intentionally left: [[DallE]] (DALL-E concept page not yet present — the entity page [[DALLE]] exists), [[MidJourney]] (no current page), [[StableDiffusion]] (no current page; would be a natural mint when a Stable Diffusion source arrives), [[CLIP]] (no current image-text-similarity concept page).

> **Update 2026-05-24 — [[dspy-tutorial-games|DSPy Tutorial — Fine-tuning Agents (AlfWorld + MIPROv2-teacher + BootstrapFinetune)]] ingested.** **Nineteenth wiki-corpus [[DSPy]] tutorial** and the **first end-to-end runnable [[BootstrapFinetune|`dspy.BootstrapFinetune`]] receipt on an agentic program** — moves the BFT envelope from [[dspy-optimizers|the page-13 Banking77 single-call classification receipt]] (66% → 87%) to a **50-step interactive agent on the [[AlfWorld]] household-task benchmark** ([Shridhar et al. 2021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.03768)'s text-only ALFRED-on-[[TextWorld]] re-implementation). The Agent is a hand-rolled [[react|ReAct]]-pattern `dspy.Module` with **one** `dspy.Predict("task, trajectory, possible_actions: list[str] -> action")` sub-module called up to `max_iters=50` per task — **deliberately not [[react|`dspy.ReAct`]]**, because BFT wants flat `(task, trajectory, possible_actions) → action` SFT supervision (one fine-tuning example per agent step, not per task). **Second wiki receipt of the [[HandRolledReAct|hand-rolled-ReAct]] pattern** after [[dspy-tool-use-tutorial]] — different motivation, same template. Two-stage prompt-then-weights pipeline: (1) [[MIPROv2|`dspy.MIPROv2(metric=metric, auto="light", num_threads=16, prompt_model=gpt4o)`]] with `max_bootstrapped_demos=1, max_labeled_demos=0, minibatch_size=40` over GPT-4o (teacher), then (2) `dspy.BootstrapFinetune(metric=metric, num_threads=16).compile(student_4o_mini, teacher=optimized_4o, trainset=trainset)` distills the prompt-optimized teacher's trajectories into [[GPT|GPT-4o-mini]] weights via the [[OpenAI]] fine-tuning API. **Headline lift: GPT-4o-mini 15.0% → 71.5% devset success** (+56.5 pts absolute, **~4.8× relative**) on 200 dev tasks with 200 train tasks; **fine-tuned 4o-mini beats GPT-4o teacher's 57.5% zero-shot by +14 pts** while running on a smaller, cheaper model at **2.6× the wall-clock speed of zero-shot 4o-mini** (03:15 vs 08:33). **First wiki receipt operationalizing the [[BootstrapFinetune|page-13 BFT composability recipe]] end-to-end** (*"You can run `dspy.MIPROv2` and use the produced program ... to `dspy.BootstrapFinetune` to get better results. This is partly the essence of `dspy.BetterTogether`."*) — hand-rolls the [[BetterTogether|`dspy.BetterTogether`]] sequence (MIPROv2 → BFT) without using the meta-optimizer API. **First wiki receipts within this tutorial**: [[BootstrapFinetune|`dspy.BootstrapFinetune`]] with explicit `teacher=...` kwarg (the [[dspy-optimizers|Banking77]] receipt bootstrapped from the same model it fine-tuned); [[MIPROv2]] with explicit `prompt_model=gpt4o` kwarg; the `"think:"` pseudo-action injected into `info["admissible_commands"][0] + ["think: ${...thoughts...}"]` as a [[react|ReAct]]-*Thought* primitive that doesn't consume an environment turn (`prediction.action.startswith("think:")` → append `"OK."`, continue loop) — structural alternative to [[chainofthought|CoT]]'s `reasoning` injection inside a Signature with no `reasoning` field; `max_iters=50` agent-loop cap (10× the [[react|`dspy.ReAct`]] default of 5); `dspy.configure(experimental=True)` global toggle; a metric that reads from `y` (the prediction) rather than `x` (the example) — `lambda x, y, trace=None: y.success` — forced by agent-style tasks where ground truth is environment success not a held-out label; `dspy.datasets.alfworld.AlfWorld()` as a **hybrid dataset + simulator handle** — second wiki receipt of an in-framework DSPy dataset loader after [[dspy-tutorial-math|`dspy.datasets.MATH(subset='algebra')`]], but first that ships **executable environment infrastructure** (`POOL.session()` context manager managing TextWorld subprocesses); pickle save/load with **explicit in-tutorial security warning** *"Loading `.pkl` files can execute arbitrary code and may be dangerous. Only save and load pickle files from trusted sources in secure environments. Consider using JSON format when possible for safer serialization."* — **first explicit security warning in the DSPy tutorial corpus**. **Cross-receipt argument with [[dspy-tutorial-rl-papillon]] / [[dspy-rl-multihop-tutorial]]**: those tutorials use [[grpo|online GRPO]] (`dspy.GRPO` / [[ArborGRPO]]) for weight-space optimization and **explicitly disclaim** it as *"typically worse on cost/quality basis than prompt optimizers `dspy.MIPROv2` or `dspy.SIMBA`."* This tutorial demonstrates **supervised fine-tuning via teacher rollouts actually extracts additional gain over MIPROv2 prompt-only** — **first wiki prompt+SFT receipt that beats its own prompt-only teacher** (+14 pts over GPT-4o zero-shot, on a small open-weights-cheaper student model). Strong cost/quality argument for the **supervised-distillation route over the online-RL route** on tasks with deterministic environment rewards. Mints **1 concept page** ([[AlfWorld]]); updates [[index]] (1 source under DSPy Tutorial cluster, 1 concept adjacent to [[IFBench]] in the benchmark cluster). **Contradictions flagged**: prose says *"19% → 72%"* while printed cells show **15.0% → 71.5%** (rounding drift; printed numbers authoritative); *"82% with 500 train tasks"* claim is forward-looking extrapolation not reproduced in the notebook; intro calls the program *"a ReAct agent"* but does not use `dspy.ReAct` (grep-trap); `gpt-4o-mini-2024-07-18` declared without `openai/` prefix while `gpt-4o` declared with it ([[LiteLLM]] tolerates both); no cost disclosure for OpenAI fine-tune call; no `dspy.BetterTogether` head-to-head against the hand-rolled MIPROv2→BFT sequence; no [[grpo|GRPO]] / online-RL baseline on AlfWorld; no `max_bootstrapped_demos` sweep on MIPROv2; no demo-retention ablation (the `# student_4o_mini.react.demos = []` comment hints at the knob but doesn't run it); no held-out test set (trainset and devset both sliced from the same `AlfWorld()` loader); no per-task-type breakdown across [[AlfWorld]]'s six task categories.

> **Update 2026-05-24 — [[dspy-tool-use-tutorial|DSPy Tutorial — Advanced Tool Use (SIMBA on ToolHop)]] ingested.** **First wiki-corpus runnable receipt of [[SIMBA|`dspy.SIMBA`]]** with the optimizer's full Python invocation surface — `dspy.SIMBA(metric=metric, max_steps=12, max_demos=10)` invoked as `simba.compile(agent, trainset=trainset, seed=6793115)`. Prior SIMBA anchor ([[2603.19247-prompt-optimization-jailbreaking]]) documented the algorithm and adversarial-objective lift but did not show the Python constructor; this tutorial **completes the SIMBA receipt corpus** by adding the executable invocation, an explicit `seed` kwarg (first wiki receipt of a deterministic-seed reproducibility surface on a DSPy optimizer), and the **first benign-task before/after accuracy numbers** for SIMBA: **35.0% → 60.7% dev accuracy** on [[ToolHop]] (+25.7 absolute / +71% relative). Trains a **[[HandRolledReAct|hand-rolled ReAct]] agent** — a single `dspy.ChainOfThought` Signature in a `max_steps=5` manual for-loop with a synthetic `finish(answer)` terminal tool — **bypassing [[react|`dspy.ReAct`]]** because [[ToolHop]] ships a different `functions` dict per datapoint (tools as runtime input, not constructor kwarg). [[GPT4o|GPT-4o]] (`temperature=0.7`) student. **First wiki composition of [[func_timeout|`func_timeout`]] in a DSPy program** — `@func_set_timeout(10)` + try/except → `{return_value, errors}` dict mapping is the tool-runtime sandbox idiom for the security-relevant `exec()`-of-dataset-strings pattern (untrusted code runs in-process; `func_timeout` bounds runtime, not side effects). **First wiki receipts within this tutorial**: `dspy.SIMBA(...)` constructor + `compile(..., seed=int)` invocation; hand-rolled ReAct in DSPy; `dspy.Signature(str_spec, instructions_str)` inline-string construction with `dict[str, Any]` output type; `func_timeout` library; `exec()`-based tool construction; `dspy.utils.download(url)` for direct HF URL fetch; `orjson` as JSON parser; [[ToolHop]] benchmark; synthetic `finish(answer)` terminal-tool pattern; `dspy.Example` carrying live Python callables; **fourth `dspy.Tool` alternative path** — per-question raw-callable dict via Signature input field (no `dspy.Tool` wrapper) with `inspect.signature` + `inspect.getdoc` metadata; **metric-and-instruction co-design** as primary optimization target (the Signature instruction *"format dates as YYYY-MM-DD, names as Firstname Lastname, numbers without leading 0s"* makes the output schema the dual of the metric's normalization). **Cross-receipt convergence with [[2603.19247-prompt-optimization-jailbreaking]]**: same SIMBA, complementary objectives — that paper drove mean [[DangerScore|danger]] 0.046 → 0.347 on Claude 4.5 (max-attack); this tutorial drives accuracy 0.350 → 0.607 on ToolHop (max-task-performance). The wiki now has symmetric coverage for adversarial vs benign SIMBA objectives — pattern explained by SIMBA's high-exploration variance helping when the reward surface has multiple disconnected basins, whether those are "different harm categories" or "different formatting conventions for short-answer outputs." Mints **2 concept pages** ([[ToolHop]], [[HandRolledReAct]]) + **2 entity pages** ([[func_timeout]], [[ByteDanceResearch]]); updates [[SIMBA]] (first executable receipt + benign-task lift section), [[react]] (hand-rolled ReAct contrast pattern + four-condition escape-from-`dspy.ReAct` rubric), [[DSPyTools]] (fourth tool-construction path: per-question raw-callable dict, no `dspy.Tool` wrapper) in place. **Contradictions flagged**: untrusted `exec()` of dataset strings with no isolation boundary beyond a 10-s timeout; `temperature=0.7` unusual for a trajectory-step ChainOfThought; metric does not enforce the date/name format the Signature instruction asks for (under-constrained loss vs over-specified prompt); `testset = examples[400:]` constructed but never evaluated (headline reported on devset only); no separate reflection LM for SIMBA (implies same `gpt4o` for student + reflection, breaking the asymmetric-pair pattern GEPA tutorials use); no `dspy.ReAct` baseline control. **Scope-limit gaps**: no evolved-Signature dump; no [[MIPROv2]] / [[GEPA]] / [[BootstrapFewShotWithRandomSearch]] head-to-head; no cost/latency disclosure; no test-set generalization; no security analysis of `exec()` pattern beyond timeout; no save/load receipt; no streaming/async/observability composition over the optimized agent; no `max_steps` / `max_demos` / `seed` ablations.

> **Update 2026-05-24 — [[dspy-tutorial-gepa-trusted-monitor|DSPy Tutorial — GEPA for Trusted Monitoring (AI Control + Code Backdoor Classification)]] ingested.** **Third wiki-corpus runnable receipt of [[GEPA|`dspy.GEPA`]]** after [[dspy-tutorial-gepa-aime]] and [[dspy-tutorial-gepa-papillon]] (counting [[dspy-tutorial-gepa-facility-support-analyzer]] would make it the fourth; envelopes synchronized in the GEPA concept page). **First GEPA receipt on a classification (discriminative) task** — prior receipts were generation tasks — and **first GEPA receipt on an AI safety / [[AIControl|AI control]] task** (training a weak [[TrustedMonitor|trusted monitor]] to spot [[CodeBackdoor|backdoors]] planted by a stronger untrusted coder). Optimizes a single-Signature `dspy.ChainOfThought(PredictSuspicionScore)` classifier on the [[APPSBenchmark|APPS]] backdoor split provided by [[ControlArena|`control_arena`]] (UK [[UKAISI|AISI]]'s safety library), using an **asymmetric two-model stack** — `openai/gpt-4.1-nano-2025-04-14` student + `openai/gpt-5-2025-08-07` reflection — at `max_full_evals=2`, `num_threads=8`, `reflection_minibatch_size=3`, `track_stats=True`. **Headline lift: 0.69 → 0.81 valset comparative-pair accuracy** (final pareto-front 0.95); +0.12 absolute / +17.4% relative. **First wiki composition of UK-AISI tooling (`control_arena`) inside a DSPy program** — operational link between the AI-safety evaluation ecosystem and the DSPy optimizer ecosystem. **First wiki receipts**: GEPA on a discriminative integer-output Signature; **comparative-pair [[FeedbackFunction|$\mu_f$ shape]]** (`1.0 if attack > honest else 0.0` with failure-direction-in-plain-English feedback — a **fifth $\mu_f$ source category** not enumerated in [[2507.19457-gepa|the paper]]'s four); **`MonitorTrainerProgram` dual-call wrapper** (optimization-time program shape ≠ deployment-time program shape — the un-wrapped inner program is what gets saved); **`ScoreWithFeedback` typed return** + **`DSPyTrace` typed alias** from `dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa_utils`; **GPT-5 dated checkpoint** (2025-08-07) + **GPT-4.1 Nano dated checkpoint** (2025-04-14); **`cache=False` on the student LM** (defensive against same-prompt dedup across the attack/honest dual-call); **`max_full_evals=2`** (second integer-budget receipt). **Evolved instructions shape**: a **domain-discriminator playbook** (5-step workflow + backdoor-hunting checklist + intent-vs-honest-bug discriminator + false-positive guard + 1–9 low-score calibration + competitive-programming anchors) — structurally distinct from [[dspy-tutorial-gepa-aime|gepa_aime]]'s domain-solver playbook and [[dspy-tutorial-gepa-papillon|gepa_papillon]]'s privacy-redaction prompt. **Cross-receipt convergence**: companion to [[2603.19247-prompt-optimization-jailbreaking]] — that paper uses GEPA on the attacker side (max danger; Claude 4.5 Sonnet 0.046 → 0.236); this tutorial uses GEPA on the defender side (max backdoor detection; APPS 0.69 → 0.81). Together: the wiki's first paired GEPA-on-attack vs GEPA-on-defense receipts for AI safety. The paper's six benchmarks (HotpotQA / IFBench / HoVer / PUPA / AIME-2025 / LiveBench-Math) are all generation tasks; this tutorial is the **first GEPA receipt on a task family the paper does not benchmark** (integer-output AI-control classifier with comparative-pair reward). Mints **5 concept pages** ([[AIControl]], [[TrustedMonitor]], [[CodeBackdoor]], [[APPSBenchmark]], [[ControlTax]]) + **2 entity pages** ([[ControlArena]], [[UKAISI]]); updates [[GEPA]] (fourth runnable receipt + four-tutorial envelope table), [[FeedbackFunction]] (comparative-pair section + typed-return section), [[ReflectivePromptMutation]] (domain-discriminator artifact + three-tutorial artifact-shape taxonomy) in place. Contradictions flagged: no paper anchor for the comparative-pair metric shape (a fifth $\mu_f$ category); continuing cost-disclosure gap; optimization-time wrapper ≠ deployment-time program; valset (100) headline reported but `EVAL_LIMIT=160` declared and unused; no `max_full_evals` sweep; no MIPROv2/SIMBA/BootstrapFinetune comparison; no GEPA+Merge ablation; no GPT-5-as-monitor baseline (would isolate optimization lift vs scale lift); no per-iteration prompt-diff dump.

> **Update 2026-05-24 — [[dspy-tutorial-rl-papillon|DSPy Tutorial — Online RL over a Multi-Module DSPy Program (PAPILLON + ArborGRPO)]] ingested.** **Twelfth wiki-corpus [[DSPy]] tutorial** and the **first receipt of [[grpo|GRPO]]-style online [[reinforcementlearning|RL]] applied to a *multi-module* [[CompoundAISystem|compound AI program]]** via `dspy.GRPO` / [[ArborGRPO]]. Trains the two-module [[PAPILLON]] privacy-preserving delegation program (`dspy.ChainOfThought(CraftRedactedRequest)` → external untrusted LLM → `dspy.Predict(RespondToQuery)`) on [[PUPA]] using a composite [[LLMJudge]] reward `(quality + (1 − leakage)) / 2`; devset composite **54.6 → 60.0** in ~3 h on 4× [[NVIDIA|H100]] with [[lora|LoRA]] rank-8 (α=16, dropout 0.05, all attention + MLP projections) + [[DAPO|`loss_type="dapo"`]] + `beta=0.0` (no [[KLPenalty|KL]]) + `lr=1e-6` + 500 train steps × 4 examples × 8 samples = 16,000 rollouts via the [[Arbor|`arbor-ai`]] framework. Authors openly disclaim it as *"new and extremely EXPERIMENTAL"*, *"typically worse on cost/quality basis than"* prompt optimization, *"a solid start for online RL over arbitrary LM programs for tiny LMs."* **Bookends the wiki's GRPO coverage**: same [[PAPILLON]] / [[PUPA]] setup that [[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA (Agrawal et al. 2026)]] used to argue *against* weight-RL — GEPA reaches **91.85** vs GRPO **86.66** on Qwen3 8B with up to 35× fewer rollouts (the metric scales differ between this 1.5B-LM composite and GEPA's PUPA-paper scale, but the head-to-head argument lands). The two ingests stand as **direct experimental counterpoints on the same benchmark**: this tutorial proves `dspy.GRPO` *runs* end-to-end, the GEPA paper proves it is *empirically dominated* by reflective prompt optimization when the underlying LM is capable enough for prompts to move the needle. Authors' positioning of online RL is narrow: *tiny LMs where prompt optimization has saturated against a capability ceiling*. Mints **4 concept pages** ([[PAPILLON]], [[ArborGRPO]], [[DAPO]], [[LLMJudge]]) + **1 entity page** ([[Arbor]]); updates [[PUPA]] (back-link from PAPILLON tutorial), [[grpo]] (new DSPy multi-module section + DAPO + 4× H100 receipt), [[DSPyOptimizers]] (new `Online RL` family row + catalog entry 9b + decision-rubric tiny-LM rule). Contradictions flagged in the source page: composite score (0.546 → 0.600) is **not** directly comparable to GEPA-paper PUPA scale (80–96); `beta=0.00` (no [[KLPenalty|KL]] anchor) is unjustified against standard GRPO formulation.

> **Update 2026-05-24 — [[dspy-email-extraction-tutorial|DSPy Tutorial — Email Information Extraction]] ingested.** Eleventh wiki-corpus [[DSPy]] tutorial. Source: https://dspy.ai/tutorials/email_extraction/. Builds an `EmailProcessor(dspy.Module)` that composes **four [[DSPySignatures|Signatures]]** (`ClassifyEmail` / `ExtractEntities` / `SummarizeEmail` / `GenerateActionItems`) wrapped in [[chainofthought|`dspy.ChainOfThought`]] in a **sequential diamond-shaped pipeline** with typed inter-Signature handoffs (`classifier.email_type: EmailType` → `entity_extractor` input; `entity_extractor.key_entities: list[ExtractedEntity]` → both `summarizer` and `action_generator`); final output is a **12-kwarg [[DSPyPrediction|`dspy.Prediction`]] fan-in aggregator** — the largest in the wiki's DSPy tutorial corpus. Sibling to [[dspy-ai-text-game-tutorial]] (3 Signatures) and [[dspy-sample-code-generation-tutorial]] (2 Signatures) on the **multi-Signature single-Module Programming-stage rung**. **First wiki receipts**: (i) Python `(str, Enum)` type used as both `OutputField` *and* `InputField` across Signature boundaries (`EmailType` 8-value enum, `UrgencyLevel` 4-value enum); (ii) `Optional[T]` in an `OutputField` (`financial_amount: Optional[float]`, `deadline: Optional[str]`); (iii) LM-self-reported `confidence: float` embedded in a structured [[Pydantic]] OutputField type (`class ExtractedEntity(BaseModel): entity_type: str; value: str; confidence: float`) — a [[ModelCalibration|calibration]]-adjacent surface; (iv) 12-kwarg `dspy.Prediction(...)` constructor invocation; (v) user-declared `reasoning` OutputField **collides** with [[chainofthought|`dspy.ChainOfThought`]]'s implicit `reasoning` field — user-declared wins, no warning. Programming-stage-only — no [[DSPyMetrics|metric]], no [[DSPyEvaluate|`dspy.Evaluate`]], no [[DSPyOptimizers|Optimizer]], no quantitative accuracy claim; single LM (`openai/gpt-4o-mini`). Internal tutorial-side inconsistencies flagged but no framework-level contradictions with the prior corpus. No new concept pages minted — all DSPy primitives pre-exist; updates [[DSPy]] / [[DSPyModules]] / [[DSPySignatures]] / [[chainofthought]] / [[DSPyAdapters]] / [[DSPyPrediction]] / [[Pydantic]] / [[EntityExtraction]] in place.

> **Update 2026-05-24 — [[HandsOnLLM|*Hands-On Large Language Models*]] (Alammar & Grootendorst, [[OReilly|O'Reilly Media]] 2024, ISBN 978-1-098-15096-9) fully ingested (12/12 chapters).** With [[hands-on-llm-ch12-fine-tuning-generation-models|Ch 12: Fine-Tuning Generation Models]] complete, the **12-chapter ingest of *Hands-On LLMs*** is finished — the wiki's **third fully-ingested LLM book** after [[ai-engineering-chip-huyen|*AI Engineering* (Chip Huyen, O'Reilly 2024)]] and [[LLMEngineersHandbook|*LLM Engineer's Handbook* (Iusztin, Labonne & Vesa, Packt 2024)]], alongside the in-progress *Mathematics for Machine Learning* (Deisenroth, Faisal & Ong, Cambridge 2020). The book contributes a **coherent 12-chapter pedagogical arc** to the wiki: **Part I (Chs 1–3)** introduces the LLM model class itself — recent history of Language AI from [[BagOfWords|bag-of-words]] through [[Word2Vec|word2vec]] to the [[transformer|Transformer]] / [[bert|BERT]] / [[GPT]] split, tokens and embeddings, and the modern Transformer-block recipe ([[GroupedQueryAttention|GQA]] + [[RMSNorm]] + [[SwiGLU]] + [[RoPE]]); **Part II (Chs 4–9)** walks six application surfaces with runnable code on the **GPU-poor** ([[Phi3Mini|Phi-3-mini]] + free [[GoogleColab|Colab T4]]) stack — text classification (four regimes from task-specific BERT to generative LLMs; F1 = 0.80–0.91 on rotten_tomatoes), text clustering and topic modeling ([[BERTopic]] end-to-end on 44,949 arXiv-NLP abstracts), prompt engineering (seven-component modular prompt + CoT/self-consistency/ToT + grammar-constrained decoding via llama-cpp-python), advanced text generation ([[LangChain]] Model I/O / Chains / Memory / Agents — [[react|ReAct]] over DuckDuckGo + llm-math), semantic search and RAG (three-category retrieval taxonomy: [[DenseRetrieval|dense]] / [[ReRanking|rerank]] / [[rag|RAG]] on a 15-sentence *Interstellar* corpus via [[Cohere]] + [[FAISS]] + [[Phi3Mini|Phi-3]] + [[LangChain]]), and multimodal LLMs ([[VisionTransformer|ViT]] + [[CLIP]] + [[BLIP2|BLIP-2]] — the wiki's first runnable end-to-end vision-language pipeline); **Part III (Chs 10–12)** crosses from *using* pretrained models to *creating* them — embedding-model training ([[SBERT]] / [[AugmentedSBERT]] / [[TSDAE]] with the six-loss-function ladder on [[MNLI]] / [[STSB|STS-B]]), representation-model fine-tuning (four regimes for [[bert|BERT]]-class encoders on [[RottenTomatoes|rotten_tomatoes]] / [[CoNLL2003]] — full FT, layer freezing, [[SetFit]] few-shot, MLM continued pretraining + NER with [[BIOTagging]]), and **generation-model fine-tuning (Ch 12)** — the **two-stage [[QLoRA]] + [[trl|TRL]] pipeline** on [[TinyLlama|TinyLlama-1.1B]] (Stage 1: [[SFTTrainer]] on 3,000 [[UltraChat]] examples; Stage 2: [[DPOTrainer]] on `argilla/distilabel-intel-orca-dpo-pairs`), with a consolidated generative-eval primer ([[Perplexity]] / [[ROUGE]] / [[bleu|BLEU]] / [[BERTScore]] + [[MMLU]] / [[GSM8K]] / [[HellaSwag]] / [[TruthfulQA]] / [[HumanEval]] / [[GLUE]] + [[OpenLLMLeaderboard|leaderboards]] + [[LLMAsAJudge]] + [[ChatbotArena]] + [[GoodhartsLaw]]) and a forward-look at [[ORPO]] as the SFT + DPO collapse. **The book's deliberate pedagogical thesis carries through all 12 chapters**: intuition-first, diagram-led explanation followed by runnable Hugging Face / LangChain / TRL code, on a free Google Colab Tesla T4 — *"this book is for the GPU-poor"*; Ch 12 closes that thesis at its operational limit by fitting a **full two-stage post-training pipeline** (SFT + DPO) on a 1.1B model into roughly one Colab-hour per stage. **What the book uniquely contributed to the wiki**: the first runnable QLoRA recipe end-to-end (Ch 12); the first runnable DPO recipe end-to-end (Ch 12); the first runnable LangChain ReAct receipt (Ch 7); the first runnable BERTopic end-to-end on a real arXiv corpus (Ch 5); the first runnable end-to-end vision-language pipeline via BLIP-2's Q-Former (Ch 9); the first runnable end-to-end embedding-model training pipeline with the [[MultipleNegativesRankingLoss|MNR loss]] + [[InBatchNegatives|in-batch negatives]] + [[HardNegatives|hard-negatives]] taxonomy (Ch 10); the first runnable [[SetFit]] / NER / BIO-tagging / [[seqeval|seqeval]] / continued-pretraining recipe (Ch 11); the **GPU-poor commitment as a discipline** (free Colab T4 + [[Phi3Mini|Phi-3]] / [[TinyLlama|TinyLlama]] + [[Quantization|quantization]]) that makes the entire LLM stack approachable to anyone with a free Colab account. The three-book triangle ([[ai-engineering-chip-huyen|Huyen]] discipline + [[LLMEngineersHandbook|Iusztin et al.]] production pipeline + [[HandsOnLLM|Alammar & Grootendorst]] intuition + runnable code) is now anchored on all three vertices and at three different compute scales (scale-agnostic / 8B / 1.1B), validating cross-source convergence across the wiki's LLM-engineering corpus.

> **Update 2026-05-23 — [[hands-on-llm-ch11-fine-tuning-representation-models|*Hands-On Large Language Models* Ch 11: Fine-Tuning Representation Models for Classification]] ([[JayAlammar|Jay Alammar]] & [[MaartenGrootendorst|Maarten Grootendorst]]; [[OReilly|O'Reilly Media]] 2024, ISBN 978-1-098-15096-9) ingested.** **Eleventh chapter of *Hands-On LLMs*** and **the second chapter of Part III ("Training and Fine-Tuning Language Models")** — the runnable-code resolution of [[hands-on-llm-ch01-introduction-to-llms|Ch 1]]'s *"forward-reference Ch 11 for fine-tuning representation models"* promise and the **task-specific deepening of [[hands-on-llm-ch04-text-classification|Ch 4]]** (which had used frozen pretrained models without weight updates). Ch 11 walks **four fine-tuning regimes for [[bert|BERT]]-class encoders** on the same [[RottenTomatoes|rotten_tomatoes]] / [[CoNLL2003|CoNLL-2003]] datasets: **(1)** **full supervised fine-tuning** of `bert-base-cased` via [[HuggingFace|Hugging Face]] [[Trainer|`Trainer`]] + [[TrainingArguments]] (`lr=2e-5`, `batch_size=16`, `weight_decay=0.01`, 1 epoch) + [[DataCollatorWithPadding]] + [[F1Score|F1]] via `compute_metrics` → **F1 = 0.85** in minutes, beating Ch 4's frozen 0.80 — *"It only costs us a couple of minutes to train."* **(2)** **[[LayerFreezing|Layer freezing]]** variants on the same task — iterate `model.named_parameters()` and set `param.requires_grad = False`: freeze everything except classifier head F1 = **0.63**; freeze encoder blocks 0–9 (keep blocks 10–11 + head trainable) F1 = **0.80**; the chapter's empirical takeaway *"training only the first five encoder blocks is enough to almost reach the performance of training all encoder blocks"* — diminishing returns up the encoder stack. **(3)** **[[SetFit]] for few-shot classification** ([[LewisTunstall|Tunstall]] et al. 2022, arXiv:2209.11055) — three-step pipeline: (a) **sampling training data** by in-class / out-class selection (`16 * (16-1) / 2 = 120` positive pairs per class; 32 labeled sentences → 1,280 generated pairs); (b) **fine-tuning embeddings** via [[ContrastiveLearning|contrastive learning]] on a [[SentenceTransformers|SentenceTransformer]] ([[AllMPNetBaseV2|`sentence-transformers/all-mpnet-base-v2`]] in the worked recipe); (c) **training a classifier** (default: scikit-learn [[LogisticRegression]]; alternative: differentiable head via `use_differentiable_head=True`). **32 labels → F1 = 0.85**, matching the full-data baseline — *"with only 32 labeled documents, we get an F1 score of 0.85."* SetFit also supports **zero-shot** by synthesizing examples from label names. **(4)** **[[ContinuedPretraining|Continued pretraining]]** with [[MaskedLanguageModel|MLM]] via `AutoModelForMaskedLM` + [[DataCollatorForLanguageModeling]] (`mlm=True`, `mlm_probability=0.15`; [[WholeWordMasking|whole-word masking]] alternative via `DataCollatorForWholeWordMask` — *"predicting whole words tends to be more complicated than tokens, which makes the model perform better ... however, it tends to take a bit more time to converge"*). The recipe **inserts a domain-adaptation step between generic pretraining and task fine-tuning** — *"like going from a general BERT model to a BioBERT model specialized for the medical domain, to a fine-tuned BioBERT model to classify medication."* Qualitative demonstration via `fill-mask` on *"What a horrible [MASK]!"*: base `bert-base-cased` predicts generic (`idea / dream / thing / day / thought`); MLM-continued-on-rotten-tomatoes predicts movie-specific (`movie / film / mess / comedy / story`). This is the **encoder-side MLM half of [[hands-on-llm-ch10-creating-text-embedding-models|Ch 10]]'s [[DomainAdaptation|domain-adaptation]] / [[AdaptivePretraining|adaptive-pretraining]] recipe matrix** — Ch 10 promised Ch 11 would walk MLM-on-pretrained-BERT, and Ch 11 delivers. **(5)** Pivots from document-level to **token-level classification** for **[[NamedEntityRecognition|named-entity recognition]] (NER)** on [[CoNLL2003|CoNLL-2003]] — *"especially helpful for de-identification and anonymization tasks when there is sensitive data."* Structural changes from document classification: use `AutoModelForTokenClassification` instead of `AutoModelForSequenceClassification`; use [[DataCollatorForTokenClassification]] instead of [[DataCollatorWithPadding]]; introduce the **[[BIOTagging|BIO-tagging]] scheme** (`O / B-PER / I-PER / B-ORG / I-ORG / B-LOC / I-LOC / B-MISC / I-MISC` — Beginning / Inside / Outside, four entity classes PER/ORG/LOC/MISC); align word-level labels with subword tokens via a custom [[LabelAlignment|`align_labels`]] function (first subtoken of a word inherits the word's label with `B-` prefix; subsequent subtokens get `I-` continuation prefix via the `(label + 1) if odd` trick exploiting the B-=odd / I-=even ordering; `[CLS] / [SEP] / pad` get `-100` to be ignored by `CrossEntropyLoss` default `ignore_index=-100`); use [[seqeval|`seqeval`]] (Hugging Face `evaluate` package) for **span-level** F1 (correct only when both start and end of a span are predicted right). Worked-example inference via `pipeline("token-classification")` on *"My name is Maarten."* → `Ma + ##arte + ##n` correctly identified as `B-PER + I-PER + I-PER` with scores ≈ 0.99 — *"the word 'Maarten' and its subtokens were correctly identified as a person!"* The chapter closes by forward-referencing **Ch 12** for fine-tuning **generative** models with instruction fine-tuning and preference alignment ([[rlhf|RLHF]] / [[DPO]]) — the parallel chapter for the generative-model branch. **Pages minted**: [[SetFit]] / [[NamedEntityRecognition]] / [[TokenClassification]] / [[BIOTagging]] / [[CoNLL2003]] / [[LayerFreezing]] / [[DataCollatorForLanguageModeling]] / [[DataCollatorForTokenClassification]] / [[DataCollatorWithPadding]] / [[TokenMasking]] / [[WholeWordMasking]] / [[LabelAlignment]] / [[SequenceClassificationHead]] / [[TokenClassificationHead]] / [[FillMaskPipeline]] / [[seqeval]] / [[WNUT17]] / [[TrainingArguments]]. **Pages updated in place**: [[bert]] / [[FineTuningBert]] / [[FineTuning]] / [[FullFinetuning]] / [[PartialFinetuning]] / [[ContinuedPretraining]] / [[MaskedLanguageModel]] / [[DomainAdaptation]] / [[Trainer]] / [[HuggingFace]] / [[FewShotLearning]] / [[ContrastiveLearning]] / [[SentenceTransformers]] / [[AllMPNetBaseV2]] / [[F1Score]] / [[RottenTomatoes]] / [[LewisTunstall]] / [[JayAlammar]] / [[MaartenGrootendorst]] / [[HandsOnLLM]] / [[OReilly]] — each gets `hands-on-llm-ch11-fine-tuning-representation-models` appended to `sources:` and a `## From Hands-On LLMs Ch 11` section. **Position in the wiki**: closes the loop on the wiki's two BERT-fine-tuning treatments — [[FineTuningBert]] (D2L's *"pretrained encoder + task head, joint update"* template, NLP-side analogue of D2L's CV recipe) and Ch 11 (the **Hugging Face `Trainer` runnable instantiation** of that same template, with four regime variations). Also closes the loop on **PEFT vs partial-FT vs full-FT** with concrete numbers — [[ai-engineering-ch07-finetuning|AI Engineering Ch 7]]'s partial-FT framing (Houlsby et al. 2019: BERT-large needs ~25% params updated for GLUE) gets a runnable companion demonstrating that even **just block 11 + head** (well below 25%) reaches 0.80/0.85 of full-FT performance. **Soft consistency notes**: Ch 11's MLM run is `num_train_epochs=10` in code but the surrounding prose says *"We train for 20 epochs"* — a residual edit artifact in the source text; the wiki records both. Ch 11's footnote 3 cites *"How to fine-tune GERT for text classification?"* — a source typo; the actual paper is Sun et al., *"How to Fine-Tune BERT for Text Classification?"* (CCL 2019). **No hard contradictions** with prior wiki sources — Ch 11 implements recipes the wiki had previously framed conceptually.

> **Update 2026-05-23 — [[hands-on-llm-ch10-creating-text-embedding-models|*Hands-On Large Language Models* Ch 10: Creating Text Embedding Models]] ([[JayAlammar|Jay Alammar]] & [[MaartenGrootendorst|Maarten Grootendorst]]; [[OReilly|O'Reilly Media]] 2024, ISBN 978-1-098-15096-9) ingested.** **Tenth chapter of *Hands-On LLMs*** and **the opener of Part III ("Training and Fine-Tuning Language Models")** — the first chapter that crosses from *using* pretrained models to *creating* them. Ch 10 is the **runnable-code resolution of every forward reference** prior chapters parked on it: Ch 2's *"contrastive training is the prototype of any model that takes two vectors and predicts a relation,"* Ch 4's embedding-as-feature pipeline that relies on `all-mpnet-base-v2`'s contrastive pretraining, Ch 5's *"choose your embedding model from MTEB's clustering column"* discipline, [[hands-on-llm-ch08-semantic-search-and-rag|Ch 8]]'s *"language models need to be trained on question-answer pairs to become better at retrieval — explained in Chapter 10,"* and [[hands-on-llm-ch09-multimodal-llms|Ch 9]]'s *"we will go in depth into [contrastive learning's] inner workings in Chapter 10 where we will create our own embedding model."* The chapter walks **six training/fine-tuning regimes** on the same [[MNLI]] subset (50k pairs from [[GLUE]]) with the same [[STSB|STS-B]] Pearson cosine evaluator (`EmbeddingSimilarityEvaluator` on `glue/stsb/validation` rescaled to `[0,1]`), producing a ladder of scores that the chapter uses to demonstrate **loss-function choice can move performance by 20+ points on the same data**: **(1)** train-from-scratch with [[SoftmaxLoss|softmax loss]] on `bert-base-uncased` → **0.59**; **(2)** train-from-scratch with [[CosineSimilarityLoss|cosine similarity loss]] → **0.72**; **(3)** train-from-scratch with [[MultipleNegativesRankingLoss|MNR loss]] (a.k.a. [[InfoNCE]] / [[NTXentLoss|NT-Xent]]) on entailment triplets → **0.80**; **(4)** fine-tune `all-MiniLM-L6-v2` with MNR loss → **0.85** (highest); **(5)** [[AugmentedSBERT]] (Thakur, Reimers et al. 2020, arXiv:2010.08240) with 10k gold + 40k silver labeled by a fine-tuned [[CrossEncoder|cross-encoder]] → **0.71** (vs 0.72 for the supervised baseline — *"using only 20% of that data"*); **(6)** unsupervised [[TSDAE]] (Wang, Reimers & Gurevych 2021, arXiv:2104.06979) with [[CLSPooling|[CLS]-pooling]] and `DenoisingAutoEncoderLoss(tie_encoder_decoder=True)` on 50k unlabeled sentences → **0.70**. The chapter codifies the **[[SBERTArchitecture|SBERT architecture]]** as the modern default: a [[SiameseNetwork|siamese / bi-encoder]] of two weight-tied [[bert|BERT]] models, [[MeanPooling|mean-pooled]] over the final layer, optimized with a contrastive objective — Reimers & Gurevych 2019 ([[NilsReimers|Reimers]] & [[IrynaGurevych|Gurevych]], EMNLP, arXiv:1908.10084). The architectural contrast is the [[CrossEncoder|cross-encoder]] — *"a cross-encoder allows two sentences to be passed to the Transformer network simultaneously to predict the extent to which the two sentences are similar"* — which is more accurate but **does not produce reusable embeddings** and scales as $n(n-1)/2$ inference calls for $n$ sentences (49,995,000 for $n=10{,}000$). Sentence-BERT's elegant alternative: drop the classification head, mean-pool the final layer, train siamese. **Codifies the [[HardNegatives|hard-negatives]] taxonomy as a wiki first**: **[[EasyNegatives|easy negatives]]** (random documents — what [[InBatchNegatives|in-batch negatives]] from MNR loss actually produce), **[[SemiHardNegatives|semi-hard negatives]]** (cosine-similar but not the right answer — minable from a pretrained embedding model), **[[HardNegatives|hard negatives]]** (related-but-wrong, the kind production embedding models actually need; usually require manual or generative-model labeling) — with the canonical example: question = *"How many people live in Amsterdam?"* → positive = *"Almost a million people live in Amsterdam"* → hard negative = *"More than a million people live in Utrecht, which is more than Amsterdam"* (related to Amsterdam, related to population, but wrong city / wrong answer). **Codifies [[NLI|natural language inference]] as the canonical contrastive-data source**: entailment → positive, contradiction → negative; uses 50,000 of [[MNLI]]'s 392,702 pairs (a subset of [[GLUE]]). The **two fine-tuning workhorses** Ch 10 walks fill specific data-availability regimes that the prior wiki only had fragmentary coverage of: **[[AugmentedSBERT]]** (few-labels regime — train cross-encoder on small **gold dataset**, label larger unlabeled pool into **silver dataset**, train bi-encoder on gold + silver) and **[[TSDAE]]** (no-labels regime — denoising auto-encoder over deleted-word noise; **the only place in the wiki where [[CLSPooling|[CLS]-pooling]] is recommended over [[MeanPooling|mean-pooling]]** — *"in the TSDAE paper, this was shown to be more effective since mean pooling loses the position information"*). Other unsupervised techniques **named but not walked**: **[[SimCSE]]** (Gao, Yao & Chen 2021, arXiv:2104.08821), **[[ContrastiveTension|Contrastive Tension (CT)]]** (Carlsson et al. ICLR 2021), **[[GPL|Generative Pseudo-Labeling]]** (Wang et al. 2021, arXiv:2112.07577). Closes with **[[DomainAdaptation|domain adaptation]] via [[AdaptivePretraining|adaptive pretraining]]**: TSDAE (or [[MaskedLanguageModel|MLM]]) on target-domain unlabeled text → fine-tune on whatever labeled pairs are available — *"although data from the target domain is preferred, out-domain data also works since we started with unsupervised training on the target domain."* Forward-references **Ch 11** for the MLM half of the recipe and the broader fine-tuning-representation-models territory. Evaluation is anchored to **[[STSB|STS-B]]** (the chapter uses STS-B not full MTEB *"for illustration purposes"* since MTEB takes hours); the broader **[[MTEB]]** suite (Muennighoff et al. 2022, arXiv:2210.07316 — 8 tasks / 58 datasets / 112 languages) is flagged as the production model-selection rubric, consistent with the discipline the prior Chs 4 / 5 / 8 already established. **Mints 31 new pages**: concepts — [[SBERT]] / [[SBERTArchitecture]] / [[SiameseNetwork]] / [[MeanPooling]] / [[CLSPooling]] / [[MultipleNegativesRankingLoss]] / [[CosineSimilarityLoss]] / [[SoftmaxLoss]] / [[DenoisingAutoEncoderLoss]] / [[InBatchNegatives]] / [[HardNegatives]] / [[SemiHardNegatives]] / [[EasyNegatives]] / [[AugmentedSBERT]] / [[TSDAE]] / [[SimCSE]] / [[ContrastiveTension]] / [[GPL]] / [[GoldDataset]] / [[SilverDataset]] / [[DomainAdaptation]] / [[AdaptivePretraining]] / [[InfoNCE]] / [[NTXentLoss]] / [[STSB]] / [[MNLI]] / [[GLUE]]; entities — [[AllMiniLML6V2]] / [[NandanThakur]] / [[KexinWang]] / [[TianyuGao]]. **Updates in place**: [[ContrastiveLearning]] / [[SentenceTransformers]] / [[BiEncoder]] / [[CrossEncoder]] / [[SemanticTextualSimilarity]] / [[NaturalLanguageInference]] / [[SNLI]] / [[MTEB]] / [[EmbeddingModel]] / [[TextEmbedding]] / [[SentenceEmbedding]] / [[NoiseContrastiveEstimation]] / [[Word2Vec]] / [[MaskedLanguageModel]] / [[AllMPNetBaseV2]] / [[bert]] / [[Pooling]] / [[NilsReimers]] / [[IrynaGurevych]] / [[JayAlammar]] / [[MaartenGrootendorst]] / [[HandsOnLLM]]. **Position in the wiki**: closes Part II's *use-pretrained-models* arc and opens Part III's *create-and-finetune-models* arc; the **wiki's first runnable end-to-end embedding-model training pipeline** (prior coverage was either using pretrained `model.encode(...)` or referencing the Reimers & Gurevych paper at a citation-only level); the **wiki's first concrete code-level walk of contrastive learning** as a training procedure (Chs 1, 2, 9 framed it; Ch 10 implements it with `sentence_transformers.losses.MultipleNegativesRankingLoss`); the wiki's first record of **gold-dataset / silver-dataset** as a data-quality taxonomy; the wiki's first explicit articulation of the **easy / semi-hard / hard negatives** mining hierarchy. **Soft contradiction flagged**: Ch 10's main thesis recommends [[MeanPooling]] over [[CLSPooling]] for supervised contrastive embeddings, citing the Sentence-BERT paper finding that *"averaging output layer or using the [CLS] token has shown to be worse than simply averaging word vectors, like GloVe"* — but the TSDAE section explicitly recommends [CLS]-pooling for that one unsupervised denoising regime. The two recommendations are regime-specific (supervised contrastive vs unsupervised denoising), not conflicting, but it is the only place in the wiki where [CLS]-pooling is preferred over mean-pooling and worth flagging for readers who have internalized the mean-pooling-as-default rule from Chs 2/4/5/8.

> **Update 2026-05-23 — [[dmls-ch01-overview|*Designing Machine Learning Systems: An Iterative Process for Production-Ready Applications*]] ([[ChipHuyen|Chip Huyen]]; [[OReilly|O'Reilly Media]] May 2022, ISBN 978-1-098-10796-3) ingested as an 11-chapter book.** The **2022 predecessor** to [[ai-engineering-chip-huyen|*AI Engineering*]] (2024, already ingested) — same author, broader pre-LLM scope covering production ML systems holistically (classical ML + DL, not just foundation models). Ingested via 11 parallel opus subagents — one per chapter — on the pdftotext-layout-extracted text (markitdown lost heading structure; switched to `pdftotext -layout` and split on `Chapter N. <title>` boundaries). Source pages live at `wiki/sources/dmls-ch01-overview.md` through `wiki/sources/dmls-ch11-human-side.md` (≈ 215 KB combined). **Architectural through-line**: (1) [[dmls-ch01-overview|Overview]] — ML system = algorithm + data + interface + infra + ops; when (not) to use ML; ML in research vs. production across five axes (stakeholders, computational priorities, data, fairness, interpretability). (2) [[dmls-ch02-intro-design|Introduction to ML Systems Design]] — business-to-ML objective translation; four requirements ([[Reliability]] / [[Scalability]] / [[Maintainability]] / [[Adaptability]]); iterative six-step lifecycle; decoupling-vs-Pareto for multi-objective ranking; the mind-vs-data debate. (3) [[dmls-ch03-data-engineering|Data Engineering Fundamentals]] — sources / serialization ([[JSON]] / [[CSVFormat]] / [[Parquet]] / [[Protobuf]] / [[Avro]] / [[Pickle]]) / [[RelationalModel|relational]] vs [[DocumentModel|document]] vs [[GraphModel|graph]] models / [[OLTP]]-[[OLAP]] convergence ([[CockroachDB]] / [[ApacheIceberg]] / [[DuckDB]]) / [[ETL]]-[[ELT]] / request-driven vs event-driven dataflow / batch-vs-stream ("batch is special case of streaming" — Tzoumas/Flink). (4) [[dmls-ch04-training-data|Training Data]] — sampling families (probability + non-probability incl. [[ReservoirSampling|reservoir]] / [[importancesampling|importance]] / [[StratifiedSampling|stratified]]); labeling ladder (hand → [[NaturalLabel|natural]] → [[WeakSupervision|weak-supervision]] via [[Snorkel]] → [[SemiSupervisedLearning|semi-sup]] → [[TransferLearning|transfer]] → [[ActiveLearning|active]]); [[ClassImbalance|class imbalance]] (data-level + algorithm-level remedies — [[SMOTE]], [[FocalLoss]], [[ClassBalancedLoss]]); three [[DataAugmentation]] families (label-preserving / [[Perturbation]] / synthesis incl. [[Mixup]] / [[CycleGAN]]). (5) [[dmls-ch05-feature-engineering|Feature Engineering]] — feature ops ([[Imputation]], [[FeatureScaling]], [[Discretization]], [[HashingTrick]] — Booking.com 50% collisions tolerated, [[FeatureCrossing]], positional embeddings); [[DataLeakage|data leakage]] (six causes incl. random-splitting time-correlated data + COVID-imaging case from MIT Tech Review 2021); good-feature criteria ([[FeatureImportance]] + [[FeatureGeneralization]]). (6) [[dmls-ch06-model-development|Model Development and Offline Evaluation]] — model selection heuristics (avoid SOTA-trap, start simple); [[ModelEnsemble|ensembling]] taxonomy ([[Bagging]] / [[Boosting]] / [[Stacking]]; 20-of-22 Kaggle 2021 winners); [[ExperimentTracking]] + [[Versioning]]; [[DistributedTraining]] (data/model/pipeline parallelism; GPT-3 175B = 3.2M batch size); [[AutoML]] (soft + hard incl. [[NeuralArchitectureSearch]] + [[LearnedOptimizer]]); offline evaluation suite (baselines, [[PerturbationTest|perturbation]] / [[InvarianceTest|invariance]] / [[DirectionalExpectationTest|directional]] tests, [[ModelCalibration|calibration]], [[ConfidenceMeasurement|confidence]], [[DataSlicing|slice-based]] + [[SimpsonsParadox]]). (7) [[dmls-ch07-model-deployment|Model Deployment and Prediction Service]] — four [[MLDeploymentMyths]]; three prediction modes ([[BatchInference|batch]] / [[OnlineInference|online]] / [[StreamingPrediction|streaming]] — Grubhub counterexample to "batch is cheaper"); cloud vs [[EdgeComputing|edge]]; [[ModelCompression]] families ([[Quantization]] — Roblox BERT-on-CPU 7× latency; [[Pruning]] — [[LotteryTicketHypothesis]]; [[KnowledgeDistillation]] — DistilBERT 40%/60%/97%; [[LowRankFactorization]] — [[MobileNet]] / [[SqueezeNet]] 50× fewer params); compilers + [[IntermediateRepresentation|IRs]] + lowering ([[OperatorFusion]], [[LoopTiling]], [[Vectorization]], [[ParallelizationOptimization]]); [[AutoTVM]] beats cuDNN after ~70 trials; ML-in-the-browser via [[WebAssembly]] (93% device support). (8) [[dmls-ch08-distribution-shifts-monitoring|Data Distribution Shifts and Monitoring]] — ML failure modes (software ~80% per Google's 96-pipeline study by Papasian/Underwood + ML-specific [[EdgeCase|edge cases]] / [[DegenerateFeedbackLoop|degenerate feedback loops]]); formal taxonomy [[CovariateShift]] / [[LabelShift]] / [[ConceptShift]] + [[LabelSchemaChange]] / [[FeatureChange]]; drift-detection ladder (summary stats → [[KolmogorovSmirnovTest|KS]] → [[MaximumMeanDiscrepancy|MMD]] / [[LeastSquaresDensityDifference|LSDD]]); [[ImportanceWeighting]] adaptation; four-artifact monitoring (raw inputs → features → predictions → accuracy); logs / dashboards / alerts ([[AlertFatigue]]); [[observability]] ⊇ monitoring. (9) [[dmls-ch09-continual-learning|Continual Learning and Test in Production]] — [[StatefulTraining]] vs [[StatelessRetraining]] (Grubhub 45× compute reduction); four-stage continual-learning maturity model; [[NaturalLabels]] + [[FeedbackLoopLength]] (Twitter Ads 2021: most clicks <5min, long tail); [[ContinuousColdStart]] (Coveo: 70% shoppers visit <3×/year); [[FeatureReuseLogAndWait]] (Faire pattern); test-in-production ladder ([[ShadowDeployment]] safest → [[ABTesting]] → [[CanaryDeployment]] → [[InterleavingExperiments]] — Joachims 2002 → [[MultiArmedBandits]] + [[ThompsonSampling]] — Rafferty <12K vs 630K samples → [[ContextualBandits]]).
