(10) [[dmls-ch10-infrastructure-mlops|Infrastructure and Tooling for MLOps]] — four-layer infrastructure model (storage/[[ComputeLayer|compute]] → resource management → [[MLPlatform]] → dev environment); [[Cron|scheduler]] vs [[Orchestrator]] (when/what vs where); workflow managers ([[Airflow]] — 3 named limitations / [[Prefect]] / [[ArgoWorkflows]] — K8s+YAML / [[Kubeflow]] "abstracts boilerplate by making you write Kubeflow boilerplate" / [[Metaflow]] — Netflix, `@conda`/`@batch`); [[ModelDeployment]] (most mature) vs [[ModelStore]] (least mature — 8 artifacts); [[FeatureStore]] three problems (management — [[Amundsen]] / [[DataHub]]; computation; consistency — [[Feast]] / [[Tecton]]); [[Jupyter]] + Netflix's [[Papermill]] / [[Commuter]] / fast.ai's [[nbdev]]; [[MultiCloud]] (81% Gartner, rarely by design — Josh Wills) + [[CloudRepatriation]] (Dropbox $75M case); build-vs-buy. (11) [[dmls-ch11-human-side|The Human Side of ML]] — UX trade-offs ([[ConsistencyAccuracyTradeoff]] — Booking.com / [[SpeedAccuracyTradeoff]] via [[SmoothFailing]] with [[BackupModel]] / [[MostlyCorrectPredictions]] presented as [[MultipleCandidatePredictions]] — Jessy Lin's *Rethinking Human-AI Interaction*); team structure ([[SubjectMatterExpert]]s as developers / [[EndToEndDataScientist]] vs [[FullCycleDeveloper]] — Approach 1 cross-functional vs Approach 2 — Eugene Yan, Eric Colson "Beware the Data Science Pin Factory"; Huyen self-retracts her 2020 "data scientists need Kubernetes" position); [[ResponsibleAI]] practitioner framework ([[OfqualGradingAlgorithm]] UK 2020 A-level scandal, [[StravaHeatmap]] 2018 — anonymization insufficient; [[ModelCard]]s — Mitchell et al. 2018; [[CompactnessFairnessTradeoff]] — Hooker et al.: pruning > quantization on disparate impact; [[PrivacyAccuracyTradeoff]] — DP loss non-uniform; six-step framework: discover sources, understand limits, trade-offs, processes, model cards, stay-up-to-date). **Position in the wiki**: this is the **canonical 2022 reference** the wiki's later [[ai-engineering-chip-huyen|AI Engineering (2024)]] sections refer back to as the broader-scope predecessor. Where DMLS Ch 7 covers compression + compilers + browser deployment, AI Engineering Ch 9 deepens for LLMs; where DMLS Ch 9 covers continual learning + test-in-production, AI Engineering builds on that with LLM-era specifics; where DMLS Ch 6 covers offline evaluation, AI Engineering Chs 3-4 extend it for foundation models. **Soft contradictions / disambiguation** (no hard conflicts): DMLS Ch 1's "latency = response time" overloads Kleppmann's stricter usage; DMLS Ch 2's "data is essential, for now" predates compute-optimal [[ChinchillaScalingLaw]] refinements; DMLS Ch 5's "quantization" (binning) collides with [[Quantization]]'s weight-precision sense (→ [[Discretization]] cross-reference); DMLS Ch 6's "graduate student descent" dominance has shifted by 2024; DMLS Ch 7's "online is more expensive than batch" needs request-density qualification (Grubhub); DMLS Ch 9 argues against "continuous learning" + "online learning" as synonyms; DMLS Ch 11 retracts the 2020 "Kubernetes is essential" Huyen position. **Scale of the ingest**: subagents wrote only the 11 source pages and produced structured reports listing ~200 concept-page seeds + ~80 entity-page seeds; per the agent-isolation strategy, those pages were left as unresolved `[[wikilinks]]` for a follow-up consolidation pass (avoids race conditions across 11 parallel agents on shared wiki/index.md / wiki/log.md / wiki/concepts/ / wiki/entities/). The 11 source pages each contain 10–18 key claims, 4–8 quotes with context attribution, and a comprehensive Connections section with `[[WikiLinks]]` to existing and proposed pages.

> **Update 2026-05-23 — [[ai-engineering-chip-huyen|*AI Engineering: Building Applications with Foundation Models*]] ([[ChipHuyen|Chip Huyen]]; [[OReilly|O'Reilly Media]] 2024, ISBN 978-1-098-16630-4) ingested as a 10-chapter book.** The wiki's **first end-to-end AI-engineering textbook** and the **third O'Reilly 2024 LLM book** alongside [[hands-on-llm-ch01-introduction-to-llms|*Hands-On LLMs*]] (Alammar & Grootendorst) and the [[leh-ch01-understanding-llm-twin-concept|*LLM Engineer's Handbook*]] (Iusztin, Labonne & Vesa) — completes the wiki's three-book triangle on the production-LLM discipline. Each book sits at a distinct point on the abstraction ladder: Hands-On LLMs is **intuition-first runnable-code pedagogy** (load Phi-3-mini in `transformers.pipeline`, see the embedding output); the LLM Engineer's Handbook is **end-to-end production-pipeline cookbook** (an FTI-architecture ZenML deployment on AWS SageMaker for a synthetic LLM Twin); Huyen's *AI Engineering* is **framework-and-trade-off discipline** — *"this book isn't a tutorial; it provides a framework for selecting tools."* The book positions [[AIEngineering]] as **distinct from ML engineering**: ML engineering builds models from scratch, AI engineering works **with** readily-available foundation models as a service; the bottleneck shifts from training to **evaluation, prompt design, context construction, and inference cost**. The book's core thesis — *"start simple"* — applies recursively: prompt before [[rag|RAG]], RAG before [[Agent|agents]], prompt before finetuning, finetuning before training-from-scratch; and **evaluation is the hardest part** (two of ten chapters dedicated to it). **The 10-chapter structure**: (1) [[ai-engineering-ch01-intro|Intro to FM applications]] — defines AI engineering, walks use cases, the AI engineering stack; (2) [[ai-engineering-ch02-foundation-models|Understanding FMs]] — training data / architecture / scale ([[ChinchillaScalingLaw]] / [[EmergentAbilities]] / [[InverseScaling]]) / post-training ([[rlhf]] / [[DPO]] / [[PPO]]) / sampling ([[Temperature]] / [[Topk]] / [[Topp]] / [[TestTimeCompute]]); (3) [[ai-engineering-ch03-evaluation-methodology|Evaluation Methodology]] — perplexity, [[ExactEvaluation]], [[llmasjudge|AI-as-a-judge]], [[ComparativeEvaluation]] ([[EloRating]] / [[BradleyTerry]]); (4) [[ai-engineering-ch04-evaluate-ai-systems|Evaluate AI Systems]] — criteria taxonomy ([[DomainSpecificCapability]] / [[GenerationCapability]] / [[InstructionFollowingCapability]] / [[CostAndLatency]]), [[ModelSelection]], [[EvaluationPipeline]] design; (5) [[ai-engineering-ch05-prompt-engineering|Prompt Engineering]] — best practices + [[DefensivePromptEngineering]] ([[PromptAttack]] taxonomy + [[InstructionHierarchy]] + [[InputGuardrail]] / [[OutputGuardrail]] / [[Isolation]] / [[LLMRedTeaming]]); (6) [[ai-engineering-ch06-rag-agents|RAG and Agents]] — full retrieval stack + agent discipline + [[CompoundErrorAccumulation]] caveat + memory hierarchy; (7) [[ai-engineering-ch07-finetuning|Finetuning]] — memory math, [[PEFT]] / [[lora|LoRA]] / [[QLoRA]], [[ModelMerging]] ([[TaskVector]] / [[TIES]] / [[DARE]]); (8) [[ai-engineering-ch08-dataset-engineering|Dataset Engineering]] — six-characteristics quality framework, [[DataSynthesis]], [[ModelCollapse]] risk, [[BloomFilter]] / [[MinHashDeduplication]]; (9) [[ai-engineering-ch09-inference-optimization|Inference Optimization]] — the book's most quantitative chapter: [[ArithmeticIntensity]] / [[RooflineModel]] / [[MFU]] / [[MBU]] / [[Goodput]] / [[SpeculativeDecoding]] / [[PagedAttention]] / [[ContinuousBatching]] / [[PrefillDecodeDisaggregation]] / [[Quantization]] families; (10) [[ai-engineering-ch10-architecture-feedback|Architecture and User Feedback]] — additive reference architecture ([[ContextConstruction]] → [[Guardrail]] → [[ModelGateway]] / [[ModelRouter]] → [[ExactCache]] / [[SemanticCache]] → [[AIPipelineOrchestration]] → [[WriteAction]] → [[observability]] / [[Monitoring]]) + [[ExplicitFeedback]] / [[ImplicitFeedback]] taxonomy + [[Sycophancy]] / [[DegenerateFeedbackLoop]] failure modes. **Scale of the ingest**: mints **545 new concept pages + 156 new entity pages** across 10 chapters; updates ~250 existing wiki pages in place with `## From [[ai-engineering-chN-...|AI Engineering Ch N]]` appended sections; lifts ~30 prior stub pages ([[safety]], [[factuality]], [[mmlu]], [[gpqa]], [[ifeval]], [[posttraining]], [[KVCache]], [[InferenceOptimization]], [[observability]], [[Monitoring]]) to substantive treatments. **Position in the wiki**: the **survey-style anchor** the wiki's prior paper-level entries point back to. Where the wiki already has dedicated paper-level coverage ([[2305.18290-dpo|DPO paper]], [[2106.09685-lora|LoRA paper]], [[2406.11695-mipro|MIPRO paper]], [[2210.03629-react|ReAct paper]], [[2203.11171-self-consistency|self-consistency paper]], [[2205.11916-zero-shot-cot|zero-shot-CoT paper]], [[2310.11511-self-rag|Self-RAG paper]]), the AI Engineering chapter source pages cite back to those as the authoritative entries; where the book extends or contextualizes the prior coverage, the chapter pages flag what's new. **First wiki coverage of**: the AI-engineering-vs-ML-engineering distinction as a discipline framing ([[AIEngineeringVsMLEngineering]]); the three-axis planning framework ([[CriticalOrComplementary]] / [[DynamicOrStatic]] / [[ReactiveOrProactive]]); the inference-time MFU / MBU / [[ArithmeticIntensity]] / [[RooflineModel]] regime distinction with concrete numbers (Llama 2-70B MBU charts, CharacterAI >20× KV-cache stack, Anyscale 100:1 prefill-decode ratio); the Berkeley Function Calling Leaderboard and the [[TravelPlannerBenchmark]] for agents; the [[InstructionHierarchy]] four-level priority defense (System > User > Model output > Tool output); the [[ModelGateway]] / [[ModelRouter]] reference architecture pattern; the [[ExplicitFeedback]] / [[ImplicitFeedback]] taxonomy with concrete failure modes. **Cross-book convergences**: the Ch 6 RAG treatment is the **engineering-discipline framing** in the now four-source wiki RAG curriculum (Huyen Ch 6 / [[hands-on-llm-ch08-semantic-search-and-rag|Hands-On LLMs Ch 8]] / [[leh-ch04-rag-feature-pipeline|LEH Ch 4]] / [[dspy-rag-tutorial|DSPy RAG tutorial]]); the Ch 5 Prompt Engineering treatment pairs with [[hands-on-llm-ch06-prompt-engineering|Hands-On LLMs Ch 6]] (modular-component framing) as the **discipline vs design-surface** complementary pair; the Ch 7 Finetuning treatment pairs with [[leh-ch05-supervised-fine-tuning|LEH Ch 5]] (memory math + LoRA) and [[leh-ch06-preference-alignment|LEH Ch 6]] (DPO derivation). Contradictions flagged per chapter on the chapter source pages: RLHF effect on hallucination (Schulman vs InstructGPT paper); test-time-compute saturation (OpenAI peaks at 400 vs Stanford *Monkey Business* log-linear to 10,000); Llama compute-suboptimal-by-design vs Chinchilla optimality; finetuning > training-from-scratch contradicted by [[Ossification]] at scale; "synthetic data is lower quality than human" contradicted by Llama 3 / MetaMath / AlphaGeometry findings; positive-feedback collection (Apple HIG says don't ask vs Huyen's product-manager interviewees say do ask). No book-level contradictions with the existing wiki corpus. Re-split note: an initial chapter-boundary error caused by Python `splitlines()` treating form-feed characters as separators while `grep -n` does not was caught and corrected at the Ch 5 boundary; the [[ai-engineering-ch05-prompt-engineering|Ch 5 Defenses Against Prompt Attacks]] section was re-ingested as a supplement and the remaining chapters were re-split before ingest.

> **Update 2026-05-23 — [[hands-on-llm-ch09-multimodal-llms|*Hands-On Large Language Models* Ch 9: Multimodal Large Language Models]] ([[JayAlammar|Jay Alammar]] & [[MaartenGrootendorst|Maarten Grootendorst]]; [[OReilly|O'Reilly Media]] 2024, ISBN 978-1-098-15096-9) ingested.** **Ninth chapter of *Hands-On LLMs*** (after Chs 1–8) and **the book's vision-language chapter** — the only chapter that extends the wiki's existing text-only LLM coverage to **multimodal models that accept images**. Closes **Part II** of the book before opening **Part III** on training and fine-tuning. Three architecturally distinct moves walked intuition → mechanism → runnable code: **(1) [[VisionTransformer|Vision Transformer (ViT)]]** (Dosovitskiy et al. 2020) — adapting the [[transformer|Transformer]] encoder to images by tokenizing the image into [[PatchEmbedding|16×16 patches]] and treating patch embeddings the same way as text token embeddings; the chapter's structural punchline — *"the moment the embeddings are passed to the encoder, they are treated as if they were textual tokens. From that point forward, there is no difference in how a text or image trains"* — is the cleanest statement in the wiki of why the Transformer machinery generalizes across modalities and the structural precondition for adapter-style multimodal LLMs. **(2) [[CLIP|Contrastive Language-Image Pre-training (CLIP)]]** (Radford et al. 2021) — the multimodal *embedding* model that projects images and texts into the **same 512-dim vector space** via [[ContrastiveLearning|contrastive learning]] on (image, caption) pairs; demonstrated via [[OpenCLIP]] (`openai/clip-vit-base-patch32`) using `CLIPTokenizerFast` / `CLIPProcessor` / `CLIPModel` on an AI-generated puppy-in-the-snow image — L2-normalize-then-dot-product gives **0.33 similarity** (high in CLIP's distribution, even though sounds low). Codifies the wiki-novel **CLIP `[CLS]` convention inversion**: *"in CLIP, the [CLS] token is actually used to represent the image embedding"* (inverse of [[bert|BERT]]'s convention). Names **four CLIP applications**: [[ZeroShotClassification|zero-shot classification]] / clustering / search / generation ([[StableDiffusion|Stable Diffusion]] cited as canonical downstream generative use, Rombach et al. 2022). [[SentenceTransformers|sentence-transformers]]' `SentenceTransformer("clip-ViT-B-32")` named as the few-lines easy-mode wrapper. **(3) [[BLIP2|BLIP-2]]** (Li et al. 2023) — the multimodal *generative* model that bridges a **frozen [[VisionTransformer|ViT]]** and a **frozen LLM** (OPT-2.7b from [[meta]]) via the trainable **[[QFormer|Querying Transformer (Q-Former)]]** + a linear projection; loaded via `Salesforce/blip2-opt-2.7b` + `transformers.Blip2ForConditionalGeneration` + `AutoProcessor` in `torch.float16`. **Two-stage training**: Stage 1 = Q-Former representation learning on three joint objectives ([[ImageTextContrastive|image-text contrastive]] / [[ImageTextMatching|image-text matching]] / [[ImageGroundedTextGeneration|image-grounded text generation]]); Stage 2 = LLM soft-prompting where the Q-Former's learnable embeddings serve as **[[SoftVisualPrompt|soft visual prompts]]** that condition the LLM — *"the LLM will be given information about the image in a similar manner to the context you would provide an LLM when prompting"* — the wiki's first runnable instance of soft-prompting where the soft prompt is **derived from a non-text encoder**. **Two BLIP-2 use cases**: **(a) [[ImageCaptioning|Image captioning]]** — supercar → *"an orange supercar driving on the road at sunset"*; Rorschach inkblot → *"a black and white ink drawing of a bat"*. **(b) [[VisualQuestionAnswering|VQA]] / multimodal chat** — second-turn *"What would it cost me to drive that car?"* → *"$1,000,000"* via concatenating prior `Question: ... Answer: ...` turns into the prompt. Generalizes to the [[MultimodalLLM|adapter-on-frozen-encoder family]] by naming [[LLaVA15|LLaVA]] (2-layer MLP projector + Vicuna-13B) and [[Idefics2|Idefics 2]] (Mistral 7B backbone) as siblings of BLIP-2: *"Both visual LLMs, although having different architectures, connect pretrained CLIP-like visual encoders with textual LLMs. The goal of these architectures is to project visual features from the input images to language embeddings such that they can be used as the input for an LLM."* **Position**: Ch 9 is the **pedagogical bridge** between three prior wiki strands of multimodal coverage — (a) discipline-level framing ([[ai-engineering-ch03-evaluation-methodology|Huyen Ch 3]] naming CLIP / [[ULIP]] / [[ImageBind]] as the three reference multimodal embedding models), (b) architectural pattern recognition ([[2408.08849-ecg-chat|ECG-Chat]] / [[MultimodalLLM]] codifying the [[LLaVA15|LLaVA]] adapter-on-frozen-encoder pattern), and (c) native-multimodality contrast ([[2312.11805-gemini|Gemini]]'s from-the-start joint pretraining). Ch 9 runs both a CLIP-style embedding model and a BLIP-2 generative model with **concrete tensor shapes** (`torch.Size([1, 512])` text/image embeddings; `224 × 224` preprocessed pixel tensor) on a consumer-GPU notebook — **the wiki's first runnable end-to-end vision-language pipeline**. **Mints**: zero new pages — Ch 9's full surface is already covered by prior wiki ingests (every wikilink resolves against existing concepts/entities). Updates **15 entity pages** in place ([[BLIP2]] / [[OpenCLIP]] / [[Salesforce]] / [[Idefics2]] / [[HuggingFace]] / [[openai]] / [[meta]] / [[Mistral]] / [[SentenceTransformers]] / [[StableDiffusion]] / [[GPT2]] / [[JayAlammar]] / [[MaartenGrootendorst]] / [[HandsOnLLM]] / [[OReilly]]) and **28 concept pages** ([[CLIP]] / [[VisionTransformer]] / [[PatchEmbedding]] / [[ContrastiveLearning]] / [[QFormer]] / [[ImageTextContrastive]] / [[ImageTextMatching]] / [[ImageGroundedTextGeneration]] / [[SoftVisualPrompt]] / [[ImageCaptioning]] / [[VisualQuestionAnswering]] / [[ImageEncoder]] / [[ClsToken]] / [[CosineSimilarity]] / [[BPE]] / [[ByteLevelTokenization]] / [[bert]] / [[FoundationModel]] / [[MultimodalLLM]] / [[MultimodalRAG]] / [[MultimodalEmbeddingSpace]] / [[NaturalLanguageSupervision]] / [[nativemultimodality]] / [[ImageBind]] / [[ULIP]] / [[ZeroShotClassification]] / [[LLaVA15]] / [[transformer]]) — each with `sources: [+ hands-on-llm-ch09-multimodal-llms]` + a `## From Hands-On LLMs Ch 9` section. **First wiki coverage of**: [[VisionTransformer|ViT]] tokenization-by-patching as the structural analog to text tokenization; the [[CLIP]] training algorithm walked in three figures; the [[OpenCLIP]] worked-code recipe with CLIP's `[CLS]` convention inversion; the [[BLIP2|BLIP-2]] architecture decomposed into the **three Q-Former training objectives** + **two training stages** — the wiki's **first concrete architectural recipe for an adapter-on-frozen-encoder multimodal LLM**; the [[SoftVisualPrompt|soft-visual-prompt]] mechanism where the soft prompt is derived from a non-text encoder; [[VisualQuestionAnswering|VQA]] as chat-based prompting around an image with a `prompt = "Question: ... Answer:"` template; the `Ġ`-as-space byte-level BPE convention codified at code-point-arithmetic granularity. **No direct contradictions** with the existing wiki corpus; soft consistency notes flag CLIP's `[CLS]` convention as a CLIP-specific override (not a BERT contradiction); adapter vs native multimodality coexist as architecturally distinct regimes ([[MultimodalLLM]] / [[nativemultimodality]]); ViT-vs-CNN consistent with existing [[VisionTransformer]] page's scalability-trumps-inductive-biases thesis. Forward-references **Ch 10** (training and fine-tuning embedding models) for the negatives / loss-form treatment of contrastive learning.

> **Update 2026-05-23 — [[hands-on-llm-ch08-semantic-search-and-rag|*Hands-On Large Language Models* Ch 8: Semantic Search and Retrieval-Augmented Generation]] ([[JayAlammar|Jay Alammar]] & [[MaartenGrootendorst|Maarten Grootendorst]]; [[OReilly|O'Reilly Media]] 2024, ISBN 978-1-098-15096-9) ingested.** **Eighth chapter of *Hands-On LLMs*** (after Chs 1–7) and **the book's headline [[rag|RAG]] chapter** — picks up the retrieval thread [[hands-on-llm-ch07-advanced-text-generation|Ch 7]] explicitly deferred (*"retrieval will be discussed in the next chapter"*) and resolves it via the **three-category retrieval taxonomy** the chapter organizes itself around: **[[DenseRetrieval|dense retrieval]]** (embed query + documents, find nearest neighbors), **[[ReRanking|reranking]]** (rescore an existing shortlist with a stronger model — typically a [[CrossEncoder|cross-encoder]] / [[MonoBERT|monoBERT]]-style), and **[[rag|RAG]]** (text generation grounded on retrieved documents — with citations). Each category is walked intuition → runnable code → caveats → evaluation, with the canonical worked example being a **15-sentence Wikipedia article on the film *Interstellar*** searched via [[Cohere]]'s `co.embed(input_type="search_document")` → [[FAISS|`faiss.IndexFlatL2`]] → `co.embed(input_type="search_query")` → `co.rerank(top_n=3)` → `co.chat(documents=...)` (managed-LLM RAG with **automatic span-level citations** — `ChatCitation(start=21, end=36, text='worldwide gross', document_ids=['doc_0'])`), and replicated locally with [[Phi3Mini|Phi-3]] + [[BGESmallEnV15|`BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5`]] + [[FAISS]] + [[LangChain|`langchain.RetrievalQA.from_chain_type(chain_type='stuff', ...)`]] — the **wiki's first runnable RetrievalQA receipt**. The chapter opens with the structural observation that **the first industrial-scale LLM adoption was not chat — it was search**: within months of the 2018 BERT paper, [[google|Google]] announced *"one of the biggest leaps forward in the history of Search"* and [[microsoft|Microsoft]] Bing announced *"the largest quality improvements to our Bing customers in the past year."* The capability is named — **[[SemanticSearch|semantic search]]** — *"searching by meaning, and not simply keyword matching."* On a parallel track, the fast adoption of generative LLMs produced **[[Hallucination|hallucinations]]** at scale; the leading industry mitigation is [[rag|RAG]] ([[PatrickLewis|Lewis]] et al. 2020, *"Retrieval-Augmented Generation for Knowledge-Intensive NLP Tasks"*, NeurIPS 33: 9459–9474). **Signature dense-vs-keyword demonstration**: on the *Interstellar* corpus, the query *"how precise was the science"* returns the correct *"praise from many astronomers for its scientific accuracy and portrayal of theoretical astrophysics"* via dense retrieval but the wrong *"Interstellar is a 2014 epic science fiction film co-written, directed, and produced by Christopher Nolan"* via [[BM25|BM25 keyword search]] (which overlaps on the literal word *"science"*) — *"this wouldn't have been possible if we were only doing keyword search because the top result did not include the same keywords in the query."* **Three dense-retrieval caveats**: out-of-distribution queries still return nearest-but-irrelevant results (mitigated via [[SimilarityThreshold|similarity thresholds]]); exact-phrase matching is a [[BM25]] strength → [[HybridSearch|hybrid search]] is the production default; domain transfer is hard. **[[Chunking|Chunking]] design space**: one-vector-per-document (slice or average) vs multiple-vectors-per-document (sentence / paragraph / overlapping / LLM-driven) — Ch 8 adds the top-level **one-vector-vs-multiple-vectors** axis prior wiki sources didn't make explicit. **Library vs database**: [[ApproximateNearestNeighbor|ANN]] libraries ([[Annoy]] / [[FAISS]]) vs [[VectorDatabase|vector databases]] ([[Weaviate]] / [[Pinecone]]) on the **CRUD-and-no-rebuild** axis. **Reranking** is framed as the easiest LLM-add to an existing search system — *"this one step can vastly improve search results and it's in fact what Microsoft Bing added to achieve the improvements to search results using BERT-like models"* — with the **headline efficacy claim**: *"on a multilingual benchmark like [[MIRACL]], a reranker can boost performance from 36.5 to 62.8, measured as [[NDCG|nDCG@10]]"* (≈2× lift). Mechanism: [[CrossEncoder|cross-encoder]] (query + result presented together) → relevance score [0, 1] = **classification problem** — Ch 8 explicitly back-references Ch 4's classification framing. Reference architecture: [[MonoBERT|monoBERT]]. **Retrieval evaluation** walked step-by-step: [[PrecisionAtK|precision-at-k]] → [[AveragePrecision|average precision]] → [[MAP|mean average precision]]; [[NDCG]] as graded-relevance alternative. **RAG section**: structural extension from search by adding an LLM at the end; *"this generation step is called [[GroundedGeneration|grounded generation]] because the retrieved relevant information we provide the LLM establishes a certain context that grounds the LLM in the domain we're interested in."* Managed example produces **automatic span-level [[CitationGeneration|citations]]** (the wiki's first runnable demonstration). **Generative-search examples** named: [[Perplexity]], Microsoft Bing AI, [[gemini|Google Gemini]]. **Advanced RAG techniques continuum** (delegation-increasing): [[QueryRewriting]] → [[MultiQueryRAG]] → [[MultiHopRAG]] → [[QueryRouting]] → [[AgenticRAG]] — with the same **agent-capability-cliff caveat** as Ch 7 (*"likely only the largest managed models may be able to attempt this behavior"*). **RAG evaluation** four-axis taxonomy per [[NelsonFLiu|Liu]], Zhang & [[PercyLiang|Liang]] 2023 *"Evaluating verifiability in generative search engines"* (arXiv:2304.09848): [[Fluency]] / [[PerceivedUtility|Perceived Utility]] / [[CitationRecall|Citation Recall]] / [[CitationPrecision|Citation Precision]]; automated via [[llmasjudge|LLM-as-a-judge]] with [[RAGAS|Ragas]] adding [[Faithfulness]] + [[AnswerRelevance|Answer Relevance]]. **Mints 22 concept pages** ([[DenseRetrieval]], [[SparseRetrieval]], [[GroundedGeneration]], [[GenerativeSearch]], [[CitationGeneration]], [[CitationRecall]], [[CitationPrecision]], [[Faithfulness]], [[AnswerRelevance]], [[Fluency]], [[PerceivedUtility]], [[RAGEvaluation]], [[MultiQueryRAG]], [[MultiHopRAG]], [[QueryRouting]], [[AgenticRAG]], [[MonoBERT]], [[SimilarityThreshold]], [[AveragePrecision]], [[PrecisionAtK]], [[RelevanceJudgment]], [[InformationRetrieval]], [[StopWord]]) + source-stub [[Lewis2020RAG]] + **8 entity pages** ([[MIRACL]], [[BGESmallEnV15]], [[BAAI]], [[CohereRerank]], [[CohereChat]], [[CohereEmbed]], [[PatrickLewis]], [[NelsonFLiu]], [[PercyLiang]]). Updates [[rag]] / [[SemanticSearch]] / [[EmbeddingBasedRetrieval]] / [[TermBasedRetrieval]] / [[BM25]] / [[HybridSearch]] / [[FAISS]] / [[Annoy]] / [[ApproximateNearestNeighbor]] / [[VectorDatabase]] / [[Chunking]] / [[ChunkingStrategy]] / [[CrossEncoder]] / [[ReRanking]] / [[QueryRewriting]] / [[MAP]] / [[NDCG]] / [[Hallucination]] / [[llmasjudge]] / [[RAGAS]] (concepts) and [[Cohere]] / [[LangChain]] / [[HandsOnLLM]] / [[JayAlammar]] / [[MaartenGrootendorst]] / [[Perplexity]] / [[meta]] / [[google]] / [[microsoft]] / [[gemini]] / [[Pinecone]] / [[Weaviate]] / [[Phi3Mini]] / [[SentenceTransformers]] / [[MTEB]] (entities) in place. **Position**: the **third book-chapter RAG treatment** after [[ai-engineering-ch06-rag-agents|Huyen Ch 6]] (engineering-discipline framing) and [[leh-ch04-rag-feature-pipeline|LEH Ch 4]] (production-pipeline-recipe framing); Ch 8 is the **pedagogical-introduction-with-runnable-code** treatment — the chapter that puts dense retrieval, BM25, reranking, and grounded generation on the same 15-sentence corpus side-by-side. **First wiki coverage of**: the three-category retrieval taxonomy; [[MonoBERT|monoBERT]] as the named reference cross-encoder; the [[MIRACL]] reranker-lift benchmark; **span-level citation generation** as a runnable RAG primitive; the multi-query / multi-hop / query-routing / agentic-RAG continuum; the Liu et al. 2023 four-axis RAG-evaluation taxonomy; the `RetrievalQA.from_chain_type(chain_type='stuff', ...)` LangChain primitive; the reranker-as-classification framing. **The four-source wiki RAG curriculum is now complete**: Ch 8 (intuition + runnable code) → Huyen Ch 6 (engineering discipline) → LEH Ch 4 (ZenML batch pipeline) → [[dspy-rag-tutorial|DSPy RAG tutorial]] (MIPROv2 optimization). No direct contradictions; soft consistency notes reconcile dense-vs-sparse vs term-vs-embedding vocabularies, Cohere `co.chat` citation primitive as Liu et al.'s axes operationalized, and the bge-small/gte-small transcription inconsistency in the chapter's local-RAG worked example.

> **Update 2026-05-23 — [[hands-on-llm-ch07-advanced-text-generation|*Hands-On Large Language Models* Ch 7: Advanced Text Generation Techniques and Tools]] ([[JayAlammar|Jay Alammar]] & [[MaartenGrootendorst|Maarten Grootendorst]]; [[OReilly|O'Reilly Media]] 2024, ISBN 978-1-098-15096-9) ingested.** **Seventh chapter of *Hands-On LLMs*** (after Chs 1–6) and **the wiki's first LangChain-centric source** — picks up Part III where [[hands-on-llm-ch06-prompt-engineering|Ch 6]] left off, walking four advanced techniques that **extend an LLM without fine-tuning**, all integrated through [[LangChain]] as the organizing substrate. Where Ch 6 frames *"how to write a better prompt"*, Ch 7 frames *"how to compose a prompt with other components into a system"* — Ch 6's seven-component modular prompt extends naturally into Ch 7's `prompt | llm` operator-style composition. Four movements: (1) **Model I/O** — loading a [[GGUF]]-quantized [[Phi3Mini|Phi-3]] via [[LangChainLlamaCpp|`langchain.LlamaCpp`]] (wraps [[llamacpp|`llama-cpp-python`]]); the **empty-output gotcha** reveals that `LlamaCpp.invoke()` does NOT auto-apply Phi-3's [[ChatTemplate|chat template]] (unlike `transformers.pipeline`'s `apply_chat_template`), motivating explicit [[PromptTemplate|`PromptTemplate`]] use; (2) **Chains** — [[LCEL|LangChain Expression Language]] pipe-operator (`basic_chain = prompt | llm`) and the [[LLMChain|`LLMChain`]] named-output primitive; the chapter's **three-stage story-generation worked example** (`title | character | story`) decomposes a creative task across three sequential LLM calls — the [[LangChain]] operationalization of Ch 6's [[PromptChaining|chain prompting]] / Huyen Ch 5's [[PromptDecomposition|prompt decomposition]]; (3) **Memory** — three pros-and-cons memory types as a design surface, all justified by [[StatelessLLM|LLM statelessness]]: [[ConversationBufferMemory]] (full history; instant but hogs tokens), [[ConversationBufferWindowMemory]] (last-`k` turns; the [[LangChain]] operationalization of [[FIFOMemory|FIFO eviction]]), [[ConversationSummaryMemory]] (LLM-summarized running history; two LM calls per turn; the operationalization of [[SummarizationMemory|summarization-based memory]]); all demonstrated with the *"Maarten is 33"* identity-retention probe; the chapter's framing — *"Where ConversationBufferMemory is instant but hogs tokens, ConversationSummaryMemory is slow but frees up tokens to use"* — codifies the **speed-vs-memory-vs-accuracy** trade-off; (4) **Agents** — [[react|ReAct]] (Yao et al. 2022, [[ShunyuYao|Shunyu Yao]] first author) implemented via [[LangChainAgent|`langchain.agents.create_react_agent` + `AgentExecutor`]]; two-tool inventory ([[DuckDuckGoSearchResults|DuckDuckGo search]] + [[LLMMathTool|llm-math]] calculator) running on [[ChatGPT|GPT-3.5-turbo]] — Ch 7 explicitly notes Phi-3-mini is *"not sufficient to run these examples"* (the **agent capability cliff** — consistent with [[ai-engineering-ch06-rag-agents|Huyen Ch 6]]'s [[CompoundErrorAccumulation|compound-error-accumulation]] argument). Canonical worked benchmark: *"What is the current price of a MacBook Pro in USD? How much would it cost in EUR if the exchange rate is 0.85 EUR for 1 USD?"* → *"$2,249.00 ... approximately 1911.65 EUR"* via two ReAct cycles (search → calculate). The chapter ends with a load-bearing **safety caveat** — *"there is no [[humanintheloop|human in the loop]] to judge the quality of the output or reasoning process. This double-edged sword requires a careful system design to improve its reliability"* — with two concrete partial-HITL suggestions (return the source URL; ask the user to confirm each step). Retrieval-augmented generation is **explicitly deferred to Ch 8**. The chapter names **[[DSPy]] and Haystack** as *"newer frameworks of note"* alongside LangChain — committing to LangChain pedagogical-first while acknowledging the alternatives. **Mints 10 concept pages** ([[ConversationBufferMemory]], [[ConversationBufferWindowMemory]], [[ConversationSummaryMemory]], [[LCEL]], [[LLMChain]], [[LangChainLlamaCpp]], [[LangChainAgent]], [[LLMMathTool]], [[StatelessLLM]], [[ToolUse]]) + [[DuckDuckGoSearchResults]] (LangChain class as concept page) + **2 entity pages** ([[ShunyuYao]] — ReAct + Tree of Thoughts first author; [[DuckDuckGoSearch]] — the search engine itself). Updates [[ChatGPT]] / [[openai]] / [[HuggingFace]] / [[DSPy]] (entities) and [[Agent]] / [[AgenticAI]] / [[react]] / [[humanintheloop]] / [[CompoundErrorAccumulation]] / [[ContextLength]] / [[ContextWindow]] / [[ChatTemplate]] / [[PromptDecomposition]] / [[ReflectionMemory]] / [[ToolInventory]] / [[PromptAttack]] / [[PromptEngineeringTools]] (concepts) in place. **First wiki LangChain deep-dive at runnable-code granularity** — prior LangChain references named the framework or used it for a single utility; Ch 7 makes it the chapter's organizing structure across Model I/O / Chains / Memory / Agents. **First runnable LangChain-native ReAct agent** in the wiki, complementing existing [[DSPy]]-native receipts ([[dspy-customer-service-agent]] / [[dspy-modules]] / [[dspy-tools]]). **First wiki coverage of the LangChain memory class hierarchy** (Buffer / Buffer-Window / Summary) as a concrete pros-and-cons design surface complementing Huyen Ch 6's FIFO / Summarization / Reflection conceptual taxonomy. **First wiki coverage of LangChain's LCEL pipe operator** as a composition primitive. **First wiki appearance of [[ShunyuYao|Shunyu Yao]]** (ReAct first author). Soft consistency notes: [[LangChain]] **operating-mode duality** — Huyen Ch 5's security-fragile framing vs Ch 7's pedagogical-first framing (different operating modes, not a contradiction); `LlamaCpp` does NOT auto-apply chat templates (unlike `transformers.pipeline`) — a tooling-detail load-bearing gotcha; Ch 7's three memory classes are a subset of Huyen Ch 6's four-memory taxonomy (no [[ReflectionMemory|reflection]] in Ch 7); `dspy.ReAct` and `create_react_agent` operationalize the same Yao 2022 scaffold with different ergonomics.

> **Update 2026-05-23 — [[hands-on-llm-ch06-prompt-engineering|*Hands-On Large Language Models* Ch 6: Prompt Engineering]] ([[JayAlammar|Jay Alammar]] & [[MaartenGrootendorst|Maarten Grootendorst]]; [[OReilly|O'Reilly Media]] 2024, ISBN 978-1-098-15096-9) ingested.** **Sixth chapter of *Hands-On LLMs*** (after Chs 1–5) and **the book's first chapter whose subject is squarely [[PromptEngineering|prompt engineering]]** — opens **Part III** on the generation-focused half of the book. Ch 6 is the **wiki's second book-chapter prompt-engineering treatment** after [[ai-engineering-ch05-prompt-engineering|Huyen's *AI Engineering* Ch 5]]; the two are highly complementary — Huyen Ch 5 frames the **discipline** (six best practices + defensive prompt engineering + [[PromptStructure|three-part anatomy]] + [[PromptEngineeringTools|tools taxonomy]] with hidden-cost warnings); Alammar & Grootendorst Ch 6 frames the **modular-component design surface** (seven Lego-block prompt parts + iterative build-it-up workflow). Four movements with [[Phi3Mini|Phi-3-mini]] as the substrate: (1) **using a text generation model** — proprietary vs open source; loading via `transformers.pipeline`; the [[ChatTemplate|chat template]] `<s><|user|>...<|end|><|assistant|>` that `apply_chat_template` hides; controlling output via [[Temperature|temperature]] + [[Topp|top_p]] + [[Topk|top_k]] (with Ch 6's use-case quadrant table mapping brainstorming / email / creative-writing / translation to high/low combinations); (2) **intro to prompt engineering** — basic ingredients (instruction + data + output indicator); three instruction-prompting tips (**specificity** — the most important; **[[Hallucination|hallucination]] mitigation** via *"if you don't know, say 'I don't know'"*; **order / [[PrimacyEffect|primacy]] + recency** per [[lostinthemiddle|Liu et al. 2023]]); (3) **advanced prompt engineering** — **the seven-component modular prompt** ([[Persona|persona]] + [[InstructionPrompt|instruction]] + [[ContextPrompt|context]] + [[OutputFormat|format]] + [[AudiencePrompt|audience]] + [[TonePrompt|tone]] + data — *"think of prompts as pieces of a larger puzzle"*; iterate by adding/removing/reordering Lego blocks; creative add-ons like **[[EmotionPrompt|emotional stimuli]]** per Li et al. 2023); **[[InContextLearning|in-context learning]]** via zero / one ([[OneShotPrompting]]) / [[FewShotLearning|few-shot]] prompting (the *Gigamuru / screeg* made-up-word demonstration requiring alternating `user` / `assistant` chat messages); **chain prompting** = [[PromptDecomposition|prompt decomposition]] across LLM calls (`name + slogan` → `sales pitch` worked example — MindMeld Messenger; three use cases — **response validation** / **parallel prompts** / **writing stories**); (4) **reasoning with generative models** — [[System1And2|Kahneman's System 1 vs System 2]] frames generative models as System 1 (constant-time, no self-reflection) and reasoning-prompting techniques as **constructive operationalizations of System 2** (sitting in soft tension with [[2402.01817-llm-modulo|Kambhampati et al.'s critique]] that the resemblance is empty without external verifiers — both positions now documented on [[System1And2]]); **[[chainofthought|CoT]]** with both few-shot examples (Wei et al. 2022 — the tennis-balls + cafeteria-apples worked example) and the **[[ZeroShotCoT|zero-shot *"Let's think step-by-step"* trigger]]** (Kojima et al. 2022; alternative triggers per Yang et al. 2023); the **compute justification** *"each additional token allows the LLM to stabilize its output"*; **[[selfconsistency|self-consistency]]** (Wang et al. 2022 — N-CoT + majority vote; *"n times slower"*); **[[TreeOfThoughts|tree-of-thought]]** (Yao et al. 2023) both architecturally and as a **single-prompt three-experts-roleplay approximation** (*"Imagine three different experts are answering this question..."*); (5) **output verification** — four motivations (**structured output** / **valid output** / **ethics** / **accuracy**) and three control methods (**examples** / **grammar** / **fine-tuning** — fine-tuning deferred to Ch 12); few-shot output examples (RPG character JSON template — Lysandra Shadowstep); **grammar = [[ConstrainedSampling|constrained sampling]]** — names **[[Guidance]] / [[Guardrails]] / [[LMQL]]**; the **wiki's first runnable [[GrammarConstrainedDecoding|grammar-constrained decoding]] demonstration** via [[llamacpp|`llama-cpp-python`]] + [[GGUF]]-quantized [[Phi3Mini|Phi-3]] + `response_format={"type": "json_object"}` produces a valid JSON warrior `{"name": "Eldrin Stormbringer", "class": "Warrior", "level": 10, ...}` that `json.loads()` validates. Forward-references **Ch 7** (memory + tool use beyond chaining LLMs) and **Ch 12** (fine-tuning as the third output-control lever). **Mints 11 concept pages** ([[InstructionPrompt]], [[ContextPrompt]], [[OutputFormat]], [[AudiencePrompt]], [[TonePrompt]], [[OutputVerification]], [[GrammarConstrainedDecoding]], [[PromptChaining]], [[OneShotPrompting]], [[PrimacyEffect]], [[ZeroShotCoT]], [[EmotionPrompt]]) + **1 entity page** ([[Guardrails]] — Shreya Rajpal's grammar-constrained output library). Updates [[PromptEngineering]] / [[chainofthought]] / [[selfconsistency]] / [[TreeOfThoughts]] / [[Persona]] / [[InContextLearning]] / [[FewShotLearning]] / [[ZeroShotLearning]] / [[ConstrainedSampling]] / [[Temperature]] / [[Topp]] / [[Topk]] / [[Hallucination]] / [[lostinthemiddle]] / [[ChatTemplate]] / [[PromptDecomposition]] / [[System1And2]] (concept pages) + [[HandsOnLLM]] / [[JayAlammar]] / [[MaartenGrootendorst]] / [[OReilly]] / [[HuggingFace]] / [[llamacpp]] / [[Guidance]] / [[Outlines]] / [[Phi3Mini]] / [[microsoft]] (entity pages) in place. **First wiki coverage of the seven-component modular prompt** (persona / instruction / context / format / audience / tone / data) as an iterable design surface. **First wiki coverage of the single-prompt three-experts-roleplay approximation of tree-of-thought**. **First wiki coverage of grammar-constrained decoding in worked runnable code** (via `llama-cpp-python` + GGUF Phi-3); the [[ConstrainedSampling]] page was previously conceptual-only. **First wiki worked example using `llama-cpp-python`** in the [[HandsOnLLM]] book corpus (Chs 1–5 used `transformers` only). **First wiki worked example using GGUF-quantized inference at chapter-tutorial level**. No direct contradictions; soft consistency notes flagged on the source page ([[System1And2]] constructive-vs-critical stance vs [[2402.01817-llm-modulo]]; [[chainofthought]] variant catalog vs Huyen Ch 5; [[PromptEngineering|prompt anatomy]] seven-component vs Huyen's three-part; [[ConstrainedSampling]] toolchain set Guidance/Guardrails/LMQL vs Huyen's guidance/outlines/instructor/llama.cpp — all reconciled).

> **Update 2026-05-23 — [[hands-on-llm-ch05-text-clustering-topic-modeling|*Hands-On Large Language Models* Ch 5: Text Clustering and Topic Modeling]] ([[JayAlammar|Jay Alammar]] & [[MaartenGrootendorst|Maarten Grootendorst]]; [[OReilly|O'Reilly Media]] 2024, ISBN 978-1-098-15096-9) ingested.** **Fifth chapter of *Hands-On LLMs*** and **the unsupervised twin of [[hands-on-llm-ch04-text-classification|Ch 4]]** — codifies the three-step **embed → reduce → cluster** pipeline ([[SentenceTransformers|sentence-transformers]] [[GTESmall|`thenlper/gte-small`]] 384-dim → [[UMAP]] 384→5 dim → [[HDBSCAN]] `min_cluster_size=50`) and extends it via **[[BERTopic]]** — [[MaartenGrootendorst|Grootendorst's]] own modular topic-modeling framework ([[2203.05794-bertopic|Grootendorst 2022, arXiv:2203.05794]]) — where stage 2 represents each cluster via **class-based [[TFIDF|TF-IDF]] ([[ClassBasedTFIDF|c-TF-IDF]])** built on [[sklearn|scikit-learn]]'s `CountVectorizer`. Run end-to-end on [[ArXivNLP|`maartengr/arxiv_nlp`]] — **44,949 cs.CL abstracts** (1991–2024) from [[HuggingFace|Hugging Face]] — producing **156 clusters** (155 topics + outlier topic `-1` with 14,520 abstracts). Selected topics: speech recognition (topic 0, 2,290 docs), medical NLP (topic 1), sentiment / aspect-based analysis (topic 2), neural machine translation (topic 3), topic modeling itself (topic 22, where the BERTopic paper's own abstract is filed). The chapter's **second pedagogical move** is the **representation-model abstraction** — reranking blocks layered on c-TF-IDF, applied **once per topic** rather than once per document (*"if we have millions of documents and a hundred topics, the representation block only needs to be applied once for every topic instead of for every document"*): (1) **[[KeyBERTInspired]]** (rerank candidate keywords by cosine similarity to the per-topic average document embedding; modeled after [[KeyBERT]]); (2) **[[MaximalMarginalRelevance|MMR]]** (Carbonell & Goldstein 1998 diversity-vs-relevance selector); (3) **[[GenerativeTopicLabeling|LLM-based topic labeling]]** (feed each topic's top keywords + 4 most-representative documents into a `[DOCUMENTS]` / `[KEYWORDS]` prompt template and have either [[FLANT5|Flan-T5-small]] or [[ChatGPT|GPT-3.5-turbo]] emit a short label). Closes with a [[Datamapplot|`datamapplot`]] visualization of the top-20 GPT-3.5-labeled topics over a 2D [[UMAP]] projection. The chapter explicitly **forward-references Ch 8**: *"this idea of reranking an initial set of results is a main staple in neural search, a subject that we cover in Chapter 8."* It also fulfils Ch 1's promise that *"bag-of-words is by no means completely obsolete"* — c-TF-IDF preserves bag-of-words at the topic-representation step while replacing topic discovery with embedding-based clustering. Classical [[LatentDirichletAllocation|LDA]] (Blei, Ng & Jordan 2003) is named as the explicit baseline contrasted with BERTopic on context-sensitivity, automatic K, and outlier handling. The chapter's **core architectural commitment** is **modularity** — *"You can think of this modularity as building with Lego blocks; each part of the pipeline is completely replaceable with another, similar algorithm. Through this modularity, newly released models can be integrated within its architecture. As the field of Language AI grows, so does BERTopic!"* This makes BERTopic the wiki's first canonical demonstration of the *"pipeline of swappable LLM-adjacent components"* design pattern that recurs across the AI Engineering / LLM Engineer's Handbook books. **Position in the wiki**: complements rather than replaces existing clustering coverage. Where [[TopicClustering|LEH Ch 5's TopicClustering page]] named the same pipeline in a single paragraph alongside Hugging Face's `text-clustering`, [[NomicAtlas]], BunkaTopics, and Lilac as tooling, *Hands-On LLMs* Ch 5 is the **full pedagogical walkthrough** with a runnable worked example. **Mints 26 concept pages** ([[TextClustering]], [[TopicModeling]], [[BERTopic]], [[UMAP]], [[HDBSCAN]], [[DBSCAN]], [[DensityBasedClustering]], [[CentroidBasedClustering]], [[DimensionalityReduction]], [[ClassBasedTFIDF]], [[KeyBERTInspired]], [[MaximalMarginalRelevance]], [[GenerativeTopicLabeling]], [[LatentDirichletAllocation]], [[GuidedTopicModeling]], [[SeededTopicModeling]], [[DynamicTopicModeling]], [[HierarchicalTopicModeling]], [[SemiSupervisedTopicModeling]], [[ZeroShotTopicModeling]], [[OnlineTopicModeling]], [[MultimodalTopicModeling]], [[Outlier]], [[NeuralSearch]], [[Plotly]], [[Datamapplot]]) + **14 entity pages** ([[ArXiv]], [[ArXivNLP]], [[KeyBERT]], [[GTESmall]], [[GPT35Turbo]], [[AlibabaDAMOAcademy]], [[LelandMcInnes]], [[HaroldHotelling]], [[MartinEster]], [[DavidBlei]], [[AndrewNg]], [[MichaelJordan]], [[UMAPLibrary]], [[HDBSCANLibrary]]) + **1 source stub page** ([[2203.05794-bertopic|BERTopic paper]]). Updates [[BagOfWords]] / [[TFIDF]] / [[CosineSimilarity]] / [[CurseOfDimensionality]] / [[PCA]] / [[Embedding]] / [[SentenceEmbedding]] / [[HierarchicalClustering]] / [[KMeansClustering]] / [[Dendrogram]] / [[MTEB]] / [[Reproducibility]] / [[ReRanking]] / [[RepresentationModel]] / [[UnsupervisedLearning]] / [[TopicClustering]] / [[matplotlib]] / [[NumPy]] / [[pandas]] (concepts) and [[HandsOnLLM]] / [[JayAlammar]] / [[MaartenGrootendorst]] / [[OReilly]] / [[HuggingFace]] / [[SentenceTransformers]] / [[FLANT5]] / [[ChatGPT]] / [[openai]] / [[google]] / [[sklearn]] / [[Gensim]] / [[NomicAtlas]] (entities) in place. **Second wiki appearance of [[sklearn|scikit-learn]] as an explicit LLM-stack dependency** (after Ch 4) — this time providing `CountVectorizer` for the c-TF-IDF bag-of-words step. No direct contradictions.

> **Update 2026-05-23 — [[hands-on-llm-ch04-text-classification|*Hands-On Large Language Models* Ch 4: Text Classification]] ([[JayAlammar|Jay Alammar]] & [[MaartenGrootendorst|Maarten Grootendorst]]; [[OReilly|O'Reilly Media]] 2024, ISBN 978-1-098-15096-9) ingested.** **Fourth chapter of *Hands-On LLMs*** and **the book's first applications chapter** — runs a single binary [[SentimentAnalysis|sentiment-analysis]] task ([[RottenTomatoes|`rotten_tomatoes`]] from [[HuggingFace|Hugging Face]]; 5,331 + 5,331 movie reviews from Pang & Lee 2005, split 8,530 / 1,066 / 1,066) across **four progressively more permissive pretrained-LLM classification regimes** with side-by-side weighted-average [[F1Score|F1]] scores: (1) **[[TaskSpecificModel|task-specific representation model]]** — [[TwitterRoBERTa|`cardiffnlp/twitter-roberta-base-sentiment-latest`]] via `transformers.pipeline`, **F1 = 0.80**; (2) **[[EmbeddingModel|embedding model]] + [[LogisticRegression|logistic regression]]** — [[SentenceTransformers|sentence-transformers]] [[AllMPNetBaseV2|`all-mpnet-base-v2`]] (768-dim) + [[sklearn|scikit-learn]] `LogisticRegression(random_state=42)`, **F1 = 0.85**; (3) **[[ZeroShotClassification|zero-shot classification]] via [[LabelEmbedding|label embeddings]] + [[CosineSimilarity]]** — `model.encode(["A negative review", "A positive review"])` → `argmax(cosine_similarity(...))`, **F1 = 0.78** with no labeled training data; (4) **[[GenerativeClassification|generative classification]] via instruction prompts** — [[FLANT5|`google/flan-t5-small`]] (encoder-decoder via `text2text-generation` pipeline; prompt: *"Is the following sentence positive or negative? "*; parse `"negative"` → 0 / `"positive"` → 1; **F1 = 0.84**) and [[ChatGPT|`gpt-3.5-turbo-0125`]] (decoder-only via OpenAI Chat Completions API; `temperature=0`; **F1 = 0.91** — the chapter's best result). The chapter's **central pedagogical move** is to **decouple the embedding step from the classification step**: by keeping the embedding model frozen, per-task work collapses from full BERT fine-tuning (Ch 11) to training a logistic regression on **CPU** over a 768-dim feature vector — *"a major benefit of this separation is that we do not need to fine-tune our embedding model, which can be costly."* It then generalizes to the **no-labels** case via zero-shot label embeddings — describing labels as natural-language sentences (*"A negative review"*), embedding the descriptions with the same model used for documents, and assigning labels by cosine similarity between document and label embeddings. The chapter explicitly contrasts this with **[[NaturalLanguageInference|NLI]]-based zero-shot classification** (the prior dominant approach) — *"natural language inference models are amazing for zero-shot classification, the example here demonstrates the flexibility of embeddings for a variety of tasks. As you will see throughout the book, embeddings can be found in most Language AI use cases and are often an underestimated but incredibly vital component."* The generative-models half introduces **[[GenerativeClassification|generative classification]] via [[PromptEngineering|prompt engineering]] + label parsing**: instead of taking class logits, prefix each input with an instruction and parse the model's free-text output back to a class label. **[[FLANT5|Flan-T5]]**'s training recipe is sketched ([[spancorruption|span-corruption pretraining]] → multi-task instruction tuning across 1,800+ tasks per Chung et al. 2022 *"Scaling instruction-finetuned language models"*); **[[ChatGPT]]** training is sketched in three steps (pretraining → [[InstructionTuning|instruction-tuning]] on manually-created prompt+output pairs → [[PreferenceFinetuning|preference tuning]] on manually-ranked outputs — *"a major benefit of using preference data over instruction data is the nuance it represents"*). Names **rate limits + [[ExponentialBackoff|exponential backoff]]** for API workflows and the OpenAI API **cost data** (3 cents for the 1,066-row Rotten Tomatoes test sweep at time of writing). Closes with the **[[DataContamination|benchmark-contamination caveat]]** that bounds the validity of the 0.91 ChatGPT F1: *"since we do not know what data the model was trained on, we cannot easily use these kinds of metrics for evaluating the model. For all we know, it might have actually been trained on our dataset!"* The chapter opens with a load-bearing endorsement of **classical baselines** — *"it is highly advised to compare these examples against classic, but strong baselines such as representing text with [[TFIDF|TF-IDF]] and training a logistic regression classifier on top of that"* — echoing [[ai-engineering-ch04-evaluate-ai-systems|Huyen's AI Engineering Ch 4]] discipline on baselining. The chapter walks confusion matrix → [[Precision|precision]] / [[Recall|recall]] / [[Accuracy|accuracy]] / [[F1Score|F1]] for the metric vocabulary; uses **weighted-average F1** from `sklearn.metrics.classification_report` as its headline metric throughout the book. The **[[MTEB|MTEB leaderboard]]** is named as the canonical embedding-model-selection rubric (forward-reference for Ch 10's embedding-creation chapter). **Position in the wiki**: complements rather than replaces existing classification coverage. Where [[d2l-nlp-applications|D2L's NLP-Applications]] treatment is the **architecture-survey** (RNN baseline / textCNN / BERT fine-tuning) and the existing [[SentimentAnalysis|SentimentAnalysis page]] focuses on D2L's [[IMDb]] worked examples, Ch 4 is the **practitioner-comparison-table tour** with side-by-side F1 scores across four pretrained-LLM regimes on a single smaller dataset. **First wiki coverage of zero-shot classification via label embeddings + cosine similarity** as a distinct technique from prompt-engineering zero-shot or NLI-based zero-shot. **First wiki coverage of generative classification via instruction prompts + label parsing** for using decoder-only and encoder-decoder LLMs as classifiers. **First wiki worked example using `transformers.pipeline("text-classification")` and `pipeline("text2text-generation")`** (Chs 1–3 used `text-generation` only). **First wiki worked example using the OpenAI Chat Completions API**. **First wiki appearance of the [[RottenTomatoes|rotten_tomatoes]] dataset** as a small / balanced / HF-distributed alternative to D2L's [[IMDb|IMDb]]. **First wiki appearance of [[TwitterRoBERTa|Twitter-RoBERTa]]**, the [[MTEB|MTEB leaderboard]], and **[[sklearn|scikit-learn]] as an explicit LLM-stack dependency**. **Mints 14 concept pages** ([[TaskSpecificModel]], [[EmbeddingModel]], [[ZeroShotClassification]], [[LabelEmbedding]], [[GenerativeClassification]], [[ClassificationHead]], [[BinarySentimentClassification]], [[ClassificationReport]], [[ConfusionMatrix]], [[Precision]], [[Recall]], [[Accuracy]], [[ExponentialBackoff]], [[BenchmarkContamination]]) + **12 entity pages** ([[RottenTomatoes]], [[TwitterRoBERTa]], [[CardiffNLP]], [[RoBERTa]], [[DistilBERT]], [[ALBERT]], [[sklearn]], [[MTEB]], [[BoPang]], [[LillianLee]], [[ColinRaffel]], [[HyungWonChung]]). Updates [[TextClassification]] / [[SentimentAnalysis]] / [[ZeroShotLearning]] / [[LogisticRegression]] / [[CosineSimilarity]] / [[F1Score]] / [[NaturalLanguageInference]] / [[t5]] / [[bert]] / [[DataContamination]] (concept pages) + [[HandsOnLLM]] / [[JayAlammar]] / [[MaartenGrootendorst]] / [[OReilly]] / [[HuggingFace]] / [[SentenceTransformers]] / [[AllMPNetBaseV2]] / [[FLANT5]] / [[ChatGPT]] / [[openai]] / [[Cohere]] / [[google]] (entity pages) in place. No direct contradictions; minor consistency notes flagged on the source page (zero-shot definition — prompt-engineering sense vs classification-without-labels sense are reconciled across [[ZeroShotLearning]] / [[ZeroShotClassification]]; F1 macro-vs-weighted averaging is consistent across [[2507.03152-medval|MedVAL]] and Ch 4 because the latter's data is balanced; TF-IDF baseline endorsement is consistent with Huyen Ch 4's discipline).

> **Update 2026-05-23 — [[hands-on-llm-ch03-looking-inside-llms|*Hands-On Large Language Models* Ch 3: Looking Inside Large Language Models]] ([[JayAlammar|Jay Alammar]] & [[MaartenGrootendorst|Maarten Grootendorst]]; [[OReilly|O'Reilly Media]] 2024, ISBN 978-1-098-15096-9) ingested.** **Third chapter of *Hands-On LLMs*** following [[hands-on-llm-ch01-introduction-to-llms|Ch 1]] and [[hands-on-llm-ch02-tokens-and-embeddings|Ch 2]] earlier this session. The chapter is **the wiki's canonical pedagogical walkthrough of the [[transformer|Transformer]] LLM from the inside** — the intuition-first complement to the formal [[1706.03762-attention-is-all-you-need|*Attention Is All You Need*]] paper and the practitioner inference-time framing of [[ai-engineering-ch02-foundation-models|Huyen's AI Engineering Ch 2]]. The chapter opens with the **autoregressive token-by-token generation loop** (one [[forwardpass|forward pass]] per output token; each generated token appended to the prompt for the next pass) and breaks the model into three top-level components: **[[Tokenizer|tokenizer]]** (vocabulary → token IDs) → **stack of [[transformer|Transformer blocks]]** (the bulk of processing) → **[[LMHead|LM head]]** (a single linear layer mapping the final hidden state to vocab-sized logits). The headline pedagogical move is the **live dissection of [[Phi3Mini|Phi-3-mini]]** via `print(model)`: 32,064-token vocabulary, 3,072-dim embeddings, **32 `Phi3DecoderLayer` blocks** each containing a `Phi3Attention` with `qkv_proj: Linear(3072 → 9216)` (fused [[QueryProjection|Q]] / [[KeyProjection|K]] / [[ValueProjection|V]] projection), `Phi3RotaryEmbedding`, a `Phi3MLP` (`gate_up_proj: Linear(3072 → 16384)` → `SiLU` → `down_proj: Linear(8192 → 3072)` — [[SwiGLU]]-style gated MLP), `Phi3RMSNorm` for pre-norm placement, and the LM head as `Linear(3072 → 32064)`. A worked example runs *"The capital of France is"* through the stack and recovers `Paris` as the highest-probability next token from `lm_head_output[0, -1].argmax(-1)`. The chapter introduces **parallel [[TokenStream|token-stream]] processing** — each input token has its own computation track through the model; the count of streams equals the [[ContextLength|context length]]; cross-stream interaction happens only in attention sub-layers — and gives the [[KVCache|KV cache]] its definitive intuition-first framing: by caching K and V projections across decode steps the model avoids recomputing previous streams. A **concrete on-consumer-GPU measurement** anchors this: generating 100 tokens with Phi-3-mini on a Colab T4 takes **4.5 s with `use_cache=True`** vs **21.8 s with `use_cache=False`** — a ~5× speedup; `use_cache` defaults to `True` in [[HuggingFace|Hugging Face]] Transformers, *"one reason why LLM APIs stream the output tokens as the model generates them instead of waiting for the entire generation to be completed."* The chapter then walks **[[selfattention|self-attention]] in two pedagogical steps**: (1) **relevance scoring** — multiply the current position's query vector by the keys matrix; softmax to a probability distribution over previous tokens; (2) **combining information** — multiply each previous token's value vector by its score and sum. *"Three projection matrices"* ([[QueryProjection|Q]] / [[KeyProjection|K]] / [[ValueProjection|V]]) are the learned attention parameters. **[[multiheadattention|Multi-head attention]]** duplicates this in parallel; each [[AttentionHead|attention head]] has its own Q/K/V projections; *"increases the model's capacity to model complex patterns in the input sequence."* The framing of decoder attention as **causal** (only attends to previous tokens; contrast with bidirectional [[bert|BERT]]) and the **memorization-and-interpolation framing of the [[FeedForwardNetwork|FFN]]** (*"The Shawshank ___"* → `Redemption` example; the FFN is the source of factual information, attention handles context — *"The dog chased the squirrel because it ___"*) round out the per-block intuition. The chapter's headline section is the **2024-era Transformer block recipe** vs the 2017 original: (i) **efficient attention** — [[LocalAttention|local/sparse attention]] (used by [[GPT3|GPT-3]], which **interweaves full and sparse blocks**), [[SlidingWindowAttention|sliding-window attention]] (Longformer), **[[multiqueryattention|multi-query attention (MQA)]]** (K/V shared across all heads, [[NoamShazeer|Shazeer 2019]]), **[[GroupedQueryAttention|grouped-query attention (GQA)]]** (K/V shared within head groups — *"used by models like [[Llama|Llama 2]] and 3"*); (ii) **[[FlashAttention|FlashAttention]]** + **[[FlashAttention2|FlashAttention-2]]** as IO-aware GPU kernels optimizing movement between SRAM and HBM; (iii) **[[PreNorm|pre-normalization]]** over post-norm, **[[RMSNorm]]** over LayerNorm, **[[SwiGLU]]** over ReLU; (iv) **[[RoPE|rotary positional embeddings]]** applied at the attention step (mixed into queries and keys just before relevance scoring) rather than at input — encoding absolute and relative position via vector rotation; (v) **[[SequencePacking|sequence packing]]** as the training-time efficiency that motivates relative-aware positional encodings (packed Document 50 starts at position 50 — absolute embeddings mislead the model). Also names **[[DecodingStrategy|decoding strategy]]** as the umbrella term for picking a token from the LM head's distribution — [[GreedyDecoding|greedy decoding]] (always pick highest probability; temperature=0) vs [[Sampling|sampling]] (random pick proportional to probability) — and the **raw-LM vs chat-LM distinction**: instruction-tuning + RLHF turns raw next-token predictors (like [[GPT3|GPT-3]]) into chatbots (like [[GPT4|GPT-4]]). **Position in the wiki**: complements rather than replaces the existing Transformer coverage. Where the original [[1706.03762-attention-is-all-you-need|*Attention Is All You Need* page]] is the **formal architectural paper**, and [[ai-engineering-ch02-foundation-models|Huyen's AI Engineering Ch 2]] is the **practitioner inference-time framing** (prefill vs decode, sampling, latency), *Hands-On LLMs* Ch 3 is the **intuition-first walkthrough with a runnable model dissection**. **First wiki coverage of the 2024-era Transformer block recipe as a coherent bundle** (pre-norm + RMSNorm + SwiGLU + GQA + RoPE) — the wiki had each component scattered before. **First wiki concrete on-consumer-GPU KV-cache wall-clock measurement** (4.5 s vs 21.8 s) to complement the formal memory-formula treatment from [[leh-ch08-inference-optimization|LEH Ch 8]]. **First wiki coverage of [[SequencePacking|sequence packing]]** as the training-time motivation for relative-aware positional encodings. **First wiki coverage of [[LMHead|the LM head]] as a named distinct component** swappable with sequence/token classification heads. **Mints 16 concept pages** ([[GroupedQueryAttention]], [[RoPE]], [[RMSNorm]], [[SwiGLU]], [[SiLU]], [[LMHead]], [[DecodingStrategy]], [[LocalAttention]], [[SlidingWindowAttention]], [[SequencePacking]], [[QueryProjection]], [[KeyProjection]], [[ValueProjection]], [[AttentionHead]], [[TokenStream]], [[ModelDimension]]) + **substantially extends [[KVCache]]** + **1 entity page** ([[NoamShazeer]]). Updates [[transformer]] / [[selfattention]] / [[multiheadattention]] / [[multiqueryattention]] / [[scaleddotproductattention]] / [[flashattention]] / [[FlashAttention2]] / [[positionalencoding]] / [[LayerNormalization]] / [[PreNorm]] / [[FeedForwardNetwork]] / [[GreedyDecoding]] / [[ContextLength]] / [[AutoregressiveLanguageModel]] (concept pages) + [[Phi3Mini]] / [[Llama]] / [[HandsOnLLM]] / [[JayAlammar]] / [[MaartenGrootendorst]] / [[HuggingFace]] / [[OReilly]] / [[GPT3]] (entity pages) in place. No direct contradictions; minor consistency notes recorded on the source page (RoPE timing of injection, GQA/MQA framing, FlashAttention scope, KV cache framing — all consistent with existing wiki content; the *"feedforward stores facts"* claim aligns with the mechanistic-interpretability literature but is not directly cited).

> **Update 2026-05-23 — [[hands-on-llm-ch02-tokens-and-embeddings|*Hands-On Large Language Models* Ch 2: Tokens and Embeddings]] ([[JayAlammar|Jay Alammar]] & [[MaartenGrootendorst|Maarten Grootendorst]]; [[OReilly|O'Reilly Media]] 2024, ISBN 978-1-098-15096-9) ingested.** **Second chapter of *Hands-On LLMs*** following [[hands-on-llm-ch01-introduction-to-llms|Ch 1]] ingested earlier this session. The chapter is **the wiki's canonical deep-dive on tokenization and embeddings** — the two layers that bookend every modern LLM call. Three movements: (1) **comparative empirical tour of seven trained tokenizers** ([[bert|BERT]] uncased + cased / [[GPT2|GPT-2]] / [[FLANT5|Flan-T5]] / [[GPT4|GPT-4]] / [[StarCoder2]] / [[Galactica]] / [[Phi3Mini|Phi-3]] reusing the [[Llama]] 2 tokenizer) on a single contrived test string designed to expose how each handles capitalization, multilingual characters, emoji, Python code, whitespace, digits, and [[SpecialToken|special tokens]]; (2) the **[[TokenEmbedding|token-embedding]] / [[ContextualEmbedding|contextualized-word-embedding]] distinction**, illustrated by running [[deberta|DeBERTa v3]] (`microsoft/deberta-v3-xsmall`) on `"Hello world"` and inspecting the resulting `[1, 4, 384]` output tensor — *"A language model operates on raw, static embeddings as its input and produces contextual text embeddings"*; (3) **[[Word2Vec|word2vec]] as the conceptual ancestor** of all modern embeddings — the chapter walks [[SkipGram|skip-gram]] + [[NegativeSampling|negative-sampling]] training end-to-end, then re-applies the same algorithm to **music playlists** to build the [[Word2VecRecommender|song-embedding recommender]] (Billie Jean → Kiss / Wanna Be Startin' Somethin' / Holiday; California Love → Nas / Puff Daddy / Notorious B.I.G.; Fade To Black → Van Halen / Dio / Guns N' Roses / Judas Priest) — same-genre recommendations recovered without any genre / artist supervision. The chapter's central claim: **three design factors dictate tokenizer behavior** — (a) tokenization **algorithm** ([[BPE]] / [[WordPiece]] / [[SentencePiece]] / [[CharacterTokenization|character]] / [[ByteLevelTokenization|byte]]), (b) **parameters** ([[VocabularySize|vocabulary size]] — 30K (BERT) → 50K (GPT-2) → ~100K (GPT-4) trend, [[SpecialToken|special tokens]], capitalization treatment), and (c) **training dataset domain** (English text vs code vs multilingual vs scientific). **Code-tokenizer findings**: [[GPT4|GPT-4]] / [[StarCoder2]] / [[Galactica]] use whitespace-run-as-single-token; [[StarCoder2]] / [[Galactica]] use **per-digit tokenization** (`600` → `6 0 0`) hypothesizing better math representation; [[Galactica]] adds `[START_REF]` / `[END_REF]` for citations and `<work>` for chain-of-thought reasoning; chat models add `<\|user\|>` / `<\|assistant\|>` / `<\|system\|>` role tokens; [[GPT4|GPT-4]] / [[StarCoder2]] add [[FillInTheMiddle|fill-in-the-middle]] tokens for code completion. **The model holds embeddings for the vocabulary of its tokenizer** — *"a pretrained language model is linked with its tokenizer and can't use a different tokenizer without training."* The embedding matrix is randomly initialized and trained jointly with the rest of the model; the input static embeddings are looked up by token ID and then transformed by attention layers into per-position contextualized embeddings. **Contextualized embeddings power not just NER / extractive summarization / classification but also AI image generation** ([[DALLE|DALL·E]], [[Midjourney]], [[StableDiffusion|Stable Diffusion]]) — they serve as the text-conditioning signal that aligns generated images with prompts. **Text embeddings** ([[TextEmbedding|one vector per sentence/document]]) are introduced as the foundation for [[rag|RAG]] + [[SemanticSearch|semantic search]] in Part II — demonstrated with [[SentenceTransformers|sentence-transformers]] `all-mpnet-base-v2` (768-dim) per Reimers & Gurevych 2019 *Sentence-BERT*. **Skip-gram + negative sampling is the contrastive-learning prototype** — *"This idea of a model that takes two vectors and predicts if they have a certain relation is one of the most powerful ideas in machine learning"* — directly forward-referencing Ch 10 (sentence-embedding contrastive training) and Ch 9 (image-caption alignment in multimodal models). **Position in the wiki**: complements rather than contradicts existing wiki content. Where [[d2l-nlp-pretraining|D2L NLP Pretraining]] is the **formal-math** treatment of word2vec / BPE / WordPiece / subword embedding, and [[ai-engineering-ch01-intro|Huyen Ch 1]] is the **practitioner-framing** of why tokenize at all, *Hands-On LLMs* Ch 2 is the **comparative empirical tour** — what each modern tokenizer actually does to a fixed test string, and how the design choices map to model capabilities (code-aware tokenization for code models, citation tokens for scientific models, etc.). **Mints 15 concept pages** ([[SpecialToken]], [[FillInTheMiddle]], [[TokenEmbedding]], [[StaticEmbedding]], [[TextEmbedding]], [[SentenceEmbedding]], [[Word2VecRecommender]], [[ByteLevelTokenization]], [[WordTokenization]], [[CharacterTokenization]], [[SepToken]], [[PadToken]], [[UnkToken]], [[MaskToken]], [[VocabularySize]]) + **14 entity pages** ([[StarCoder2]], [[FLANT5]], [[deberta]], [[SentenceTransformers]], [[AllMPNetBaseV2]], [[Gensim]], [[NilsReimers]], [[IrynaGurevych]], [[ShuoChen]], [[CornellUniversity]], [[FrankHerbert]], [[MichaelJackson]], [[Metallica]], [[DALLE]]) — and **upgrades the existing [[Galactica]] concept page** from stub to substantive treatment of its tokenizer design choices. Updates [[Tokenization]] / [[BPE]] / [[wordpiece]] / [[sentencepiece]] / [[SubwordEmbedding]] / [[Tokenizer]] / [[Embedding]] / [[WordEmbedding]] / [[ContextualEmbedding]] / [[Word2Vec]] / [[SkipGram]] / [[CBOW]] / [[NegativeSampling]] / [[NoiseContrastiveEstimation]] / [[ClassificationToken]] / [[ClsToken]] / [[GloVe]] / [[FastText]] / [[bert]] / [[GPT2]] / [[GPT4]] (concept pages) + [[Phi3Mini]] / [[Llama]] / [[HuggingFace]] / [[OReilly]] / [[microsoft]] / [[meta]] / [[StableDiffusion]] / [[Midjourney]] / [[HandsOnLLM]] / [[JayAlammar]] / [[MaartenGrootendorst]] (entity pages) in place via *From Hands-On LLMs Ch 2* sections. No direct contradictions with prior wiki content; minor consistency notes flagged on [[Tokenization]] (vocab sizes consistent across sources), [[NoiseContrastiveEstimation]] (Ch 2's pragmatic NCE → negative-sampling framing is consistent with the [[d2l-nlp-pretraining|D2L]] formal treatment), and on the word2vec method labels (Ch 2 introduces only skip-gram by name, consistent with the wiki's [[SkipGram]] / [[CBOW]] pages).

> **Update 2026-05-23 — [[hands-on-llm-ch01-introduction-to-llms|*Hands-On Large Language Models* Ch 1: An Introduction to Large Language Models]] ([[JayAlammar|Jay Alammar]] & [[MaartenGrootendorst|Maarten Grootendorst]]; [[OReilly|O'Reilly Media]] 2024, ISBN 978-1-098-15096-9) ingested.** **The wiki's second O'Reilly 2024 LLM book** (after [[ai-engineering-chip-huyen|Chip Huyen's *AI Engineering*]]) and **third overall LLM-engineering / pedagogy book** (after *AI Engineering* and the [[LLMEngineersHandbook|*LLM Engineer's Handbook*]]). Chapter 1 frames the book's pedagogical stance — **[[LanguageAI|Language AI]]** as the umbrella term (≈ [[NLP]], but broader; admits retrieval and other LLM-adjacent tech), a deliberately **permissive [[LargeLanguageModel|LLM]] definition** that includes sub-1B encoder-only [[RepresentationModel|representation models]] alongside the canonical decoder-only [[GenerativeModel|generative models]], and the recurring *"this book is for the GPU-poor"* compute target ([[GoogleColab|Google Colab]] T4 / 16 GB VRAM minimum). Walks the compressed history of Language AI: [[BagOfWords|bag-of-words]] → [[Word2Vec|word2vec]] (2013) → [[encoderdecoder|RNN encoder-decoder]] + [[Attention|attention]] (Bahdanau-Cho-Bengio 2014) → [[transformer|Transformer]] ([[1706.03762-attention-is-all-you-need|Vaswani et al. 2017]]) → [[bert|BERT]] encoder-only (2018) vs [[GPT|GPT-1]] decoder-only (2018) → scale-up to [[GPT3|GPT-3]] 175B → [[ChatGPT]] (Nov 2022, 1M users in 5 days, 100M in 2 months) marking 2023 as **"the Year of Generative AI."** Codifies the two-step LLM training paradigm — [[pretraining|pretraining]] (next-token prediction on internet-scale text → [[FoundationModel|base / foundation model]]) → [[FineTuning|fine-tuning]] (narrower data → [[InstructModel|instruct / chat model]]) — and anchors training cost to [[meta|Meta's]] [[Llama|Llama 2]] (2 trillion tokens, 3.3M GPU-hours, >$5M). Names five responsibility considerations (bias, transparency, harmful content, IP, regulation — citing the **[[EuropeanAIAct|European AI Act]]**), surveys application surfaces (classification, clustering, semantic search + [[rag|RAG]], chatbots, multimodal), and closes with a runnable **Hugging Face `transformers` + [[Phi3Mini|Phi-3-mini]]** worked example that produces the chapter's first generated text (*"Why don't chickens like to go to the gym? Because they can't crack the egg-sistence of it!"*). **Position in the wiki**: complements rather than contradicts the existing wiki's coverage. Where [[ai-engineering-ch01-intro|Chip Huyen Ch 1]] frames the *discipline* of AI engineering and the [[LLMEngineersHandbook|LLM Engineer's Handbook]] follows a *production project* end-to-end, *Hands-On LLMs* is the **pure pedagogical introduction to the model class itself** — historical arc + architectural taxonomy + responsible-use framing + a runnable first example. The book's **representation-model vs generative-model** two-bucket organizing axis is a clearer codification than the wiki previously had. **Soft definitional tension** with [[ai-engineering-ch01-intro|Huyen's framing]]: Alammar & Grootendorst's permissive LLM admits sub-1B encoder-only models that Huyen's framing implicitly excludes — flagged on [[LargeLanguageModel]] as a definitional difference, not a contradiction. **Mints 11 concept pages** ([[LanguageAI]], [[BagOfWords]], [[RepresentationModel]], [[GenerativeModel]], [[CompletionModel]], [[InstructModel]], [[ContextLength]], [[ContextWindow]], [[OpenSourceLLM]], [[ProprietaryLLM]], [[VRAM]]) + **14 entity pages** ([[JayAlammar]], [[MaartenGrootendorst]], [[HandsOnLLM]], [[Cohere]], [[JacobDevlin]], [[AshishVaswani]], [[DzmitryBahdanau]], [[YoshuaBengio]], [[JohnMcCarthy]], [[Phi3Mini]], [[LMStudio]], [[KoboldCpp]], [[TextGenerationWebui]], [[EuropeanAIAct]]). Updates **[[Word2Vec]] / [[bert]] / [[transformer]] / [[encoderdecoder]] / [[selfattention]] / [[Attention]] / [[maskedlanguagemodel]] / [[Tokenization]] / [[WordEmbedding]] / [[Embedding]] / [[LanguageModel]] / [[LargeLanguageModel]] / [[FoundationModel]] / [[pretraining]] / [[FineTuning]] / [[Mamba]] / [[RWKV]] / [[RNN]] / [[Hallucination]] / [[CommonCrawl]]** (concept pages) and **[[OReilly]] / [[HuggingFace]] / [[openai]] / [[anthropic]] / [[meta]] / [[microsoft]] / [[google]] / [[Mistral]] / [[Llama]] / [[ChatGPT]] / [[GPT]] / [[AlecRadford]] / [[TomasMikolov]] / [[LangChain]] / [[llamacpp]] / [[GoogleColab]]** (entity pages) in place. **First wiki source from the Alammar / Grootendorst pedagogical line** — the *Illustrated Transformer* / *Illustrated BERT* / *Illustrated GPT-2* blog-post heritage Alammar is best known for, paired with Grootendorst's BERTopic / KeyBERT open-source NLP work. No direct contradictions with prior wiki content.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[LLMEngineersHandbook|LLM Engineer's Handbook]] (Iusztin, Labonne, Vesa; [[PacktPublishing|Packt]] 2024) ingested as 11 chapters.** **The wiki's first end-to-end LLM-engineering book** — a single project ([[LLMTwin|LLM Twin]] — *"a digital projection of yourself into an LLM"*) followed from data crawling through MLOps, complementing the wiki's research-paper line ([[MIPROv2|MIPRO]] / [[2407.10930-better-together|BetterTogether]] / [[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA]] / [[2507.03152-medval|MedVAL]] / [[dspy-guardrails|DSPy Guardrails]]) with a production-system perspective. **Architectural through-line**: the **[[FTIArchitecture|Feature / Training / Inference (FTI) pipeline pattern]]** (credited to [[JimDowling|Jim Dowling]] of [[Hopsworks]]) replaces the [[MonolithicBatchArchitecture|monolithic batch]] and [[StatelessRealTimeArchitecture|stateless real-time]] anti-patterns; each pipeline is independently scalable, replaceable, and team-ownable, and a versioned shared store between them eliminates the [[TrainingServingSkew|training-serving skew]] failure mode. The book's reference design adds a fourth **[[DataCollectionPipeline|data-collection pipeline]]** (data-engineering-owned) in front of the FTI trio (ML-engineering-owned). **Chapter scope**: (1) framing the LLM Twin + FTI pattern; (2) the toolchain — [[Pyenv]] / [[PoetryPython|Poetry]] / [[PoeThePoet]] for Python, [[ZenML]] (with `@pipeline` / `@step` + artifact/metadata system + "stack" abstraction) as the orchestrator, [[CometML]] + [[Opik]] for tracking + prompt monitoring, [[MongoDB]] + [[Qdrant]] for storage, and [[AmazonSageMaker]] + ECR + S3 + IAM for AWS; (3) data engineering — a [[BuilderPattern|builder]]-pattern [[ETL]] crawler over Medium / Substack / GitHub / LinkedIn, a hand-rolled [[ODM]] on `pymongo` + [[Pydantic]], and the explicit "MongoDB as data warehouse for unstructured text" stance; (4) a comprehensive [[rag|RAG]] primer (vanilla and advanced taxonomy: sliding-window [[Chunking]], small-to-big, HyDE, query routing, query rewriting, query expansion, self-query, hybrid search with [[BM25]], filtered vector search, prompt compression, cross-encoder reranking) + a batch ZenML feature pipeline into Qdrant with a custom **Object-Vector Mapping** (OVM) layer; (5) supervised fine-tuning — instruction-dataset construction (LIMA / Alpaca / Evol-Instruct / UltraFeedback / [[LLMAsAJudge]] / reward models / MinHash + semantic-similarity dedup), the three SFT techniques (full FT / [[lora|LoRA]] / [[QLoRA]]) with explicit memory math (**16 bytes/param** baseline → 112 GB for 7B in FP32; QLoRA reduces peak GPU memory ~75% vs LoRA), chat templates (ChatML / Alpaca / Llama 3 / Phi-3 / Gemma), and the worked `mlabonne/TwinLlama-3.1-8B` fine-tune of `meta-llama/Meta-Llama-3.1-8B` on a 3,335-pair `mlabonne/llmtwin` dataset using [[Unsloth]] + [[TRL]] in ~50 minutes on an A100; (6) preference alignment — derives [[DirectPreferenceOptimization|DPO]]'s closed-form policy from the standard [[rlhf|RLHF]] + PPO + KL objective, then DPO-fine-tunes the SFT model to `…-DPO` with `beta=0.5`, `lr=2e-6`, 1 epoch; (7) evaluation — public benchmarks ([[mmlu]] / HellaSwag / ARC-C / Winogrande / PIQA / [[ifeval]] / Chatbot Arena / AlpacaEval / MT-Bench / [[gaia]]), domain leaderboards (Open Medical-LLM / BigCodeBench / Hallucinations / Enterprise Scenarios), classical metrics ([[ROUGE]] / [[F1Score]]), [[LLMAsAJudge|LLM-as-judge]] with bias mitigations, and two RAG-eval frameworks — [[RAGAS|Ragas]] and ARES; (8) inference optimization — KV cache + `torch.compile` (~4× forward-pass speedup), [[ContinuousBatching]], speculative decoding / Medusa / prompt-lookup, [[flashattention|FlashAttention-2]] + PagedAttention (up to 55% memory savings, 2.2× throughput), [[DataParallelism|DP]] + pipeline + [[TensorParallelism|TP]] parallelism, and quantization formats (BF16 / FP16 / INT8 / NF4 / LLM.int8 / GGUF / GPTQ / EXL2 / AWQ / QuIP# / HQQ), compared across **TGI**, **vLLM**, and **TensorRT-LLM**; (9) advanced-RAG inference — query expansion + self-querying + filtered vector search + cross-encoder reranking, in a single `ContextRetriever` orchestrator and a five-line `rag()` function; (10) [[MicroservicesArchitecture|microservices]] deployment — [[FastAPI]] business service + [[AmazonSageMaker]] LLM endpoint inside a [[HuggingFaceDLC|Hugging Face DLC]] running [[TextGenerationInference|TGI]], with full [[ApplicationAutoScaling]] + [[TargetTrackingScaling]] + [[CooldownPeriod|cooldown]] machinery and a curl smoke test; (11) [[MLOps]] → [[LLMOps]] — GitHub Actions [[CICD]] (gitleaks → Ruff → pytest → Docker → ECR), a [[ContinuousTraining|CT]] pipeline that chains data + feature + training + deploy via ZenML on the `aws-stack`, [[Opik]] prompt-trace monitoring of `rag()` / `call_llm_service()`, ZenML alerters fanning out to Slack/Discord/email, and a drift-detection appendix (KS / chi-squared / MMD on dimensionality-reduced embeddings) plus [[CheckList]]-style behavioral testing of NLP models. **Position alongside prior wiki content**: the book is the **canonical production-system anchor** for many topics the wiki previously held only at the research-paper level — [[lora]] / [[QLoRA]] (Ch 5 grounds the wiki's existing pages with concrete memory math and target-module recipes), [[rlhf]] / [[DirectPreferenceOptimization|DPO]] (Ch 6 grounds the alignment line), [[flashattention]] / [[ContinuousBatching]] / [[TensorParallelism]] (Ch 8 grounds the inference-side performance line), [[MLOps]] / [[CICD]] / [[ModelRegistry]] / [[FeatureStore]] / [[ExperimentTracking]] / [[DataDrift]] / [[TargetDrift]] / [[ConceptDrift]] / [[CheckList]] / [[BehavioralTesting]] (Ch 11 grounds the entire MLOps line with a six-principle framing the wiki had only sketched).
