The book also introduces the **[[LLMOps]]** vocabulary as the proper parent of "prompt monitoring", "input/output guardrails", and "human-feedback loops" — concepts the [[dspy-guardrails|DSPy Guardrails paper]] and [[2603.19247-prompt-optimization-jailbreaking|the SIMBA jailbreaking paper]] both probe from narrow angles. **Live tensions** with prior wiki content: (i) the book labels MongoDB a "data warehouse," which is a deliberate redefinition relative to orthodox warehouse usage (warehouses are typically relational/columnar); (ii) the book is mildly skeptical of [[LangChain]] (*"many developers avoid using LangChain in production"*), in tension with the more enthusiastic LangChain framings in the wiki's DSPy corpus; (iii) the recommended LoRA recipe (rank 32 / alpha 32 / Q+K+V+O+MLP) differs from BetterTogether's (rank 32 / alpha 64 / Q+K only) — different but compatible operating points; (iv) the book's claim that GEPA-style optimizers belong in the LLMOps loop is consistent with [[GEPA]]'s positioning but the book itself does not run any of the wiki's preferred optimizers — flagged as a synthesis opportunity. **Mints ~50 new concept pages** (architectural: [[LLMTwin]], [[FTIArchitecture]], [[MonolithicBatchArchitecture]], [[StatelessRealTimeArchitecture]], [[MonolithicArchitecture]], [[MicroservicesArchitecture]], [[DataCollectionPipeline]], [[LogicalFeatureStore]], [[GoogleCloudMLOpsReference]]; product: [[DigitalTwin]], [[CoPilot]], [[MinimumViableProduct]], [[PromptEngineering]]; MLOps/LLMOps: [[LLMOps]], [[ContinuousTraining]], [[PromptMonitoring]], [[Artifact]], [[Orchestrator]], [[Materializer]], [[DirectedAcyclicGraph]], [[Serverless]]; data: [[VectorDatabase]], [[Chunking]], [[Embedding]], [[InstructDataset]], [[BM25]], [[WebCrawling]]; data-mapping: [[ODM]], [[ORM]], [[CRUD]], [[Polymorphism]]; alignment: [[DirectPreferenceOptimization]] / [[DPO]]; inference: [[ContinuousBatching]], [[TensorParallelism]], [[InferenceOptimization]], [[OnlineRealTimeInference]], [[AsynchronousInference]], [[OfflineBatchTransform]]; AWS deployment: [[AWSSageMakerInferenceEndpoint]], [[HuggingFaceDLC]], [[ApplicationAutoScaling]], [[TargetTrackingScaling]], [[ScalableTarget]], [[ScalingPolicy]], [[CooldownPeriod]], [[ApplicationLoadBalancer]], [[RoundRobinRouting]], [[InferenceComponent]], [[InvokeEndpoint]], [[GPUUtilization]], [[IAMUser]], [[IAMRole]], [[ARN]]; protocols: [[REST]], [[RESTAPI]], [[JSON]], [[Protobuf]], [[gRPC]], [[ServerSentEvents]], [[WebSockets]]) + **~50 new entity pages** (authors and publisher: [[PaulIusztin]], [[MaximeLabonne]], [[AlexVesa]], [[PacktPublishing]], [[LLMEngineersHandbook]], [[ChipHuyen]], [[JimDowling]], [[Hopsworks]]; AWS services: [[AmazonBedrock]], [[AmazonCloudWatch]], [[AmazonECR]], [[AmazonECS]], [[AmazonEKS]], [[AWSCLI]]; orchestrators and MLOps: [[ZenML]], [[Airflow]], [[Prefect]], [[Dagster]], [[Metaflow]], [[ArgoWorkflows]], [[Opik]], [[Langfuse]], [[LangSmith]], [[Galileo]]; model families and products: [[ChatGPT]], [[GPT]], [[Llama]], [[Mistral]], [[TwinLlama]]; deployment alternatives: [[Modal]], [[Seldon]], [[GoogleCloudVertexAI]], [[AzureML]], [[AzureOpenAI]]). Updates [[MLOps]] / [[rag]] / [[FineTuning]] / [[lora]] / [[QLoRA]] / [[rlhf]] / [[flashattention]] / [[AmazonSageMaker]] / [[HuggingFace]] / [[CometML]] / [[MLflow]] / [[FastAPI]] / [[CICD]] / [[Hallucination]] / [[BootstrapFinetune]] / [[Llama3_8BInstruct]] / [[Mistral7BInstructV02]] / [[anthropic]] / [[openai]] / [[meta]] / [[google]] / [[microsoft]] / [[NVIDIA]] / [[Databricks]] / [[GitHub]] / [[GitHubActions]] in place via sibling-agent edits.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[vanroy-programming-paradigms-for-dummies|"Programming Paradigms for Dummies: What Every Programmer Should Know" ([[PeterVanRoy|Peter Van Roy]], [[UCL]], 2009; chapter in *New Computational Paradigms for Computer Music*, Assayag & Gerzso eds., IRCAM/Delatour)]] ingested.** **Second wiki source from the programming-language-theory research line** (after the [[vol1000-oz-programming-model|1995 Smolka OPM paper]] ingested earlier this session) and the **programmer-facing sibling** of that formal-model paper. Where Smolka exposed the [[OPM|Oz Programming Model]] as a kernel calculus, Van Roy places [[Oz]] in a **27-paradigm taxonomy** and articulates the design principles behind multiparadigm programming-language design. The 39-page chapter is the **canonical paradigm-survey entry point** for a reader needing orientation across the design space, organizing paradigms along two axes — **[[ObservableNondeterminism|observable nondeterminism]]** (yes/no) and **expressiveness of [[NamedState|state]]** (none / unnamed / named, sequential / concurrent / nondeterministic) — and naming the **four most important [[ProgrammingConcept|programming concepts]]**: **records**, **[[Closure|lexically scoped closures]]** (*"the most expressive concept ever invented in programming"*), **independence ([[Concurrency|concurrency]])**, and **[[NamedState|named state]]** (essential for **modularity** — without it, adding a private counter to a module forces every caller to rewrite their code). Surfaces the **[[CreativeExtensionPrinciple|creative extension principle]]** (Felleisen 1990) as the design guide: pervasive (nonlocal) program modifications signal a new concept waiting to be discovered (concurrency, named state, exceptions). The **central empirical finding** is the **convergence of four independent definitive-language projects** on the same four-layer architecture (Table 1): **functional core → [[DeclarativeConcurrency|deterministic declarative concurrency]] → [[MessagePassingConcurrency|asynchronous message passing]] → [[NamedState|global named state]]** — independently arrived at by **[[Erlang]]** (Armstrong, Ericsson 1986+, telecom high-availability), **[[E_Language|E]]** (Miller, Barnes — secure distributed systems), **[[DistributedOz|Distributed Oz]]** ([[GertSmolka|Smolka]] / Schulte / [[PeterVanRoy|Van Roy]], PERDIO project at [[DFKI]] 1995, [[MozartProgrammingSystem|Mozart]] released 1999), and **[[CTM|Didactic Oz]]** ([[CTM|Van Roy & Haridi]] MIT Press 2004 textbook + UCL FSAB1402 course since 2005). The convergence motivates Van Roy's policy recommendations for the multi-core era: (1) **[[DeclarativeConcurrency|declarative concurrency]] is the recommended default for multi-core programming** (confluent, no race conditions, parallelism is a scheduling problem); (2) **[[MessagePassingConcurrency|message passing]] is the recommended default for general-purpose concurrent programming** (over [[SharedStateConcurrency|shared-state concurrency]]); (3) **[[Closure|closures]] are the unifying abstraction** behind functions, procedures, objects, classes, components, and aspect-oriented separation of concerns. Presents **four [[ConcurrencyParadigm|concurrent paradigms]] with no observable nondeterminism** (Table 2): [[DeclarativeConcurrency|declarative concurrency]] (eager + lazy [[DataflowVariable|dataflow]] via `NewVar` / `Bind` / `Wait` / `WaitNeeded`), [[ConcurrentConstraintProgramming|constraint programming]] ([[Gecode]] / [[Numerica]]), [[FunctionalReactiveProgramming|FRP]] ([[FrTime]] / [[Yampa]] — continuous time), [[DiscreteSynchronousProgramming|discrete synchronous]] ([[Esterel]] / [[Lustre]] / [[Signal]] / [[Faust]] — discrete-time Mealy machines). Closes with the **[[SelfSufficientSystem|self-sufficient-systems]] architecture** for large-scale software: interacting [[FeedbackLoop|feedback loops]] coordinated through **[[Stigmergy|stigmergy]]** (shared subsystem) and **management** (direct control). Real-world worked examples: TCP as a 2-loop structure (inner reliable-transfer / outer congestion-control), human respiratory system as a 4-loop structure. **Live tensions** with prior wiki content (none rise to contradictions): (i) the [[DiveIntoSystems]] / [[TheEmbeddedRustBook]] concurrency vocabulary inhabits the **named + nondeterministic + concurrent** corner of Van Roy's state lattice (the hardest-to-reason-about corner), which Van Roy explicitly recommends *against* as a default — flagged for future synthesis; (ii) Van Roy's *"composition over inheritance"* OOP discipline aligns with modern Java / C++ guidance but pushes harder than typical textbook coverage; (iii) the modern *agentic-AI* vocabulary ([[AgenticAI]], [[react|`dspy.ReAct`]]) uses "agent" in a different sense than Van Roy's concurrent-PL [[Agent|Agent]] from [[vol1000-oz-programming-model]] (LLM-driven decision loop vs. concurrent message-handler) — the wiki now has both meanings indexed. **Position in the wiki**: the wiki's **first paradigm-taxonomy** entry, **first computer-music research context** ([[IRCAM]] / [[OpenMusic]] / [[Antescofo]] / [[MaxMSP]] / [[Faust]] all referenced), and the **canonical entry point** to the [[Oz]] / [[OPM]] / [[ConcurrentConstraintProgramming]] / [[DataflowVariable|dataflow]] / [[DefinitiveLanguage]] research line for readers coming from the wiki's ML / DL cluster. Heavily cross-references the [[vol1000-oz-programming-model|prior Smolka ingest]] — all 12 OPM-derived concept pages are reused without modification. **Mints 23 concept pages** + **3 entity pages** ([[PeterVanRoy]], [[UCL]], [[IRCAM]]). No direct contradictions with prior wiki content.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[dspy-rag-tutorial|DSPy Tutorial — Retrieval-Augmented Generation]] ingested.** **Fourth wiki-corpus [[DSPy]] tutorial** (after [[dspy-conversation-history]], [[dspy-customer-service-agent]], [[dspy-custom-module]]) — and the **first DSPy receipt in the wiki to combine all four [[DSPyProgrammingModel|Programming-Model artifacts]]** ([[DSPySignatures|Signatures]] / [[DSPyModules|Modules]] / [[DSPyMetrics|Metrics]] / [[DSPyOptimizers|Optimizers]]) end-to-end on a single task with measurable per-stage gains. The tutorial walks a three-stage performance ladder on [[RAGQAArenaTech|RAG-QA Arena Tech]] (~1K technical QA pairs, 200/300/500 train/dev/test): baseline [[chainofthought|`dspy.ChainOfThought`]] (no retrieval) **42%** [[SemanticF1]] → [[rag|RAG]] module wrapping [[chainofthought|CoT]] over `k=5` embedding-retrieved docs **55.5%** → [[MIPROv2|`dspy.MIPROv2`]] `auto="medium"` over the RAG module **61.1%**. The **+13 / +6 split** is the canonical wiki demonstration that **architecture (RAG)** buys more than **optimization** on QA the bare LM cannot already do — but optimization on top of architecture is additive. Three load-bearing details: (i) **the metric is [[SemanticF1]]** — a reference-based [[llmasjudge|LLM-as-judge]] DSPy program that decomposes both gold and predicted answers into atomic claims and grades coverage, instantiating the [[dspy-metrics|AI-feedback metric pattern]] from page 11 of the *Learn* corpus and therefore itself **recursively optimizable** ([[DSPyEvaluation|Step 4]] of the four-step evaluation loop); (ii) **retrieval uses [[openai|OpenAI]] `text-embedding-3-small`** over a 28K-document downsampled technical corpus, each doc truncated to 6K characters — the **first wiki-corpus DSPy RAG receipt to use dense embeddings** (the [[dspy-custom-module|Custom Module tutorial]] used [[ColBERTv2]] late interaction over Wikipedia 2017 abstracts); (iii) **`auto="medium"` costs ~$1.50 and runs in ~20–30 min** on this dataset — the first concrete instance of [[dspy-optimizers|the Optimizers page's]] *$2 typical / cents-to-tens-of-dollars range* operating envelope on a multi-stage RAG program. **Cross-receipt convergence with [[MIPROv2|MIPROv2 Receipt 2]]**: this is the **second worked instance** of the *single-hop CoT-based RAG on gpt-4o-mini optimized with `auto="medium"`* pattern (the first was the [[dspy-optimizers|Optimizers page's]] StackExchange subset, 53→61%). **Two independent benchmarks now converge on ~61% [[SemanticF1]] as the post-optimization ceiling** for this architecture × model × optimizer combination — strong evidence that the envelope is a property of the configuration, not idiosyncratic to either dataset. **Further-improvement menu** names 5 axes for going beyond 61.1%: [[hotpotqa|multi-hop]] retrieval, other optimizers ([[GEPA]] / [[BetterTogether]]), [[DSPyMajority|ensembling]] for inference-time scaling, [[BootstrapFinetune|distillation]] for serving cost reduction, and recursive metric refinement — each axis has a paper-anchored or tutorial-anchored sibling already in the wiki, so the tutorial is **also a map of the next layer of DSPy improvements** beyond a working end-to-end program. **Position in the wiki's DSPy line**: together with [[dspy-conversation-history]] / [[dspy-customer-service-agent]] / [[dspy-custom-module]] the four DSPy tutorials now cover application-stack rungs 2 (template + measurable), 3 (history), 4 (ReAct). [[dspy-rag-tutorial|This tutorial]] fills the **rung-2-measurable** slot — the same `class MyProgram(dspy.Module)` shape as [[dspy-custom-module]] but with a benchmark and an optimizer attached, demonstrating the framework's full Programming → Evaluation → Optimization arc on a single artifact. **Live tension with [[2604.14585-prompt-optimization-coin-flip|the coin-flip paper]]**: the +6-point MIPROv2 lift on free-form QA sits *above* the paper's 2-pt headroom threshold, so the tutorial's optimization win is consistent with the diagnostic — but the +6 is on a **specific** dataset / model / optimizer combination, and the [[ModelSpecificityShelfLife|model-specificity caveat]] applies. The coin-flip caveat is **not contradicted** — [[SemanticF1]] is precisely the kind of structured-output [[CanButDoesntPattern|"can but doesn't"]] regime the paper says optimization helps in (the LM-judged claim-coverage rubric is structured, not free-form). **Mints 2 concept pages** ([[SemanticF1]] — promoted from inline mention on [[MIPROv2]] / [[dspy-optimizers]] to a dedicated concept page; [[RAGQAArenaTech]] — the dataset). Updates [[DSPy]] entity in place with a new *"RAG: the canonical end-to-end measurable receipt"* section + extends the application-stack table with a rung-2-measurable slot; updates [[rag]] concept page in place with a two-receipt comparison table; updates [[MIPROv2]] concept page in place with the *Receipt 2b RAG-QA Arena Tech* worked instance + the cross-receipt-convergence claim; updates [[MLflow]] entity page sources list. **No contradictions** with prior wiki content.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[vol1000-oz-programming-model|"The Oz Programming Model" (Gert Smolka, [[DFKI]] Programming Systems Lab, LNCS Vol. 1000 pp. 324–343, July 1995)]] ingested.** **First wiki source from the programming-language-theory / concurrent-constraint-programming research line** — and the **oldest paper in the corpus**, predating the wiki's ML / DL cluster by ~25 years and the LLM-era papers by ~30. Smolka exposes the **[[OPM|Oz Programming Model]]** — a concurrent programming model unifying higher-order functional programming, object-oriented programming, and constraint logic programming as **facets of a single general model** ($\text{OPM} = \text{OCC} + \text{first-class procedures} + \text{cells} + \text{search combinator}$, where OCC is [[VijaySaraswat|Saraswat]]'s [[ConcurrentConstraintProgramming|concurrent constraint kernel]]). Computation lives in a **[[ComputationSpace|computation space]]** — tasks connected to a three-compartment store ([[ConstraintStore|constraint store]] / procedure store / cell store) — and tasks **synchronize on logic entailment** of guards. The synchronization mechanism is **monotonic** (*"a reducible task stays reducible if other tasks are reduced before it"*) and **completely declarative** (*"the order in which constraints are told is insignificant ... conjunction is an associative and commutative operation"*). Stateful concurrent abstractions — [[Port|ports]] / [[Agent|agents]] / [[OPMObject|objects]] — are **derived**, not primitive, built from [[Cell|cells]] + first-class procedures + names + lexical scoping; *"agents are stationary and objects are mobile"* under distribution (an agent serves at its creation site; an object serves at its application site). [[Propagator|Propagators]] accommodate expressive nonbasic constraints ($x + y = z$, Boolean ordering) as tasks waiting for sufficient store information; **threads** are the reduction strategy *"as sequential as possible and as concurrent as necessary"*. The conservative extension via **choice combinator + search combinator** yields [[EncapsulatedSearch|encapsulated search]] — *"Oz gets constraint logic programming out of its problem solving ghetto and integrates it into a concurrent and lexically scoped language with first-class procedures and state."* Realized in the concurrent high-level language **[[Oz]]** (DFKI Oz implementation: GNU Emacs interface, concurrent browser, Tcl/Tk OO GUI, incremental compiler, garbage-collected emulator). **Live tension with the wiki's existing concurrency vocabulary**: the [[DiveIntoSystems]] / [[TheEmbeddedRustBook]] corpora ground concurrent programming in [[Pthreads]] / [[Mutex]] / [[Semaphore]] / [[ConditionVariable]] / [[Barrier]] (shared-memory imperative) and [[MPI]] / [[MessagePassing]] (distributed-memory imperative). OPM replaces those primitives with **logic entailment of guards over a monotonic declarative constraint store** — a fundamentally different (declarative, monotonic, lexically-scoped) discipline. Not a contradiction, a complementary point in the concurrency design space; flagged for future synthesis on *"what shared-memory threading and concurrent constraint programming would learn from each other."* **Mints 12 concept pages** ([[OPM]], [[Oz]], [[ConcurrentConstraintProgramming]], [[ComputationSpace]], [[ConstraintStore]], [[FirstClassProcedures]], [[EncapsulatedSearch]], [[Cell]], [[Port]], [[Agent]], [[OPMObject]], [[Propagator]]) + **2 entity pages** ([[GertSmolka]], [[DFKI]]) — the wiki's **first European AI-research-lab entity** and first programming-language-researcher entity distinct from the US-centric ML / DL author cluster. The 1995 paper appeared in *Computer Science Today* (J. van Leeuwen ed., LNCS Vol. 1000) — the volume celebrating 50 years of computer science. **Position in the wiki**: intellectual ancestor of every later constraint-programming system (Mozart/Oz, Gecode, Choco) and a foundational reference for the [[ConcurrencyVsParallelism|concurrency-as-discipline]] question. No direct contradictions with prior wiki content.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[dspy-custom-module|DSPy Tutorial — Building AI Applications by Customizing DSPy Modules]] ingested.** **Third wiki-corpus [[DSPy]] tutorial** (after [[dspy-conversation-history]] and [[dspy-customer-service-agent]]) and the **first** to anchor on the custom `class MyProgram(dspy.Module)` shape itself rather than on a downstream application (chatbot / agent). The tutorial commits to four positions: (1) **subclass `dspy.Module` and implement `__init__` + `forward`** — the two-method contract that mirrors PyTorch's `nn.Module`; (2) **`forward()` is unconstrained Python** — *"you are not limited to calling only other DSPy modules; you can also integrate any standard Python functions, such as those for interacting with Langchain/Agno agents, MCP tools, database handlers, and more"*; (3) **call the module instance directly, not `forward()` explicitly** — *"the `__call__` method handles necessary internal processing before executing the `forward` logic"*; (4) **custom modules unlock framework features** — [[DSPyOptimizers|DSPy Optimizer]] introspection and [[MLflow]] DSPy tracing both walk the `self.*` sub-module attributes set in `__init__`, so plain-function pipelines (or inline sub-module use outside any `dspy.Module`) get **neither**. The worked receipt is a **three-stage [[rag|RAG]] program** — `dspy.Predict(QueryGenerator)` → `dspy.ColBERTv2(url='http://20.102.90.50:2017/wiki17_abstracts')(query, k=1)` → [[chainofthought|`dspy.ChainOfThought("question,context->answer")`]] — the wiki's first end-to-end RAG code listing inside a [[DSPyModules|`dspy.Module`]] subclass and the canonical anchor for the **public Wikipedia 2017 abstracts [[ColBERTv2]] endpoint** that the [[dspy-modules|Modules page]], [[react|ReAct]] / [[hotpotqa|HotPotQA]] examples, [[MIPROv2]] / [[2407.10930-better-together|BetterTogether]] / [[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA]] optimizer demos, and [[2312.13382-dspy-assertions|DSPy Assertions]] tasks all assume but never document. Five load-bearing details in the RAG listing: (i) **class-based + inline-string [[DSPySignatures|Signatures]] compose freely** in the same `dspy.Module` (`QueryGenerator` is class-based; `"question,context->answer"` is inline); (ii) retrieval is a **plain Python function** wrapping `dspy.ColBERTv2(url=...)(query, k=1)`, **not** registered as a sub-module — so [[DSPyOptimizers|optimizers]] do not see retrieval as a tunable parameter; (iii) the canonical public [[ColBERTv2]] endpoint is `http://20.102.90.50:2017/wiki17_abstracts` (Wikipedia 2017 abstracts, same corpus as [[hotpotqa|HotPotQA]]); (iv) `forward` **unwraps** sub-module outputs by attribute access (`.query`, `.answer`); (v) `**kwargs` in `forward(self, question, **kwargs)` absorbs metadata kwargs `dspy.Module` may inject. The tutorial closes with the **string-in-string-out → structured-input-to-structured-output** operational summary of [[dspy-programming-overview|the Programming Overview's]] *writing code instead of strings* thesis, and the **bidirectional portability** claim — *"DSPy is easy to migrate to from other frameworks or vanilla SDK usage, and easy to migrate off because essentially it's just python code."* **Application-stack position**: the **template-level** anchor for **rung 2** of the [[DSPy]] application stack — the canonical worked starting receipt every other rung composes from ([[dspy-conversation-history]] adds rung-3 history; [[dspy-customer-service-agent]] adds rung-4 [[react|ReAct]] + typed [[Pydantic]] tools). **MLflow integration**: first wiki-corpus DSPy-specific [[MLflow]] tracing recipe — a four-step opt-in ending in `mlflow.dspy.autolog()`, the bridge hook that turns every `dspy.Module.__call__` into an MLflow trace span. [[Databricks]] owns [[MLflow]] — second [[Databricks]]-touchpoint in the [[DSPy]] line after [[MateiZaharia]]'s [[2406.11695-mipro|MIPRO]] / [[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA]] senior-author roles. **Live tension with [[2604.14585-prompt-optimization-coin-flip|Prompt Optimization Is a Coin Flip]]**: the tutorial's claim that *"custom modules unlock [[DSPyOptimizers|DSPy Optimizer]]"* is unaffected by the coin-flip paper's finding — the paper constrains *when* optimization helps (only when the task has [[CanButDoesntPattern|exploitable output structure]]), not whether the custom-module shape is the right surface to optimize against. **Mints 1 concept page** ([[ColBERTv2]] — promoted from long-standing forward reference carried by [[DSPyModules]] / [[DSPyOptimizers]] / [[DSPyPrediction]] / [[hotpotqa]] / [[BetterTogether]] / [[MIPROv2]] / [[react]] / [[chainofthought]] since the [[DSPy]] *Learn* corpus opened); updates [[DSPy]] entity in place with a new *"Custom modules: the canonical starting template"* section + updated three-tutorial stack-rung table + tracked source; updates [[DSPyModules]] concept page in place with a new *"Custom-module starting template"* section; updates [[MLflow]] entity page in place with the DSPy integration recipe; updates [[rag]] concept page in place with the canonical DSPy RAG receipt. No contradictions with prior wiki content.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[2604.14585-prompt-optimization-coin-flip|Prompt Optimization Is a Coin Flip (Zhang, Wang, Cui, Qiu, Li, Zhu & He, arXiv 2026)]] ingested.** The wiki's **first controlled empirical audit of [[PromptOptimization|prompt optimization]] in [[CompoundAISystem|compound AI systems]]** — a deliberate counterweight to the [[MIPROv2]] / [[BetterTogether]] / [[GEPA]] / [[2025-bionlp-archehr-qa-neural|Neural ArchEHR-QA]] thread the wiki has built up. [[XingZhang|Zhang]] / [[GuanghuiWang|Wang]] / [[YanweiCui|Cui]] ([[AWSGenerativeAIInnovationCenter|AWS Generative AI Innovation Center]]) + [[WeiQiu|Qiu]] / [[ZiyuanLi|Li]] / [[BingZhu|Zhu]] ([[HSBC]] Technology Center China) — corresponding [[PeiyangHe|Peiyang He]] — test the **two assumptions** behind end-to-end joint optimizers ([[TextGrad]], [[DSPy]]/[[MIPROv2]], [[GPTSwarm]], [[Helix]]): **(A)** individual agent prompts are worth optimizing; **(B)** agent prompts interact, requiring joint optimization. **Both fail in most conditions.** Across **72 optimization runs on [[ClaudeHaiku45|Claude Haiku 4.5]] (6 methods × 4 tasks × 3 repeats), 49% score *below* zero-shot** (binomial $p = 0.91$ — statistically indistinguishable from a fair coin); on [[AmazonNovaLite|Amazon Nova Lite]], 14/24 method×task means below zero-shot. **The $A \times B$ interaction term is non-significant in all six tested model×task conditions** ($p > 0.52$, all $F < 1.0$, 0.18–2.15% of total variance) — joint optimization is unnecessary for two-agent feed-forward pipelines. **Expert predictions about coupling were wrong**: [[hotpotqa|HotpotQA]] (the *a priori* "tight coupling" pick — multi-hop reasoning seemingly requires Agent B to build on Agent A's specific decomposition) showed the **smallest** interaction (0.18% on Haiku). **The visual signature**: $10 \times 10$ score matrices show row/column banding (additive structure) with no off-diagonal patterns; joint and independent optima are adjacent or identical (gap 0.0–3.3 pts). **Optimization helps only when the task has [[CanButDoesntPattern|exploitable output structure]]** — the *"can but doesn't" pattern* the paper coins: a format the model **can** produce but **does not default to**. The sole task where all 6 methods beat zero-shot ([[HelpSteer2]], best $+6.8$ pts from [[EvoPrompt]]) requires JSON-formatted rubric output — Haiku can produce this format (68.0 → 74.8 when prompted correctly) but its zero-shot default is unstructured prose. The other three tasks ([[FeedbackBench]] $-0.20$, [[WildBench]] $-0.82$, [[XSum]] $-0.17$ average gain) all accept **free-form natural language** — no latent capability gap for optimization to unlock. The paper introduces **[[PROSEOptimizer|PROSE]]** — the authors' own risk-aware evolutionary optimizer with Sharpe-ratio + DRO worst-case fitness penalties — and shows it offers **no measurable advantage** over simpler methods, a deliberate negative result reinforcing that optimization gains are fragile and model-specific rather than mechanism-driven. **[[ModelSpecificityShelfLife|Model specificity dominates]]**: which agent matters, which task benefits, and which method works all **reverse** with the executor model — [[HelpSteer2]] is 6/6 on Haiku but 1/6 on Nova; [[FeedbackBench]] is 1/6 on Haiku but 4/6 on Nova (complete reversal). **Constructive contribution**: a **two-stage [[CompoundAIDiagnostic|practitioner decision framework]]** that replaces the default joint-optimization assumption. **Stage 1 — [[ANOVAVarianceDecomposition|ANOVA coupling test]]** (~$80, 1 day): $10 \times 10$ prompt grid, $n=30$ samples, two-way ANOVA with question blocking; if $F_{A \times B} < 1$, optimize agents independently. **Stage 2 — [[HeadroomTest|headroom test]]** (~$5, 10 min): generate 10–20 candidate prompts; if best-of-N gain $< 2$ pts over zero-shot, the landscape is flat — use zero-shot. The two-stage diagnostic costs ~$85 total and would have ruled out the $1K–$10K [[TextGrad]] / [[DSPy]] compilation runs in 3 of 4 tasks studied. **Methodological contribution**: [[ANOVAVarianceDecomposition|ANOVA variance decomposition]] is offered as a **falsifiable evaluation protocol for compositional behavior in [[CompoundAISystem|compound AI systems]]** — a structured alternative to ad hoc aggregate-benchmark reporting. **The mechanistic hypothesis**: [[InstructionTuning|instruction-tuning]] + [[rlhf|RLHF]] compress diverse input phrasings into narrow output distributions, so Agent B's output variance is dominated by the semantic content of Agent A's response (set by the question), not by Agent A's stylistic variation (what prompt changes affect) — pipelines compose as **independently-robust functions**. **Open regimes where coupling may re-emerge** (untested): shared mutable state, schema dependence, feedback loops, 3+ agent pipelines, structured-data inter-agent communication. **Position in the wiki**: this is the **first paper to question the prompt-optimization research program empirically rather than methodologically**. It recasts the wiki's prior MIPRO / BetterTogether / GEPA / Neural-ArchEHR-QA successes as **conditional on output-structure latency** ([[CanButDoesntPattern|"can but doesn't"]] — conditional-rule on [[HotPotQAConditional]] for MIPRO, citation-format + six-metric composite reward on [[ArchEHRQA2025|ArchEHR-QA]] for Neural, JSON valid output on [[QuizGen]] for [[2312.13382-dspy-assertions|DSPy Assertions]]) rather than universal optimization wins. **Critically, it does not invalidate any of those results** — it constrains them to a specific structural regime and provides a cheap pre-test (the headroom diagnostic) for practitioners to gate optimization investment on. **Sibling structural-falsification paper**: [[2604.27707-agentic-memory-is-a-memo|Xu, Dai & Zhang (2026) — "Agentic Memory is a Memo"]] — same move: reduce a prevailing compound-AI claim to a falsifiable structural test and find the claim mostly fails. **First financial-services-affiliated co-author institution** in the wiki ([[HSBC]] Technology Center China) and **first AWS Generative AI Innovation Center** paper. **Mints 20 concept pages** ([[AgentCoupling]], [[ANOVAVarianceDecomposition]], [[HeadroomTest]], [[CanButDoesntPattern]], [[CoinFlipOptimization]], [[JointOptimization]], [[IndependentOptimization]], [[CompoundAIDiagnostic]], [[ModelSpecificityShelfLife]], [[PROSEOptimizer]], [[TextGrad]], [[GPTSwarm]], [[Helix]], [[PromptBreeder]], [[ProTeGi]], [[XSum]], [[MBPP]], [[HelpSteer2]], [[WildBench]], [[FeedbackBench]]) + **13 entity pages** ([[XingZhang]], [[GuanghuiWang]], [[YanweiCui]], [[WeiQiu]], [[ZiyuanLi]], [[BingZhu]], [[PeiyangHe]], [[AWSGenerativeAIInnovationCenter]], [[HSBC]], [[ClaudeHaiku45]], [[AmazonNovaLite]], [[ClaudeSonnet46]], [[Nie2026Iterative]]). Updates [[CompoundAISystem]] / [[PromptOptimization]] / [[MIPROv2]] / [[GEPA]] / [[BetterTogether]] / [[hotpotqa]] / [[Amazon]] / [[anthropic]] in place.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[2603.19247-prompt-optimization-jailbreaking|"When Prompt Optimization Becomes Jailbreaking" (Shamsi, Chekuru, Guzman & Garg, [[AlgoverseAIResearch|Algoverse AI Research]], arXiv:2603.19247v1, 21 Feb 2026)]] ingested.** **First wiki-corpus paper to use [[DSPy]] *offensively* — as an automated jailbreak generator** rather than as a helpful-task or defense pipeline. Repurposes three black-box [[DSPy]] optimizers — [[MIPROv2]] (*medium* config), [[GEPA]] (*light* config, [[gemini|Gemini-2.5-pro]] reflection model), and **[[SIMBA]]** (batch=16, ≤4 steps) — as adaptive system-prompt search engines whose reward is a continuous **[[DangerScore|danger score]] $r \in [0, 1]$** from a cross-family [[LLMAsAJudge|LLM judge]] ([[GPT51|GPT-5.1]] at $T=1.0$ — deliberately *not* in the same family as any target). Seeds: 150 prompts evenly drawn from [[HarmfulQA]] (DeCLaRe Lab 2023) + [[JailbreakBench]] (Chao et al., NeurIPS D&B 2024). **Four target LMs, three optimizers, baseline-vs-optimized danger** (Table 1): [[qwen|Qwen-3 8B]] **0.090 → 0.792** under [[SIMBA]] (8.8× rise — *most degraded*), [[LLaMA4Maverick|LLaMA-4 Maverick]] 0.215 → 0.623, [[ClaudeSonnet45|Claude 4.5 Sonnet]] 0.046 → 0.347 (7.5× — *largest multiplier*), [[gemini|Gemini 2.5 Pro]] 0.645 → 0.774. **SIMBA wins all four cells**; ordering **SIMBA > GEPA > MIPROv2 > baseline** is consistent across model families — *"the effect is driven by the optimization process rather than model-specific idiosyncrasies."* **Most surprising secondary finding**: the optimizer ordering **flips** the [[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA paper]]'s benign-task ranking (where GEPA dominates [[MIPROv2]]); on the adversarial objective [[SIMBA]] dominates GEPA on every cell. Not a direct contradiction — different objective spaces — but it softens any "GEPA dominates all DSPy optimizers" claim and **promotes [[SIMBA]] from third-row-of-the-GEPA-optimizer-table to a first-class paper-anchored concept**. Qualitative case study (Table 2): SIMBA-optimized prompts elicit detailed, actionable harmful content from each model — judicial-corruption tactics on Claude, school-shooting plans on Gemini, medical-billing fraud on LLaMA-4, minor-impersonation on Qwen. Methodological headline: *"static benchmarks may underestimate residual risk, indicating that automated, adaptive red-teaming is a necessary component of robust safety evaluation."* Inference via [[OpenRouter]] across the four-vendor stack. **Validation**: 25 of the ~1,800 (q, a, score) traces hand-reviewed and *"the assigned danger scores aligned with human judgment of response harmfulness"* — the rest are judge-only. **DSPy's wiki footprint now spans 4 application classes** — helpful prompt-optimization ([[MIPROv2]], [[2406.11695-mipro|MIPRO paper]], [[2407.10930-better-together|BetterTogether]]), helpful module / agent synthesis (the *Learn* corpus + [[dspy-customer-service-agent]]), **safety guardrails as defense** ([[dspy-guardrails|Yu & He 2024]] — same machinery, defensive side, drops [[CodeAttack]] [[AttackSuccessRate|ASR]] 75% → 5%), and **adversarial system-prompt search as offense** (this paper). Companion to [[AdversarialPromptSearch]] in the [[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA paper]] (Section 5 — drove GPT-5 Mini AIME-2025 pass@1 76% → 10% via universal-prefix attack) but on the *safety* surface rather than the *task-accuracy* surface. **Mints 4 concept pages** ([[SIMBA]], [[DangerScore]], [[HarmfulQA]], [[JailbreakBench]]) + **9 entity pages** ([[AlgoverseAIResearch]], [[ZafirShamsi]], [[NikhilChekuru]], [[ZacharyGuzman]], [[ShivankGarg]], [[LLaMA4Maverick]], [[ClaudeSonnet45]], [[GPT51]], [[OpenRouter]]); updates [[AdversarialPromptSearch]] / [[Jailbreak]] / [[LLMAsAJudge]] / [[AttackSuccessRate]] / [[MIPROv2]] / [[GEPA]] / [[qwen]] / [[DSPy]] / [[anthropic]] / [[meta]] / [[googledeepmind]] / [[gemini]] / [[openai]] in place. No direct contradictions with prior wiki content. **Live tension** with the [[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA paper]]'s ordering claim — Shamsi et al. report SIMBA > GEPA on every adversarial cell — formally on different objectives but worth flagging as an opening for follow-up evaluation in the helpful-task regime.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[dspy-customer-service-agent|DSPy Tutorial — Building AI Agents (Customer Service Agent)]] ingested.** **Second wiki-corpus [[DSPy]] tutorial** (after [[dspy-conversation-history]]) and the **canonical end-to-end production-shaped receipt for [[react|`dspy.ReAct`]]** — a seven-tool airline customer-service agent over a five-class [[Pydantic]] domain (`Date`, `UserProfile`, `Flight`, `Itinerary`, `Ticket`; nested at any depth — `Itinerary` ⊃ `Flight` + `UserProfile`, `Flight` ⊃ `Date`) driven by a two-field [[DSPySignatures|Signature]] (`user_request: str` → `process_result: str`). The tool list splits into the three production-agent categories — **lookup** (`fetch_flight_info` / `fetch_itinerary` / `get_user_info` / `pick_flight`), **mutation** (`book_flight` / `cancel_itinerary`), **escalation** (`file_ticket`) — establishing the discipline that every customer-service ReAct agent needs an escalation surface as the [[LLMModuloFramework|LLM-Modulo]] safety valve. Three structural properties: (i) **tools are plain Python functions with docstrings + type hints** — no `dspy.Tool(...)` wrapping required when passed into `dspy.ReAct(..., tools=[...])`; (ii) **the user-facing Signature is independent of the tool list** — two fields scope the user interface, seven tools scope the action surface; (iii) **[[Pydantic]] models compose at any depth** in tool arguments and returns. Returned [[DSPyPrediction|`Prediction`]] carries **three fields**: `trajectory` (full think-act-observe log), `reasoning` (final-decision explanation, same field [[ChainOfThought|`dspy.ChainOfThought`]] adds), and the user-declared `process_result` — only the last was declared on the Signature; the first two are added by [[react|`dspy.ReAct`]]'s under-the-hood signature expansion. `dspy.inspect_history()` is the canonical debugging surface — **cross-tutorial confirmation** that it's a standard cross-Module hook, not a Module-specific feature. **Application-stack position**: fills rung 4 of the DSPy application stack (single-agent multi-tool task) between [[dspy-conversation-history|rung 3 (multi-turn conversation)]] and [[2408.15232-co-storm|rung 5 (multi-agent collaborative discourse)]]; natural composition of rungs 1–3. **First wiki-corpus production-shaped LM agent** — every prior DSPy ingest scoped either toy benchmarks ([[hotpotqa|HotPotQA]] / [[GSM8K]] / [[Iris]] / [[Banking77]]) or research domains; the customer-service-agent shape (NL request → tool selection → structured action → user-facing response with confirmation) is the production application shape the corpus had been missing. **Scope-limit gap**: tutorial does **not** add [[DSPyAssertions|LM Assertions]] inside mutation tools — a production deployment needs `dspy.Assert(...)` checks (date in future, origin ≠ destination, user has email) inside `book_flight` etc. to prevent the agent from silently creating invalid records; the [[2402.01817-llm-modulo|LLM-Modulo]] sound-critic layer is the natural extension surface. **Mints 1 concept page** ([[CustomerServiceAgent]] — the general application pattern); updates [[react]] in place with a *reference receipt* section and [[DSPy]] entity with a "Customer service agent" section + updated 6-rung application stack table.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[dspy-conversation-history|DSPy Tutorial — Managing Conversation History]] ingested.** **First wiki-corpus [[DSPy]] page anchored to a *tutorial* rather than a *Learn* reference or paper** — scopes [[DSPy]] at the **multi-turn chatbot** application layer. Two load-bearing disclosures: (i) ***[[DSPy]] does not automate conversation-history management*** — *"DSPy does not provide such functionality out of the box, meaning users are required to manage the conversation history on their own"*; [[DSPyModules|`dspy.Module`]] has **no** built-in inter-turn state capture, the developer owns the lifecycle; (ii) **[[DSPyHistory|`dspy.History`]] is the [[DSPySignatures|Signature]] field type** — a DSPy-special tier-five type (alongside [[DSPyImage|`dspy.Image`]]) with a single attribute `messages: list[dict[str, Any]]`. Canonical chatbot loop is a `while True:` over [[DSPyPredict|`dspy.Predict`]], appending `{"question": question, **outputs}` per turn. **Structural disclosure under [[BootstrapFewShot|few-shot]]**: history-bearing [[DSPyExample|`dspy.Example`]] demos render as a **single turn with the `messages` list serialized as JSON** — *"DSPy renders the history as a single message in the few-shot examples to maintain compatibility with the OpenAI standard format"* — keeping prompt envelopes bounded under [[BootstrapFewShot]] / [[BootstrapFewShotWithRandomSearch]] / [[MIPROv2]] demo budgets and preserving the OpenAI `messages=[...]` shape the [[LiteLLM]] layer expects. **Position in the DSPy application stack**: fills the missing **single-agent multi-turn** rung (rung 3 of 6) between [[DSPyPredict|single-call]] and [[CoSTORM|multi-agent collaborative discourse]]; completes the DSPy application-stack ladder from one LM call to a Co-STORM mind-map system. **Inherits the [[2604.27707-agentic-memory-is-a-memo|*"agentic memory is lookup, not memory"*]] critique** — the flat append-only `messages` list is the most literal lookup-memory instance in [[DSPy]], with the Ω(k²) generalization gap and the persistent prompt-injection vulnerability the agentic-memory-is-a-memo paper proves. **Mints 2 concept pages** ([[DSPyHistory]], [[ConversationHistory]]); updates [[DSPy]] entity in place with a new "Conversation history" section + tracked source + 3 connection entries. No contradictions with prior wiki content — the tutorial complements every prior DSPy page rather than contradicting any.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[2507.03152-medval|MedVAL: Toward Expert-Level Medical Text Validation with Language Models (Aali, Bikia, Varma, ... Chaudhari, arXiv 2026)]] ingested.** **First wiki-corpus paper to demonstrate [[LLMAsAJudge|LLM-as-a-judge]] reaching expert-level reliability on clinical text** — and the first to extend the [[DSPy]] local PEFT pipeline with **[[QLoRA]]** (the paper contributes the integration back via a GitHub PR to `stanfordnlp/dspy`). The 28-author [[stanforduniversity|Stanford]]-only paper, led by [[AsadAali|Aali]] (first / corresponding) + [[AkshayChaudhari|Chaudhari]] (senior) + [[EmilyAlsentzer|Alsentzer]] (co-senior) out of the [[StanfordMIMI]] lab, introduces **[[MedVAL]]** — a three-stage self-supervised distillation method for training LM validators of medical text **without physician labels and without reference outputs**. Stage 1: synthetic data generation with controlled perturbation $\delta \in \{0, 0.33, 0.67, 1.0\}$ mapped to a physician-defined 4-class **[[RiskLevelTaxonomy|risk taxonomy]]** (Level 1 No Risk → Level 4 High Risk, encoding the clinical action gate: review-not-required / optional / required / rewrite-required). Stage 2: filter via the **[[GeneratorValidatorConsistency|$\mathcal{M}_\mathrm{MedVAL} = 1 - \mathcal{M}_\mathrm{consistency}/6$]]** metric combining absolute + relative agreement between generator-intended $\delta$ and validator-predicted $\hat\delta$. Stage 3: [[BootstrapFinetune|`dspy.BootstrapFinetune`]] + QLoRA on a single A6000 (open students < 8B; OpenAI managed PEFT for GPT-4o / GPT-4o Mini). Releases three artifacts: **[[MedVALBench]]** (840 physician-annotated outputs across 6 tasks; 12-physician panel: 7 internal-medicine MDs + 5 radiologists + 3 bilingual residents; Krippendorff's $\alpha = 0.848$ overall), **[[MedVAL4B|MedVAL-4B]]** (Qwen3-4B base, F1 = 0.527 — the best open-source distilled validator), and the 4-class taxonomy itself. **Headline benchmarks** ([[F1Score|macro-F1]]): average baseline **36.7% → 51.0%** across 10 LMs ($p < 0.001$, [[McNemarTest|McNemar]] + Bonferroni); safe/unsafe binary F1 **66.2% → 82.8%** (binary inter-physician $\alpha = 0.754$, MedVAL ensembles reach F1 = 0.864 — at the upper end of human consistency); **GPT-4o MedVAL F1 = 0.587** (best overall, **+8%** over zero-shot 0.545), and **statistically non-inferior to a single human expert** on 90/840 multi-annotated test cases via [[NonInferiorityTest|paired-bootstrap]] (B = 10,000, $\mathrm{LCB}_{95\%}(\Delta) > -0.05$ at $p < 0.001$). **[[MedVAL4B|MedVAL-Qwen3-4B]] (0.527) beats baselines of [[MedGemma|MedGemma-27B]] (0.482), Llama-3.3-70B (0.480), GPT-4o Mini (0.474), [[gemini|Gemini 2.0 Flash]] (0.515)** — a 4B open student outscales 7× larger domain-pretrained and proprietary baselines. **Generalization**: +65% F1 on in-distribution tasks (medication2answer, query2question, report2impression), **+84% on out-of-distribution** (impression2simplified, bhc2spanish, dialogue2note); +5.3% accuracy on the external [[MEDEC]] benchmark from [[MedHELM]]. **Direct comparison with reference-based metrics** on `report2impression` ($n = 190$): **MedVAL Pearson $r$ with physician risk grades = 0.825 (GPT-4o) / 0.833 (Qwen3-4B)**; [[AlignScore]] = **0.678**; [[BERTScore]] = **0.141**; [[ROUGE]]-L = **0.259** — *"reference-based similarity is not a faithful proxy for reference-free, input-only clinical risk grading."* This is the **direct empirical contradiction to the [[2025-bionlp-archehr-qa-neural|ArchEHR-QA (Neural)]] reward composite** (mean of BLEU + ROUGE + SARI + BERTScore + AlignScore + MEDCON) as a faithful physician-judgment proxy in clinical generation: the composite is **operationally robust** against single-metric failure, but its highest-correlated component (AlignScore, $r = 0.678$) is decisively outperformed by reference-free MedVAL ($r = 0.825$). Both papers can be right under their own framing (Neural optimizes against references; MedVAL grades reference-free) — the open question is whether a future clinical-NLP system should optimize against the Neural composite *or* the MedVAL signal. **Filter > teacher strength**: ablation shows filtering at $\tau = 0.9$ retains **57% of synthetic data** but **outperforms unfiltered 100%** on every tested student; self-distillation (model teaches itself) + MedVAL filtering on **3%** of training data beats unfiltered self-distillation on **100%** for Llama-3.2-3B (12.8% → 22.1% vs 12.8% → 13.6%) — *the filter, not the teacher, drives the gain*. **Most common error categories** (across 840 outputs): fabricated claim 45.7% / missing claim 14.0% / incorrect recommendation 12.6% — establishing [[Hallucination|fabrication]] as the dominant clinical-LM failure mode. **Funded by [[ARPAH|ARPA-H]] CARE** (Chatbot Accuracy and Reliability Evaluation) + [[MIDRC]] / NIH NIBIB. **Position in the wiki**: the **second clinical-NLP paper** in the corpus (after [[2025-bionlp-archehr-qa-neural|ArchEHR-QA 2025 (Neural)]]) and the **first paper to apply DSPy to clinical safety** — the DSPy ecosystem now spans **three application domains**: compound-AI prompt optimization ([[MIPROv2|MIPRO]] / [[BetterTogether]] / [[GEPA|GEPA]]), safety guardrails ([[dspy-guardrails|DSPy Guardrails]]), and clinical text validation (MedVAL). **Mints 29 concept pages** ([[MedVAL]], [[MedVALBench]], [[MedVAL4B]], [[RiskLevelTaxonomy]], [[MedicalTextValidation]], [[GeneratorValidatorConsistency]], [[QLoRA]], [[MedGemma]], [[LLMAsAJudge]], [[MEDEC]], [[MedHELM]], [[FActScore]], [[GREEN]], [[MedHAL]], [[VeriFact]], [[DocLens]], [[ReXTrust]], [[ReXErr]], [[FineRadScore]], [[NonInferiorityTest]], [[McNemarTest]], [[KrippendorffAlpha]], [[CohensKappa]], [[MedicationQA]], [[MeQSum]], [[Openi]], [[MIMICIV]], [[MIMICIVBHC]], [[ACIBench]]) + **13 entity pages** ([[AsadAali]], [[AkshayChaudhari]], [[EmilyAlsentzer]], [[DaveVanVeen]], [[ChristianBluethgen]], [[SanmiKoyejo]], [[CurtisLangloo]], [[JasonHom]], [[RoxanaDaneshjou]], [[StanfordMIMI]], [[ARPAH]], [[MIDRC]], [[VanVeen2024]]); updates [[DSPy]] / [[stanforduniversity]] / [[BootstrapFinetune]] / [[lora]] / [[qwen]] / [[AlignScore]] / [[BERTScore]] / [[ROUGE]] / [[Hallucination]] / [[F1Score]] / [[knowledgedistillation]] / [[MIMIC]] in place.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[2408.15232-co-storm|Co-STORM (Jiang, Shao, Ma, Semnani & Lam, 2024)]] ingested.** [[YuchengJiang]] / [[YijiaShao]] (co-first; [[StanfordOVAL]]) + [[DekunMa]] ([[YaleUniversity|Yale]]) + [[SinaSemnani]] + [[MonicaLam]] (senior; OVAL) introduce **[[CoSTORM|Collaborative STORM (Co-STORM)]]** — a [[MixedInitiativeDiscourse|mixed-initiative]] [[InformationSeeking|information-seeking]] system that recasts the task from one-question-one-answer [[rag|RAG]] into **observable collaborative discourse** between a user and multiple LM agents. Architecture: $N=3$ [[PerspectiveGuidedExpert|perspective-guided experts]] + a [[ModeratorAgent|moderator]] that reranks **uncited** sources via $\cos(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{t})^\alpha (1-\cos(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{q}))^{1-\alpha}$ to steer toward [[UnknownUnknowns|unknown unknowns]]; a dynamic hierarchical **[[CoSTORMMindMap|mind map]]** $\mathcal{M}=(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{E})$ with `insert` + `reorganize` as shared knowledge substrate; a [[TurnManagement|turn-management]] protocol triggering moderator intervention after $L=2$ consecutive non-question expert turns. Implementation: [[gpt-4o|gpt-4o-2024-05-13]] + [[YouCom|You.com]] over [[DSPy]] — **zero-shot, no [[MIPROv2]]/[[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA]] optimization applied**, an open methodological gap given the wiki's MIPRO/GEPA evidence that multi-stage LM programs benefit from automated prompt optimization. Introduces the **[[WildSeek]] dataset** — 100 (topic, goal) pairs across 24 domains from publicly-deployed STORM logs; the **first dataset in this wiki to capture user *goals***, not just queries. Co-STORM beats [[RAGChatbot|RAG Chatbot]] and STORM+QA on every report-quality dim (Relevance 3.78 / Breadth 3.79 / Depth 3.77 / Novelty 3.05 — Depth/Novelty $p<0.05$; [[InformationDiversity]] 0.602; 2× unique URLs/turn) graded by **[[Prometheus2]]**; human eval ($n=20$): **70% prefer over [[GoogleSearch|Search]], 78% over [[RAGChatbot|RAG]]**. **Ablation key result**: removing the moderator drops scores more than removing multi-experts — *"having just one expert and one moderator can already provide most of the benefits"*; the **moderator-on-uncited-sources is the load-bearing innovation**, not perspective multiplicity. Positions the [[CollaborativeDiscourse]] pedagogical frame ([[Nussbaum2008]]) as a tractable LM-system design target. **First [[YaleUniversity|Yale]]-affiliated paper** in this wiki (via [[DekunMa]]). **Resolves the long-standing [[Shao2024]] dangling reference** invoked by the [[2406.11695-mipro|MIPRO]] / [[2407.10930-better-together|BetterTogether]] / [[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA]] [[DSPy]]-optimizer line — the [[STORM]] paper is now anchored as a concept page rather than an unresolved citation. **Mints 14 concept pages** ([[CoSTORM]], [[STORM]], [[WildSeek]], [[UnknownUnknowns]], [[CollaborativeDiscourse]], [[CoSTORMMindMap]], [[ModeratorAgent]], [[PerspectiveGuidedExpert]], [[UtteranceIntent]], [[TurnManagement]], [[MixedInitiativeDiscourse]], [[InformationDiversity]], [[Prometheus2]], [[RAGChatbot]], [[InformationSeeking]]) + **8 entity pages** ([[YuchengJiang]], [[YijiaShao]], [[DekunMa]], [[SinaSemnani]], [[MonicaLam]], [[YaleUniversity]], [[StanfordOVAL]], [[YouCom]]). Updates [[stanforduniversity]] in place — Stanford's wiki footprint is now two distinct research threads: the NLP / [[ChristopherPotts]] / [[OmarKhattab]] DSPy-optimizer line, and the OVAL / [[MonicaLam]] LM-agent application line. Live tension with [[2402.01817-llm-modulo]]: the [[ModeratorAgent|moderator]] is a *soft critic* in Kambhampati's taxonomy — useful but not sound; Co-STORM does not claim the moderator *reliably* finds unknown unknowns, only that it does so often enough to improve user-facing Novelty / Engagement / Serendipity ratings. The 71% mind-map accuracy figure is consistent with the soft-critic framing.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[2025-bionlp-archehr-qa-neural|Neural at ArchEHR-QA 2025 (Reddy, Majeedi, Gajjala, Xu, Rai & Potlapalli, BioNLP 2025)]] ingested.** First wiki-corpus paper to apply [[MIPROv2|`dspy.MIPROv2`]] in a **peer-reviewed clinical-NLP shared-task placement**. The [[Ours_Neural|Neural team]] from [[universityofchicago|UChicago]] takes **2nd place (overall 51.5)** at the [[ArchEHRQA2025|ArchEHR-QA 2025]] shared task on [[EvidenceGroundedQA|evidence-grounded clinical QA]] over [[MIMIC]]-derived EHR notes by decomposing the task into a two-stage [[LMProgram|LM program]] — Stage 1 sentence-level **essentiality classification** with [[selfconsistency|self-consistency]] $R=5$ majority vote, Stage 2 **answer synthesis** ≤75 words with explicit parenthetical citations — and **optimizing each stage's prompt independently with [[MIPROv2]]** on the 20-case dev split. Backbone [[GPT4_1|GPT-4.1]] at 10k context (temp 0.3 during optimization for low-variance feedback, temp 0.7 for $R=5$ stochastic sampling at Stage 1 inference). The Stage 2 composite reward $\mathcal{R}(a_{\mathrm{gen}}, a^*, E) = \mathbb{1}[\le 75\text{ words}] + \mathbb{1}[\text{format}] + \tfrac{1}{6}\sum_m m$ averages **six surface + semantic metrics** $\{[[bleu|BLEU]], [[ROUGE]], [[SARI]], [[BERTScore]], [[AlignScore]], [[MEDCON]]\}$ — making the optimization signal robust against any single-metric pathology and yielding cross-axis consistency (Neural wins overall by being competitive everywhere; the 1st-place [[DMISLab]] is BLEU/ROUGE/BERTScore-heavy but SARI-weak at 36.7 vs. Neural's 73.1). **Beats few-shot baseline by 10 points, zero-shot by 20** — empirical case that data-driven prompt optimization on a frontier LM is a **cost-effective alternative to fine-tuning** when supervised clinical data is scarce. Continues the [[PromptOptimization|prompt-optimization]] arc ([[OPRO]] → [[MIPROv2|MIPRO]] → [[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA]]) and supplies its first published clinical-NLP application. Live tension with [[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA]]: GEPA outperforms MIPROv2 by >10% on six non-clinical benchmarks with **9.2× shorter prompts** — open question whether GEPA's [[ReflectivePromptMutation|reflective mutation]] + [[FeedbackFunction|feedback function]] $\mu_f$ would beat this 51.5 on ArchEHR-QA if reproduced. **Mints 10 concept pages** ([[ArchEHRQA2025]], [[MIMIC]], [[emrQA]], [[BERTScore]], [[AlignScore]], [[MEDCON]], [[ROUGE]], [[SARI]], [[EvidenceGroundedQA]], [[GPT4_1]]) + **11 entity pages** ([[SaiPrasannaTejaReddy]], [[Ours_Neural]], [[SarveshSoni]], [[DinaDemnerFushman]], [[BioNLP2025]], [[DMISLab]], [[LAILab]], [[LAMAR_BioNLP]], [[AnusriPampari]], [[TianyiZhang]], [[WenwaiYim]]); updates [[universityofchicago]] / [[MIPROv2]] / [[selfconsistency]] / [[bleu]] / [[PromptOptimization]] in place. Also **broadens [[universityofchicago|UChicago]]'s wiki footprint** from one paper ([[2604.25067-frontier-coding-agents-c4|the RSI early-warning benchmark]]) to two — different groups in different sub-disciplines.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[dspy-guardrails|DSPy Guardrails (Yu & He 2024)]] ingested.** [[BoxiYu]] & [[PinjiaHe]] ([[CUHKShenzhen|CUHK-Shenzhen]]) propose **[[DSPyGuardrails]]**: build LLM safety guardrails as **auto-optimized [[DSPy]] programs** (a `guardrails` [[DSPySignatures|signature]] input field over [[ChainOfThought|`dspy.ChainOfThought`]], compiled with [[BootstrapFewShot]]) rather than hand-written rules. On [[CodeAttack]] vs gpt-3.5-turbo-instruct, **[[AttackSuccessRate|ASR]] drops 75% → 5%** — with [[BootstrapFewShot]]; **30% without the optimizer**, so the optimizer is load-bearing, not the DSPy framing alone. Positions itself against three **manual-configuration baselines** — [[LlamaGuard]] ([[InanEtAl2023|Inan et al. 2023]], [[meta|Meta]] trained classifier + safety taxonomy), [[NeMoGuardrails]] ([[RebedeaEtAl2023|Rebedea et al. 2023]], [[Nvidia]] [[Colang]] dialog flows), and [[GuardrailsAI]] ([[Rajpal2023|Rajpal 2023]] output validators) — arguing the human-rule layer cannot keep up with evolving [[Jailbreak|jailbreaks]] (*"jailbreak techniques are constantly evolving every day"*). **Earliest DSPy-as-safety-tool paper in this wiki**; predates [[2406.11695-mipro|MIPRO]] / [[2407.10930-better-together|BetterTogether]] / [[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA]] as stronger successor optimizers for the same `dataset + metric + optimizer` recipe applied to the narrow problem of jailbreak defense. **Narrow eval** (1 attack family / [[CodeAttack]], 1 defender LM / gpt-3.5-turbo-instruct, 20 prompts) and **no head-to-head against the three baselines it critiques** — the empirical claim is single-cell, generalization to attacks crafted after the bootstrap dataset is untested. [[PinjiaHe]] is also a co-author of [[YuanEtAl2023|the GPT-4 cipher-attack paper]] (2023) — sits on both the attack and defense sides of the jailbreak literature.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[2406.11695-mipro|MIPRO (Opsahl-Ong, Ryan, Purtell, Broman, Potts, Zaharia & Khattab, EMNLP 2024)]] ingested.** Closes the **canonical-source gap** on [[MIPROv2|MIPRO]] — the wiki previously had only the DSPy-docs-anchored version of the optimizer, now promoted to paper-anchored. The MIPRO paper (i) formalizes [[PromptOptimization|prompt optimization]] for multi-stage [[LMProgram|LM programs]] $\Phi$ as $\Phi^* = \arg\max_{\mathbf{V}\mapsto S}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}|}\sum\mu(\Phi_{\mathbf{V}\mapsto S}(x), x')$ over latent per-module instruction + demonstration variables, (ii) identifies the **proposal challenge** (intractable string space) and the **[[CreditAssignment|credit-assignment challenge]]** (no per-module labels), (iii) instantiates a 3×3 strategy matrix (bootstrap-demos / grounding / learning-to-propose × greedy / [[TreeStructuredParzenEstimator|TPE]]-surrogate / history-based) as the optimizer family [[BootstrapRandomSearch]] / Bayesian Bootstrap / [[ModuleLevelOPRO]] / 0-Shot MIPRO / 0-Shot MIPRO++ / **MIPRO** / Program-Level OPRO / CA-OPRO, and (iv) releases the **[[DSPyOptimizerBenchmark]]** — 7 tasks ([[hotpotqa|HotPotQA]] / [[HotPotQAConditional]] / [[Iris]] / [[IrisTypo]] / [[HeartDisease]] / [[ScoNe]] / [[HoVer]]) on which MIPRO wins 5/7 by up to **13% absolute** ([[Llama3_8BInstruct|Llama-3-8B]] task LM + GPT-3.5 proposer). Five practitioner lessons: (L1) bootstrapped demos are essential, (L2) joint optimization wins overall, (L3) **instruction optimization is most important for conditional-rule tasks** — including correcting the misspelled "versicolour" seed prompt on Iris-Typo, (L4) grounding helps overall but best proposal strategy varies by task (motivates MIPRO++), (L5) optimizer rankings vary with budget. This is the **direct ancestor** of [[2407.10930-better-together|BetterTogether (2024)]] (uses [[BootstrapFewShotWithRandomSearch|BFRS]] as its prompt-axis primitive — MIPRO was not yet released when BT trained) and [[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA (ICLR 2026 Oral)]] (which explicitly beats MIPRO by >10% across six benchmarks). The MIPRO paper itself **predicts the GEPA gap**: §3.2 notes surrogate-based optimization *"only allows for optimization over a fixed set of proposals; learnings from past evaluations cannot be used to improve the proposals themselves"* — the exact gap GEPA closes with reflective prompt mutation. The Stanford-side DSPy-optimizer line of papers now spans three years (**MIPRO 2024 → BetterTogether 2024 → GEPA 2026**) with consistent authorship ([[KristaOpsahlOng]], [[MichaelJRyan]], [[ChristopherPotts]], [[OmarKhattab]], [[DilaraSoylu]] across various subsets). The **prompt-vs-demonstration prior is model-generation-dependent**: MIPRO finds demos dominate on most tasks (2024 LMs); GEPA finds instructions decisively beat exemplars (2026 LMs) — both can be true with respect to different LM generations.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[2407.10930-better-together|BetterTogether (Soylu, Potts & Khattab 2024)]] ingested.** Resolves the year-prior predecessor to [[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA]] in the [[DSPy]]-adjacent optimizer line. [[DilaraSoylu|Soylu]] / [[ChristopherPotts]] / [[OmarKhattab]] ([[stanforduniversity|Stanford]], arXiv 2407.10930v2) introduce **[[BetterTogether]]** — the first published bi-axial optimizer for [[CompoundAISystem|compound AI systems]]: a three-step schedule `Π → Θ → Π` that alternates [[BootstrapFewShotWithRandomSearch|BFRS]] prompt optimization with [[BootstrapFinetune|BFT]] + [[lora|LoRA]] fine-tuning over the *same* compound program. Three tasks ([[hotpotqa|HotPotQA]] 3-module CoT+retrieval pipeline / [[GSM8K]] 1-module CoT / [[Iris]] 1-module CoT classification) × three open instruction-tuned LMs ([[Mistral7BInstructV02]] / [[Llama2_7BChat]] / [[Llama3_8BInstruct]]) = 9 cells; **BetterTogether wins 7 of 9**, with gains of **5–78% on HotPotQA, 2.5–10% on GSM8K, 3.5–88% on Iris** over the better single-axis baseline. Two-direction motivation: (i) prompt-opt *before* fine-tune produces more metric-passing bootstrap traces and therefore a higher-quality SFT dataset — the Iris-Llama-2 cell shows the hard-stop (vanilla 0% → no SFT traces → "—" cells for Θ-first strategies; Π-first lifts to 65.3%); (ii) prompt-opt *after* fine-tune adjusts prompts to the shifted prior of the fine-tuned LM. **Critically: this paper introduces the $\Phi = (M, C, \mathcal{X}, \mathcal{Y})$ compound-AI-system formalism with learnable parameters $\langle \Pi, \Theta \rangle$ that [[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA]] adopts unchanged** — BetterTogether updates *both* $\Pi$ and $\Theta$; GEPA shows in the sample-constrained frontier-LM regime that updating *only* $\Pi$ via reflective mutation can outperform RL on $\Theta$. **Resolves the long-standing dangling `[[soylu2024fine]]` reference** in [[CompoundAISystem]]. Released as `dspy.BetterTogether` — **the only Meta-Optimizer family entry** in [[DSPyOptimizers|the DSPy optimizer catalog]]. **75 A100-GPU-hours** total to produce Table 1.

> **Update 2026-05-22 — [[2507.19457-gepa|GEPA]] (ICLR 2026 Oral) ingested.** Agrawal, Tan, Soylu, Ziems et al. ([[UCBerkeley]] + [[stanforduniversity|Stanford]] + [[BespokeLabsAI]] + [[NotreDame]] + [[Databricks]] + [[MITAcademic|MIT]]; senior authors [[MateiZaharia]] / [[OmarKhattab]] / [[DanKlein]] / [[IonStoica]] / [[AlexandrosDimakis]] / [[ChristopherPotts]]) introduce **GEPA (Genetic-Pareto)**: a [[ReflectivePromptMutation|reflective prompt mutation]] + [[ParetoBasedCandidateSelection|Pareto-based candidate selection]] (+ optional [[SystemAwareMerge|System Aware Merge]] crossover) optimizer for [[CompoundAISystem|compound AI systems]]. **Outperforms [[grpo|GRPO]] RL by 6% avg / 20% peak with up to 35× fewer rollouts**; outperforms [[MIPROv2]] by >10% (+12% on [[AIME2025|AIME-2025]] for GPT-4.1 Mini). Six benchmarks ([[hotpotqa|HotpotQA]], [[IFBench]], [[HoVer]], [[PUPA]], [[AIME2025]], [[LiveBenchMath]]) × two models (Qwen3 8B, GPT-4.1 Mini). Adds the **[[FeedbackFunction|feedback function]] $\mu_f$** that extends scalar reward $\mu$ with natural-language [[EvaluationTrace|evaluation-trace]] text — the load-bearing non-metric supervision channel that lets a single rollout teach far more than its scalar reward alone could. Also demonstrates GEPA as **[[InferenceTimeSearch|inference-time search]]** for code generation (30.52% mean vector utilization on AMD XDNA2 NPU kernels via [[NPUEval]]; >20% $fast_1$ on [[KernelBench]] V100 CUDA) and as **[[AdversarialPromptSearch|adversarial prompt search]]** (drops GPT-5 Mini AIME-2025 pass@1 from 76% → 10% via a universal trivia-injection + strict-format-literal-conflict adversarial prefix). **Prompts up to 9.2× shorter than [[MIPROv2]]'s** — declarative natural-language instructions now beat few-shot exemplars, a reversal of the Wan et al. 2024 "quasi-exemplars dominate" finding. **Cross-model generalization**: prompts optimized on Qwen3-8B and evaluated unmodified on GPT-4.1 Mini still beat baselines that optimized directly on GPT-4.1 Mini. This is the first paper in this wiki's 2026 cluster to demonstrate **prompt-space optimization decisively outperforming weight-space RL** in the sample-constrained regime, and it formalizes the **[[CompoundAISystem|compound AI system]]** target $\Phi = (M, C, \mathcal{X}, \mathcal{Y})$ with learnable parameters $\langle \Pi, \Theta \rangle$ that gives a clean optimizer-comparison vocabulary (GRPO updates $\Theta$, GEPA updates $\Pi$). Continues the [[DSPy]]-adjacent optimizer arc ([[MIPROv2]] → GEPA) and **promotes the existing [[GEPA]] concept page** from DSPy-docs-only to paper-anchored.

> **Update 2026-05-18 — [[DiveIntoSystems]] textbook fully ingested (167 chapters / 167 leaves).** Matthews / Newhall / Webb's open-textbook *Dive into Systems* is now the wiki's most thorough single-source ingest: a complete C-to-multicore systems traversal spanning Ch 0 (intro), Ch 1–2 ([[CLanguage|C]] programming + pointers + dynamic memory + structs), Ch 3 ([[GDB]] + [[Valgrind]] debugging), Ch 4 ([[BinaryRepresentation|binary representation]] + [[TwosComplement]] + [[FloatingPoint]] + [[Endianness]]), Ch 5 ([[VonNeumannArchitecture|computer architecture]] + [[LogicGate|gates]] + [[Pipelining]] + [[MulticoreProcessor|multicore]] + [[MooresLaw]]), Ch 6–10 (assembly: [[X86_64]] / [[IA32]] / [[ARM64]] across data-movement / arithmetic / control flow / functions / [[BufferOverflow|buffer overflow]]), Ch 11–12 ([[MemoryHierarchy]] + [[Caching]] + [[CodeOptimization|code optimization]] + [[Profiling]]), Ch 13 ([[OperatingSystem]] + [[Process|processes]] + [[VirtualMemory]] + [[InterprocessCommunication|IPC]]: [[Signal|signals]] / [[MessagePassing|message passing]] / [[SharedMemory|shared memory]]), Ch 14 ([[Pthreads]] + [[Synchronization]] + [[Mutex|mutexes]] / [[Semaphore|semaphores]] / [[Barrier|barriers]] / [[ConditionVariable|condition variables]] + [[CacheCoherency]] + [[FalseSharing]] + [[ThreadSafety]] + [[OpenMP]]), Ch 15 ([[CUDA]] / [[GPU]] + [[MPI]] / [[Cluster|distributed memory]] + [[ExascaleComputing]]), Appendix 1 / Ch 16 ([[JavaVsC|Java-vs-C cross-walk]] for Java programmers), and Appendix 2 / Ch 17 (*Using Unix* — full 16-subchapter [[UnixShell|shell]] / [[UnixFileSystem|filesystem]] / [[VimEditor|editors]] / [[Make|build tools]] / [[Grep|search]] / [[FilePermissions|permissions]] / [[Tar|archives]] / [[JobControl|job control]] / [[TimeCommand|timing]] / [[BashHistory|history]] / [[IORedirection|I/O redirection]] / [[ShellPipe|pipes]] / [[DotFile|dotfiles]] / [[ShellProgramming|shell programming]] / [[ProcFS|/proc-based system info]] survey). DIS fills the **systems-programming and architecture layer** the wiki previously approximated only through fragments — it now sits underneath every other corpus (the [[CPython]] interpreter the [[madewithml]] course runs on, the [[PyTorch]] kernels the [[d2l-preface|*Dive into Deep Learning*]] book trains, the [[GPU|GPUs]] the LLM-research arxiv papers run on, the [[ARMCortexM|Cortex-M]] microcontrollers [[TheEmbeddedRustBook|*The Embedded Rust Book*]] targets) as the canonical bottom-of-stack textbook. Twin to [[TheEmbeddedRustBook]] in coverage breadth (44 vs 167 chapters) but DIS covers the **general-purpose CPU + OS** axis where the Rust book covered the **embedded MCU + bare-metal** axis. Both together with the rest of the [[CLanguage|C]] / [[RustLanguage|Rust]] / [[Pthreads]] / [[CUDA]] / [[MPI]] / [[Unix]] vocabulary now constitute the wiki's **systems block**.


This wiki holds **eleven** distinct corpora as of **2026-05-17**: a structured math knowledge base from algebrica.org (80 pages), a fast-moving cluster of 2026 LLM/AI research papers (18+ arxiv PDFs) anchored by the foundational sequence-modeling and pretraining papers [[1409.3215-seq2seq]] (2014), [[1706.03762-attention-is-all-you-need]] (2017) and [[1810.04805-bert]] (2018), Christoph Molnar's *Interpretable Machine Learning* book (35 chapters), [[WesMcKinney]]'s *Python for Data Analysis 3E* (16 chapters — the working-knowledge layer underneath every applied ML / data project), [[GokuMohandas]]'s *[[MadeWithML]]* course (39 lessons — end-to-end MLOps + foundations curriculum that bridges textbook ML to production systems), [[KevinMurphy]]'s *Probabilistic Machine Learning: An Introduction* ([[pml1-murphy]], 860 pages — the canonical foundations-and-theory textbook the other corpora tacitly assume), [[GarethJames|James]], [[DanielaWitten|Witten]], [[TrevorHastie|Hastie]] & [[RobertTibshirani|Tibshirani]]'s [[islr-seventh-printing|*An Introduction to Statistical Learning*]] (10 chapters / R labs — the classical-statistical-learning canon that Murphy's probabilistic framing and Mohandas's hands-on track tacitly assume), [[MarcDeisenroth|Deisenroth]], [[AAldoFaisal|Faisal]] & [[ChengSoonOng|Ong]]'s [[mml-book|*Mathematics for Machine Learning*]] (417 pages / 12 chapters — the **prerequisites** layer that closes the high-school-math-to-Murphy/Bishop gap by surfacing the linear algebra, vector calculus, probability, and optimization machinery the other textbook corpora assume), [[AstonZhang|Zhang]], [[ZacharyLipton|Lipton]], [[MuLi|Li]] & [[AlexanderSmola|Smola]]'s [[d2l-preface|*Dive into Deep Learning*]] (26 chapters complete — the **applied-deep-learning textbook** with runnable [[PyTorch]] / [[JAX]] notebooks), and — **complete as of 2026-05-16** — *[[TheEmbeddedRustBook|The Embedded Rust Book]]* (**all 44/44 chapters ingested**, from [[rust-embedded-book-intro-index|Introduction]] through [[rust-embedded-book-appendix-glossary|Appendix A: Glossary]] — the wiki's first **systems-programming / [[EmbeddedSystems|embedded-systems]]** corpus, scoping [[RustLanguage|Rust]] for [[BareMetalProgramming|bare-metal]] [[Microcontroller|microcontroller]] development on [[ARMCortexM|ARM Cortex-M]] via the [[STM32F3DISCOVERY]] dev board; covers the full pipeline — environment install / toolchain / [[QEMU]] + real-hardware [[OnChipDebugging|debugging]] / [[NoStd|`#![no_std]`]] / [[MemoryMappedIO|memory-mapped]] [[Peripheral|peripherals]] / [[Singleton|singletons]] / the four-layer crate stack ([[MicroArchitectureCrate|µarch crate]] → [[PeripheralAccessCrate|PAC]] → [[HALCrate|HAL]] → [[BoardCrate|BSP]]) / [[TypeStateProgramming|type-state]] [[StaticGuarantee|static guarantees]] / [[ZeroCostAbstraction|zero-cost abstractions]] / [[Portability|portability]] via [[EmbeddedHalCrate|`embedded-hal`]] / [[Send|`Send`]]+[[Sync|`Sync`]]+[[Mutex|mutex]]-based [[Concurrency|concurrency]] / [[AllocCrate|`alloc`]]-vs-[[HeaplessCrate|`heapless`]] collections / [[rust-embedded-book-design-patterns-hal-index|HAL design patterns]] / [[Bindgen|C↔Rust FFI]] / [[OptLevel|opt-level]] tuning / [[LibmCrate|`libm`]] math). They exemplify eleven complementary uses for the wiki: a reference graph for stable foundational knowledge, a research log, a textbook-grade canonical reference for an ML interpretation domain, a programming-tools tutorial corpus, an applied MLOps curriculum that connects the theoretical and tooling layers, a probabilistic-ML foundations textbook layer, a classical-ML survey layer, the mathematical-prerequisites textbook layer that **every** other textbook corpus tacitly assumes the reader already has, a deep-learning-centric pedagogical textbook with executable code, an **embedded-systems / systems-programming** textbook corpus (Rust on Cortex-M) that takes the wiki outside its ML-only origin, and — newly added **2026-05-17** — a *second* embedded-systems corpus, [[JamesFiore]]'s [[embedded-controllers-fiore|*Embedded Controllers Using C and Arduino*]] ([[OER]], 166 pages, CC BY-NC-SA), which covers the same domain from the *opposite* point in the embedded-language tradeoff space: [[CLanguage|C]] + Arduino library + 8-bit [[AVR]] ([[ATmega328P]]), with a pedagogy of *"read the library source, then bypass it with direct [[MemoryMappedIO|register]] writes when speed or interrupt-safety matters."* Where [[TheEmbeddedRustBook|the Rust book]] argues for [[TypeStateProgramming|typestate]] [[StaticGuarantee|static safety]] and [[ZeroCostAbstraction|zero-cost abstractions]] as the correct embedded-API design, Fiore argues that the hardware comes first and the abstraction is a convenience — same physical [[GPIO]] / [[ADC]] / [[TimerCounter|timer]] / [[Interrupt|interrupt]] blocks underneath, very different programmer experience on top. The two corpora are deliberately complementary, not in conflict; together they cover the bulk of the practical MCU domain (32-bit Cortex-M at the production end, 8-bit AVR at the pedagogy end).

## I. Mathematics corpus (80 pages, algebrica.org)

The math sources span eleven domains, organized roughly from foundations upward:

| Domain | Sources | Anchors |
|---|---|---|
| Sets & numbers | 12 | [[sets]], [[types-of-numbers]], [[natural-numbers]], [[integers]], [[real-numbers]], [[supremum-and-infimum]], [[intervals]], [[absolute-value]], [[modulo-operator]], [[factorial]], [[binomial-coefficient]], [[properties-of-real-numbers]] |
| Algebraic structures | 5 | [[groups]], [[rings]], [[fields]], [[vector-spaces]], [[modules]] |
| Powers / radicals / logs | 3 | [[powers]], [[radicals]], [[logarithms]] |
| Polynomials | 13 | [[polynomials]], [[monomials]], [[binomials]], [[trinomials]], [[adding-and-subtracting-polynomials]], [[polynomial-division]], [[synthetic-division-method]], [[partial-fraction-decomposition]], [[notable-products]], [[completing-the-square]], [[factoring-polynomials-ac-method]], [[binomial-theorem]], [[roots-of-a-polynomial]], [[vieta-formulas]] |
| Equations | 8 | [[equations]], [[linear-equations]], [[linear-equations-with-parameters]], [[quadratic-equations]], [[incomplete-quadratic-equations]], [[factoring-quadratic-equations]], [[quadratic-formula]], [[polynomial-equations]] |
| Linear systems | 1 | [[rouche-capelli-theorem]] |
| Vectors & matrices | 8 | [[vectors]], [[matrices]], [[determinant-of-a-square-matrix]], [[rank-of-a-matrix]], [[inverse-matrix]], [[linear-combinations]], [[eigenvalues-and-eigenvectors]], [[matrix-diagonalization]] |
| Trigonometry | 15 | [[unit-circle]], [[sine-and-cosine]], [[tangent-and-cotangent]], [[secant-and-cosecant]], [[arcsine-and-arccosine]], [[arctangent-and-arccotangent]], [[hyperbolic-sine-and-cosine]], [[hyperbolic-tangent-and-cotangent]], [[trigonometric-identities]], [[pythagorean-identity]], [[pythagorean-theorem]], [[reduction-formulas-and-reference-angles]], [[right-triangle-trigonometry]], [[the-law-of-sines]], [[the-law-of-cosines]] |
| Complex numbers | 7 | [[complex-numbers]], [[operations-with-complex-numbers]], [[complex-numbers-in-trigonometric-form]], [[complex-numbers-in-exponential-form]], [[de-moivre-theorem]], [[roots-of-unity]], [[fundamental-inequalities-for-complex-numbers]] |
| Limits | 1 | [[remarkable-limits]] |
| Integrals | 6 | [[indefinite-integrals]], [[definite-integrals]], [[integration-by-parts]], [[integration-by-substitution]], [[finding-areas-by-integration]], [[riemann-integrability-criteria]] |

**Spine of the graph.** Number systems extend in nested layers ([[types-of-numbers]] → [[natural-numbers]] → [[integers]] → [[real-numbers]] → [[complex-numbers]]) and feed every other branch. Algebraic structures ([[groups]] → [[rings]] → [[fields]]) generalize the operations on those number systems and ground the linear algebra block. Polynomials sit at the junction: as ring elements (algebraic structure side) and as solution objects for [[equations]] / [[polynomial-equations]] / [[roots-of-a-polynomial]]. Trigonometry connects geometry with [[complex-numbers-in-exponential-form]] and the [[unit-circle]] feeds back into [[eigenvalues-and-eigenvectors]] and rotation matrices. Calculus appears via [[remarkable-limits]] and the integral suite ([[definite-integrals]] / [[indefinite-integrals]] / [[integration-by-parts]] / [[integration-by-substitution]]).

**Forward references.** Source pages reference ~150 concepts that have no source in `raw/` — e.g. [[functions]], [[continuousfunctions]], [[derivatives]], [[exponentialfunction]], [[lines]], [[limits]], [[principleofmathematicalinduction]]. These are recorded as **stub concept pages** under `wiki/concepts/`. Future ingests should target these high-incoming-link stubs first.

## II. AI / LLM research corpus (25 papers; 4 foundational + 1 scaling + 1 systems + 1 frontier-template + 2 position + 16 from 2026)

The 2026 cluster orbits a shared question: *given strong base LLMs, where should the next axis of capability come from — better RL signals, better orchestration, better memory, better tooling, or better security boundaries?* That question presupposes both a base architecture — the **[[Transformer]]**, introduced in [[1706.03762-attention-is-all-you-need]] (Vaswani et al., 2017) — and a training recipe: **unsupervised pretraining followed by supervised fine-tuning**, established for NLP by [[1810.04805-bert]] (Devlin et al., 2018) and ablated end-to-end at scale by **[[t5]]** in [[1910.10683-t5]] (Raffel et al., 2020). The Transformer paper in turn inherits the **encoder-decoder seq2seq framing** of [[1409.3215-seq2seq]] (Sutskever, Vinyals & Le, 2014) — the first pure neural system to beat a phrase-based SMT baseline at scale, and the moment recurrent encoder-decoders displaced classical SMT. T5 closes that loop by demonstrating empirically (vs decoder-only and prefix-LM at matched compute) that the *encoder-decoder* structure of the original Transformer was the right call all along, and by introducing the [[c4]] corpus and the [[texttotextframework]] unifying interface that prompt-based LLMs would later generalize. The progression `SMT → recurrent seq2seq → attention-only Transformer → BERT pretrain-then-finetune → T5 unified text-to-text + C4` plays out across WMT translation and GLUE/SQuAD benchmarks; all four foundational papers now anchor this corpus, and their core ideas — [[SeqToSeq]], [[LSTM]], [[BeamSearch]], [[EncoderDecoder]], [[SelfAttention]], [[MultiHeadAttention]], [[ScaledDotProductAttention]], [[PositionalEncoding]], [[BERT]], [[MaskedLanguageModel]], [[NextSentencePrediction]], [[WordPiece]], [[SpanCorruption]], [[c4]], [[texttotextframework]], [[SentencePiece]] — appear (often unstated) inside every later result.

A **scaling** layer sits between the architecture papers and everything later: [[2001.08361-scaling-laws]] (Kaplan, McCandlish et al., OpenAI + [[JohnsHopkinsUniversity]], 2020) supplies the empirical **[[PowerLaw]] argument** that pretraining loss falls as a clean power law in non-embedding parameter count $N$, dataset size $D$, and compute $C$ over 7+ orders of magnitude. The paper's prescription — *very large models, modest data, stop short of convergence* — is the [[ComputeEfficientTraining]] regime ($N \propto C^{0.73}$, $S \propto C^{0.03}$) that justifies the GPT-3-and-beyond scale-up. Every later paper in this corpus implicitly assumes a base model trained inside this envelope; [[2312.11805-gemini]]'s three-size family, the agentic-model training budgets in [[2604.21590-agenticqwen]], and the pretraining-stage interventions of [[2601.21343-self-improving-pretraining]] all take [[ScalingLaws]] as background. Notably, the paper's same data-vs-params exponent is *not* what later "Chinchilla" work would land on — a known live tension between this 2020 result and subsequent revisions.

A **systems** layer sits below the scaling argument and above the 2026 application papers: [[2205.14135-flashattention]] (Dao et al., 2022) makes the standard attention operation **IO-aware** — a single fused CUDA kernel using tiling and recomputation that runs many times faster than the naive implementation without changing what the model computes. [[FlashAttention]] is now the default attention kernel behind essentially every modern Transformer; the long-context behavior assumed by the 2026 papers' agentic harnesses, productivity simulations, and long-horizon RL training (see [[2604.28181-synthetic-computers-at-scale]], [[2605.02572-long-horizon-llm-training]]) is what FlashAttention's [[IOComplexity]] reduction made affordable. It is the load-bearing exception to the rule that subsequent results "just inherit" the 2017 Transformer: the kernel is genuinely different even though the math is the same.

[[2312.11805-gemini]] (Gemini Team, [[GoogleDeepMind]], 2023) sits a layer above: it doesn't define new architectural primitives but instead establishes the **frontier-multimodal-deployment template** that the 2026 papers tacitly assume — a three-size model family (Ultra / Pro / Nano), [[NativeMultimodality|native multimodality]] across text/image/audio/video, an [[RLHF]] data flywheel, structured responsible-deployment review, and a [[DangerousCapabilities]] evaluation suite. Where the 2017 paper supplies the *substrate*, the 2023 Gemini report supplies the *production target*: when subsequent work on agentic harnesses, memory, or skill verification mentions a "frontier base model", they mean a Gemini- or Claude- or GPT-4-class system trained along these lines.

A **position-paper** layer sits orthogonal to the architecture/scaling/systems stack and supplies the field's most explicit *capability-claim audit*. Two position papers anchor this layer. First, [[2402.01817-llm-modulo]] (Kambhampati et al., [[ArizonaStateUniversity]], ICML 2024) argues that autoregressive LLMs — being constant-time-per-token approximate retrievers, i.e. a pseudo-[[System1And2|System 1]] — **cannot plan or self-verify autonomously**.
