[[pydata-advanced-numpy]] gives the [[NDArray]] internals (strides, dtype, broadcasting) that the kernel-level papers in Corpus II ([[2205.14135-flashattention]], [[2605.12357-delta-mem]]) tacitly assume the reader understands.

**Why this corpus matters now.** It is the *only* corpus in the wiki that describes the *tools* used to do data work, rather than the *theories or findings* of that work. When a paper in Corpus II mentions GroupBy / Patsy / value_counts / ndarray strides / `np.where`, the matching `pydata-*` chapter is the canonical reference.

## V. *Made With ML* course ([[GokuMohandas]] / [[Anyscale]], 39 lessons)

[[GokuMohandas]]'s [[MadeWithML]] course is ingested lesson-by-lesson under the `madewithml-` slug prefix. Where Corpus IV ([[WesMcKinney|pydata]]) is the *tabular-tooling* layer and Corpus III ([[imlbook-intro|Molnar]]) is the *interpretability vocabulary* layer, this corpus is the **applied-MLOps production layer** — what it takes to take a model from notebook to a versioned, monitored, continually-retrained service. The course's running example is a content-tagging service for Made With ML projects ([[TextClassification]] over scientific abstracts using [[SciBERT]] fine-tuned via [[RayTrain]]).

**Structure.** The 39 lessons split across two tracks:

- **Foundations (15 lessons)** — toolkit + ML + DL primer.
  - *Toolkit:* [[madewithml-foundations-notebooks]] ([[Jupyter]] / [[GoogleColab]]), [[madewithml-foundations-python]] (Python primer), [[madewithml-foundations-numpy]] ([[NumPy]] / [[NDArray]] / [[Broadcasting]]), [[madewithml-foundations-pandas]] ([[pandas]] / [[DataFrame]]), [[madewithml-foundations-pytorch]] ([[PyTorch]] / [[Tensor]] / [[Autograd]]).
  - *Machine learning:* [[madewithml-foundations-linear-regression]] (from-scratch [[NumPy]] then [[PyTorch]]; establishes the forward/loss/backward/update training-loop template), [[madewithml-foundations-logistic-regression]] ([[Softmax]] + [[CrossEntropyLoss]]; the standard final layer of every later NN), [[madewithml-foundations-neural-networks]] ([[MultilayerPerceptron|MLP]] + [[ReLU]] + [[Backpropagation]] + [[Dropout]]), [[madewithml-foundations-data-quality]] (*garbage in, garbage out* demonstration), [[madewithml-foundations-utilities]] (reusable PyTorch training scaffolding — [[Dataset]] / [[DataLoader]] / [[Trainer]] / [[EarlyStopping]]).
  - *Deep learning:* [[madewithml-foundations-convolutional-neural-networks]] (1-D [[CNN]] text classifier with multi-width filters), [[madewithml-foundations-embeddings]] ([[Word2Vec]] / [[FastText]] / [[GloVe]]; frozen-vs-fine-tuned-embedding ablation on the CNN), [[madewithml-foundations-recurrent-neural-networks]] ([[RNN]] → [[LSTM]] / [[GRU]] / [[BidirectionalRNN]]; exposes [[VanishingGradient]] motivating attention), [[madewithml-foundations-attention]] (softmax-weighted pooling over hidden states), [[madewithml-foundations-transformers]] ([[scaleddotproductattention]] + [[multiheadattention]] + [[PositionalEncoding]] from [[1706.03762-attention-is-all-you-need]]; [[HuggingFace]] [[SciBERT]] as feature extractor).

- **MLOps (24 lessons)** — the production lifecycle.
  - *Design:* [[madewithml-mlops-setup]] ([[Anyscale]] cluster, [[Ray]] init), [[madewithml-mlops-product-design]] ([[MLCanvas]]), [[madewithml-mlops-systems-design]] ([[F1Score]], offline/online eval, batch vs online inference).
  - *Data:* [[madewithml-mlops-preparation]] (stratified split), [[madewithml-mlops-exploratory-data-analysis]] (goal-driven EDA), [[madewithml-mlops-preprocessing]] ([[Tokenization]] / [[FeatureScaling]] / [[OneHotEncoding]]), [[madewithml-mlops-distributed-data]] ([[Ray]] Data `map_batches`).
  - *Model:* [[madewithml-mlops-training]] (fine-tune [[SciBERT]] with [[RayTrain]]; benchmarks [[GPT4]]/[[GPT35]] zero/few-shot first), [[madewithml-mlops-experiment-tracking]] ([[MLflow]] wired into [[RayTrain]]), [[madewithml-mlops-tuning]] ([[RayTune]] + [[HyperOpt]] + [[ASHA]]), [[madewithml-mlops-evaluation]] (per-class / confusion-sample / [[ConfidentLearning]] / [[SHAP]] / [[LIME]] / [[BehavioralTesting]] / [[ABTesting]] / [[CanaryDeployment]] / [[ShadowDeployment]]), [[madewithml-mlops-serving]] ([[RayServe]] + [[FastAPI]]).
  - *Developing & utilities:* [[madewithml-mlops-scripting]] (notebooks → workload functions), [[madewithml-mlops-cli]] ([[Typer]]), [[madewithml-mlops-logging]] ([[PythonLogging]] + [[RotatingFileHandler]]), [[madewithml-mlops-documentation]] ([[MkDocs]] + [[Mkdocstrings]]), [[madewithml-mlops-styling]] ([[Black]] + [[isort]] + [[flake8]]), [[madewithml-mlops-pre-commit]].
  - *Testing & reproducibility:* [[madewithml-mlops-testing]] ([[Pytest]] + [[GreatExpectations]] + [[CheckList]]), [[madewithml-mlops-versioning]] (code via [[Git]], data via [[DVC]] / [[GitLFS]] / [[Dolt]] / [[Pachyderm]], models via [[MLflow]]).
  - *Production:* [[madewithml-mlops-jobs-and-services]] ([[AnyscaleJobs]] / [[AnyscaleServices]]), [[madewithml-mlops-cicd]] ([[GitHubActions]] retrain-on-PR), [[madewithml-mlops-monitoring]] ([[KolmogorovSmirnovTest]] / [[ChiSquaredTest]] / [[MaximumMeanDiscrepancy]] for [[DataDrift]] / [[TargetDrift]] / [[ConceptDrift]]), [[madewithml-mlops-data-engineering]] ([[ELT]] via [[Airbyte]] + [[GoogleBigQuery]] + [[dbt]]).

**Connections back to other corpora.**
- The foundations track *recapitulates* much of Corpus IV ([[NumPy]] / [[pandas]] / matplotlib chapters) but pivots earlier to deep learning ([[PyTorch]] / [[CNN]] / [[RNN]] / [[Attention]] / [[transformer]]). Use [[madewithml-foundations-pytorch]] when the question is *how to do tensor ops*; use [[pydata-numpy-basics]] when the question is *how to do tabular work*.
- The transformers lesson connects directly to Corpus II's anchor paper [[1706.03762-attention-is-all-you-need]] — Mohandas's lesson is a *pedagogical reconstruction* of scaled-dot-product + multi-head + positional encoding before reaching for [[HuggingFace]] [[SciBERT]].
- The evaluation lesson invokes [[SHAP]] / [[LIME]] / [[BehavioralTesting]] / [[CheckList]] — the same vocabulary formalized in Corpus III ([[imlbook-shap]] / [[imlbook-lime]]). Use this lesson as the *applied counterpart* to the IML book.
- The MLOps track is the wiki's *only* end-to-end production-systems reference. When a 2026 paper in Corpus II mentions [[CICD]], [[ModelRegistry]], [[ExperimentTracking]], [[ModelMonitoring]], or [[FeatureStore]], the matching `madewithml-mlops-*` lesson is the canonical hands-on reference.

**Why this corpus matters now.** Corpus II describes *what frontier LLMs are doing*; Corpus III describes *how to interpret them*; Corpus IV describes *the tools used to do data work*. This corpus describes *what it takes to put any of that into production* — versioning, testing, monitoring, retraining, CI/CD. It is also the wiki's primary reference for the [[Ray]] / [[Anyscale]] stack (distributed training, tuning, and serving) which several Corpus II papers ([[2604.21590-agenticqwen]], [[2604.28181-synthetic-computers-at-scale]]) implicitly assume as their compute substrate.

**Sibling practitioner course.** [[practical-deep-learning-for-coders|*Practical Deep Learning for Coders*]] ([[JeremyHoward|Howard]] / [[RachelThomas|Thomas]], [[FastAI|fast.ai]]) is the wiki's other coder-first ML course. Where Mohandas optimizes for *shipping to production* on the [[Ray]]/[[Anyscale]] stack, fast.ai optimizes for *building intuition fast* via a top-down, code-first pedagogy on [[PyTorch]] + the fastai library — train a SOTA model first, unpack the theory after. Its Part-2 capstone rebuilds [[StableDiffusion|Stable Diffusion]] from foundations (matmul → [[Backpropagation|backprop]] → autoencoders → attention/[[transformer|transformers]]), and a dedicated Lesson-9 [[DataEthics|data-ethics]] bonus has no counterpart in the Made With ML track. It pairs naturally with Howard & Parr's [[matrix-calculus-for-deep-learning|matrix-calculus tutorial]] (Corpus I-adjacent math) as the "*why the gradients work*" companion to the course's "*here's the working model*" framing.

## VI. *Probabilistic Machine Learning: An Introduction* ([[KevinMurphy|Murphy]], 23 chapters)

[[KevinMurphy]]'s 860-page [[MITPress]] textbook ([[pml1-murphy]], 2022; third printing January 2025; CC-BY-NC-ND) is the wiki's **theory-and-foundations layer**. Twenty-three chapters across five parts — Foundations (probability, statistics, decision theory, information theory, linear algebra, optimization), Linear Models (LDA, logistic / linear / generalized linear regression), Deep Neural Networks (tabular / image / sequence), Nonparametric Models (KNN, kernels, trees + boosting), and Beyond Supervised Learning (semi-/self-supervised, dimensionality reduction, clustering, recommender systems, graph embeddings) — all framed through the unifying [[ProbabilisticPerspective]].

**The probabilistic perspective** (§1.1) is the commitment that *every* unknown quantity (predictions, parameters, latent factors, missing data) is a random variable; learning is inference over distributions, not point estimation. Two justifications: (i) [[DecisionMakingUnderUncertainty|decision-theoretic]] necessity — Dutch-book / Cox / Savage axiomatizations show non-probabilistic uncertainty representations are inconsistent; (ii) it is the lingua franca of every neighboring computational science (control, OR, information theory, statistical physics).

**Three ML regimes, one math** (§1.2–§1.4). Supervised: $p(y|\mathbf{x};\boldsymbol\theta)$. Unsupervised: $p(\mathbf{x};\boldsymbol\theta)$. RL: policy $a=\pi(\mathbf{x})$ from sparse reward. The LeCun chocolate-cake analogy ranks information density: unsupervised is the cake (millions of bits/sample), supervised the icing, RL the cherry.

**The ERM → MLE → NLL bridge** (§1.2). [[EmpiricalRiskMinimization|ERM]] $\hat\theta=\arg\min \frac{1}{N}\sum\ell(y_n,f(\mathbf{x}_n;\theta))$ specializes to misclassification under zero-one loss, MSE under quadratic loss, and [[MaximumLikelihoodEstimation|MLE]] under NLL. For Gaussian regression NLL = MSE/($2\sigma^2$) + const, so least squares *is* MLE under fixed-variance Gaussian noise. For softmax classification, NLL = cross-entropy. These equivalences are how the rest of the book (and most of Corpus II's pretraining objectives) connect probabilistic modeling to optimization.

**The overfitting/generalization apparatus** (§1.2.3, §1.2.4). [[GeneralizationGap]] $\mathcal{L}(\theta;p^*)-\mathcal{L}(\theta;\mathcal{D}_{\text{train}})$ defines overfitting; train/validation/test partitions estimate it. [[NoFreeLunchTheorem]] (Wolpert 1996) forces every learner to commit to an inductive bias. Classical U-curves coexist with modern double-descent in the overparameterized regime LLM pretraining operates in.

**Why this corpus matters now.** Corpus II's 2026 LLM papers tacitly assume the *whole* of Murphy's prerequisites — Bayes' rule, NLL/MLE equivalence, GLMs, SGD/Adam, PCA, the [[CurseOfDimensionality]]. When [[2001.08361-scaling-laws]] fits a power law to pretraining cross-entropy, Murphy Ch 6 is the cross-entropy reference; when [[2605.12966-agentic-ai-to-agi]] argues the [[AverageTrap]] under a [[StructuredRealWorldDistribution]], it builds on Murphy's quadratic-loss / weighted-average / [[EmpiricalRiskMinimization|ERM]] primitives; when [[1706.03762-attention-is-all-you-need]] outputs $p(\mathbf{x}_t|\mathbf{x}_{<t})$, that conditional density is exactly Murphy §1.2.1.5.

**Connections to other corpora.**
- **Corpus I (math):** Murphy's Ch 7 (Linear Algebra) is a compressed version of the [[matrices]] / [[vectors]] / [[eigenvalues-and-eigenvectors]] / [[matrix-diagonalization]] / [[rank-of-a-matrix]] block in algebrica.org. Ch 6 (Information Theory) is downstream of [[logarithms]]. Ch 2–3 (Probability) extend the [[real-numbers]] / [[supremum-and-infimum]] / [[absolute-value]] foundations.
- **Corpus II (LLM research):** Murphy is the prerequisite-and-vocabulary layer. The [[NoFreeLunchTheorem]] of §1.2.4 is the entry point [[2605.12966-agentic-ai-to-agi]] uses to motivate the [[StructuredRealWorldDistribution]]; Murphy's Ch 8 (Optimization) underwrites every gradient-based result in the corpus.
- **Corpus III (Molnar interpretability):** Murphy provides the *fitted* models that Corpus III interprets. Ch 10 (Logistic Regression) ↔ [[imlbook-logistic]]; Ch 11 (Linear Regression) ↔ [[imlbook-limo]]; Ch 17 (Kernels) ↔ [[imlbook-shap]]'s game-theoretic substrate; Ch 18 (Trees / Forests / Boosting) ↔ [[imlbook-tree]] / [[imlbook-rulefit]]; Ch 14 (CNN feature learning) ↔ [[imlbook-cnn-features]] / [[imlbook-pixel-attribution]].
- **Corpus IV (McKinney PDA):** McKinney is the *tooling* layer (pandas/numpy/sklearn API); Murphy is the *theory* layer for the same API surface. [[pydata-modeling]] uses [[scikitlearn]] estimator objects that Murphy Ch 10–18 supplies the mathematical content of.
- **Corpus V (Made With ML):** Mohandas's foundations track is a *fast-applied* recapitulation of Murphy Ch 10–15 (logistic, MLP, CNN, RNN, attention) — same content, optimized for production rather than for theoretical completeness. Use Murphy for *why*, Mohandas for *how to ship*.

**Live tensions.** Murphy's framing is *agnostic* on the AGI path question — Part III (DNNs) is organized around monolithic supervised learning. By contrast, [[2605.12966-agentic-ai-to-agi]] argues monolithic scaling is *structurally* bottlenecked by the [[AverageTrap]] and the [[CurseOfDimensionality]] applied to ambient $D$. The two are compatible (Murphy doesn't take a position) but Murphy's structural choices read as implicit acceptance of the scaling story Liao et al. contest. Likewise, Murphy's MAR (missing-at-random) assumption (§1.5.5) is standard but bites in causal-inference / RLHF preference-data settings Corpus II partially inhabits. Finally, Murphy's "self-supervised learning as a recently popular approach" (§1.3.3) is now dated — pretraining-then-finetuning is the default, not the curiosity it was when the book was written.

---

## VII. *An Introduction to Statistical Learning* ([[GarethJames|James]] / [[DanielaWitten|Witten]] / [[TrevorHastie|Hastie]] / [[RobertTibshirani|Tibshirani]], 10 chapters)

[[islr-seventh-printing|ISLR]] ([[springer|Springer]], 2013, 8th corrected printing 2017) is the **classical-statistical-learning** layer. Ten chapters move from [[StatisticalLearning]] basics and the [[BiasVarianceTradeoff]] through the classical supervised toolbox ([[LinearRegression]], [[LogisticRegression]], [[LinearDiscriminantAnalysis|LDA]], [[QuadraticDiscriminantAnalysis|QDA]], [[KNearestNeighbors|KNN]]), resampling ([[CrossValidation]], [[Bootstrap]]), regularization & dimension reduction ([[RidgeRegression]], [[Lasso]], [[PrincipalComponentsRegression|PCR]], [[PartialLeastSquares|PLS]]), non-linearity ([[RegressionSplines]], [[SmoothingSplines]], [[LocalRegression]], [[GeneralizedAdditiveModels|GAMs]]), [[TreeBasedMethods|tree-based methods]] with [[Bagging]] / [[RandomForests]] / [[Boosting]], [[SupportVectorMachine|SVMs]] via the [[MaximalMarginClassifier]] → [[SupportVectorClassifier]] progression, and unsupervised learning ([[PrincipalComponentAnalysis|PCA]], [[KMeansClustering]], [[HierarchicalClustering]]). Every chapter ends with an [[Rlanguage|R]] lab using the [[ISLRPackage]].

**The accessibility move.** ISLR is the deliberately low-math companion to [[ElementsOfStatisticalLearning|ESL]] (Hastie/Tibshirani/Friedman 2001/2009). Premise 3 — "*while it is important to know what job is performed by each cog, it is not necessary to have the skills to construct the machine inside the box*" — explains the pedagogical bet: maximize the audience by minimizing matrix algebra, sacrifice depth, regain it via R labs. The book is the canonical reference for applied practitioners outside statistics — exactly the audience Corpus V ([[MadeWithML]]) targets, but with the *classical* methods rather than the deep-learning stack.

**Connections to other corpora.**
- **Corpus I (math):** ISLR explicitly avoids matrix algebra, but [[LinearRegression]], [[RidgeRegression]], [[Lasso]] and [[PrincipalComponentAnalysis|PCA]] all rest on the [[matrices]] / [[determinant-of-a-square-matrix]] / [[eigenvalues-and-eigenvectors]] block. Treat algebrica.org as the formal prerequisite ISLR explicitly waives.
- **Corpus III (Molnar interpretability):** ISLR's Ch.3 ([[LinearRegression]]), Ch.4 ([[LogisticRegression]] / [[LinearDiscriminantAnalysis|LDA]]), Ch.8 ([[DecisionTrees]] / [[Bagging]] / [[RandomForests]] / [[Boosting]]) are the *fitted models* whose interpretation Molnar formalizes (e.g. [[imlbook-tree]] ↔ ISLR §8.1; [[imlbook-limo]] ↔ ISLR §3.1; [[imlbook-rulefit]] ↔ ISLR §8.2.3 Boosting + Friedman's RuleFit).
- **Corpus IV (McKinney PDA):** [[pydata-modeling]] uses [[scikitlearn]] / [[statsmodels]] estimators that implement the same methods ISLR teaches in R — `LinearRegression` ↔ Ch.3, `LogisticRegression` ↔ Ch.4, `RandomForestRegressor` ↔ Ch.8.2.2, `Lasso` ↔ Ch.6.2.2. The [[Patsy]] formula syntax in `pydata-modeling` is the Python port of ISLR's R formula syntax.
- **Corpus V (Made With ML):** Mohandas's foundations track recapitulates the *neural* side of statistical learning; ISLR is the *classical* counterpart. For a tabular problem, ISLR's Ch.6 ([[Lasso]] / [[RidgeRegression]]) / Ch.8 ([[RandomForests]] / [[Boosting]]) typically beats the MLP/CNN stack of the Made With ML foundations track — confirming ISLR Premise 2 ("no single approach will perform well in all possible applications").
- **Corpus VI (Murphy):** Strong overlap — Murphy Ch.10 ([[LogisticRegression]]) ↔ ISLR Ch.4; Murphy Ch.11 ([[LinearRegression]]) ↔ ISLR Ch.3; Murphy Ch.18 (Trees / Forests / Boosting) ↔ ISLR Ch.8; Murphy Ch.20 (Clustering) ↔ ISLR Ch.10. Use Murphy for the [[ProbabilisticPerspective|probabilistic]] derivation and exponential-family unifying frame ([[GeneralizedLinearModels]]); use ISLR for the applied recipe, the bias-variance intuition, and the R lab. They are deeply complementary: Murphy supplies the *why* at the level of MLE/Bayes/optimization; ISLR supplies the *how* at the level of fit-evaluate-tune.

**Live tensions.**
- **The R / Python split.** ISLR's labs are R-only; Corpora IV–V are Python-only. The methods are language-agnostic, but the lab-style "hands-on" learning loop ISLR commits to (Premise 4) does not translate verbatim to the Python ecosystem — practitioners coming from [[pydata-modeling]] need to translate from `lm()`/`glm()`/`tree::tree()` to [[scikitlearn]] / [[statsmodels]] estimators.
- **Classical vs deep.** ISLR ends in 2013 and includes no deep learning, attention, or representation learning — the entire frontier of Corpus II. This is the corpus's load-bearing limitation: when a 2026 paper discusses [[Attention]], [[Transformer]], or [[Backpropagation]] through deep stacks, ISLR is silent and Corpora V–VI take over. ISLR is canonical for *tabular* and *classical* problems; Corpus VI (Murphy) is the bridge into deep learning.
- **The Lasso lineage.** [[RobertTibshirani]] is both ISLR co-author and the original proposer of the [[Lasso]] (1996). The Lasso's role in Corpus II is mostly historical — modern LLM regularization is dropout / weight decay / layer norm — but the *idea* of $\ell_1$-induced sparsity reappears in [[MixtureOfExperts]] gating and in skill-selection mechanisms across the 2026 agentic papers. ISLR Ch.6 is the canonical reference for the original argument.

---

## VIII. *Mathematics for Machine Learning* ([[MarcDeisenroth|Deisenroth]] / [[AAldoFaisal|Faisal]] / [[ChengSoonOng|Ong]], 12 chapters)

[[MarcDeisenroth|Marc Peter Deisenroth]], [[AAldoFaisal|A. Aldo Faisal]] & [[ChengSoonOng|Cheng Soon Ong]]'s 417-page [[CambridgeUniversityPress]] textbook ([[mml-book]], 2020, draft revision 2024-01-15, freely distributed at <https://mml-book.com>) is the wiki's **prerequisites-and-foundations** layer. Where Murphy ([[pml1-murphy]]) is encyclopedic-and-probability-first and ISLR is hands-on-and-statistical, MML is **math-first** with four worked ML examples: it surfaces the [[LinearAlgebra]], [[AnalyticGeometry]], [[MatrixDecomposition]], [[VectorCalculus]], [[ProbabilityAndDistributions]], and [[ContinuousOptimization]] machinery that the other textbook corpora assume and only re-derives a small number of ML algorithms ([[LinearRegression]], [[PrincipalComponentAnalysis|PCA]], [[GaussianMixtureModel|GMM]], [[SupportVectorMachine|SVM]]) as load-bearing examples.

**Structure.** The 12 chapters split across two parts following the Foreword's "four pillars on six foundations" diagram (Fig 1.1):

- **Part I — Mathematical Foundations:**
  - **Ch 2 [[LinearAlgebra]]** — [[VectorSpace]]s, [[LinearIndependence]], [[Basis]], [[Rank]], [[LinearMapping]], Gaussian elimination, [[AffineSpace]]s.
  - **Ch 3 [[AnalyticGeometry]]** — [[Norm]], [[InnerProduct]], [[CauchySchwarzInequality]], angles, [[OrthogonalProjection]], rotations, [[SymmetricPositiveDefiniteMatrix|SPD matrices]] ↔ inner products.
  - **Ch 4 [[MatrixDecomposition]]** — [[Determinant]], [[Trace]], [[CharacteristicPolynomial]], [[Eigendecomposition]], [[CholeskyDecomposition]], [[SingularValueDecomposition|SVD]], matrix approximation, "matrix phylogeny."
  - **Ch 5 [[VectorCalculus]]** — [[PartialDerivative]], [[Gradient]], [[Jacobian]], [[Hessian]], [[ChainRule]], [[Backpropagation]] / [[AutomaticDifferentiation]], [[TaylorSeries]], [[Linearization]].
  - **Ch 6 [[ProbabilityAndDistributions]]** — [[ProbabilitySpace]], [[RandomVariable]], discrete vs continuous, [[BayesTheorem]], summary statistics, [[GaussianDistribution]], [[ExponentialFamily]] + [[ConjugatePrior]], change of variables.
  - **Ch 7 [[ContinuousOptimization]]** — [[GradientDescent]] + [[Momentum]] + [[StochasticGradientDescent|SGD]] + [[ConditionNumber]] + [[Preconditioner]]; [[LagrangeMultipliers]]; [[ConvexOptimization]] + duality + LP + QP.
- **Part II — Central Machine Learning Problems:**
  - **Ch 8 When Models Meet Data** — data/models/learning; [[EmpiricalRiskMinimization|ERM]] + loss functions; [[MaximumLikelihoodEstimation|MLE]] + [[MAPEstimation|MAP]] + full Bayesian inference; [[ProbabilisticGraphicalModel|graphical models]]; [[ModelSelection]] via [[NestedCrossValidation]]; framing as [[Abduction]] (§8.2 marginal).
  - **Ch 9 [[LinearRegression]]** — Gaussian-noise likelihood ⇒ closed-form MLE $\boldsymbol\theta_{\text{ML}}=(\mathbf{X}^\top\mathbf{X})^{-1}\mathbf{X}^\top\mathbf{y}$; [[FeatureMap]] + [[DesignMatrix]] ⇒ polynomial / nonlinear regression; [[BayesianLinearRegression]]; MLE *as* [[OrthogonalProjection]].
  - **Ch 10 [[PrincipalComponentAnalysis|PCA]]** — four equivalent derivations (max variance, min reconstruction error, [[KarhunenLoeveTransform]], probabilistic latent-variable); [[DataCovarianceMatrix]]; algorithm via [[Eigendecomposition]] (or directly [[SingularValueDecomposition|SVD]]).
  - **Ch 11 [[GaussianMixtureModel|GMM]] + Density Estimation** — [[MixtureModel]] framing; MLE has no closed form ⇒ [[EMAlgorithm]]; [[Responsibility|responsibilities]] $r_{nk}$ as soft cluster assignment; $k$-means as the hard-assignment limit.
  - **Ch 12 [[SupportVectorMachine|SVM]] + Classification** ([[mml-ch12-classification-svm|per-chapter deep dive]]) — [[SeparatingHyperplane]] + [[Margin]] maximization ($r=1/\|\mathbf{w}\|$); [[HardMarginSVM|hard-]] vs [[SoftMarginSVM|soft-margin]] ([[SlackVariable|slack]] $\xi_n$ + box $C$); the [[HingeLoss|hinge-loss]] regularized-ERM reading (margin = $\ell_2$ regularizer); the [[DualSVM|dual SVM]] via [[LagrangianDuality|Lagrangian duality]] exposing [[SupportVector|support vectors]] (+ [[ConvexHull|convex-hull]] view); the [[KernelTrick|kernel trick]] (PSD [[KernelFunction|kernel]]/[[GramMatrix|Gram matrix]], §12.4) lifting to nonlinear classifiers without computing $\boldsymbol\phi$.

With Ch 12 ingested, the **per-chapter deep ingest of all 12 MML chapters is complete** (Chs 1–12) — the four ML pillars (regression Ch 9 / dimensionality reduction Ch 10 / density estimation Ch 11 / classification Ch 12) each now have a dedicated source page resting on the six foundations.

**Why this corpus matters now.** Every other textbook corpus assumes its content. When Murphy writes "let $\boldsymbol\Sigma$ be SPD" or "by Cauchy-Schwarz" or "the Hessian $\nabla^2 f$" or "let's apply Bayes' rule with conjugate prior," the reader needs MML. When ISLR derives the normal equations or projects onto the principal subspace, the reader needs MML. When [[1706.03762-attention-is-all-you-need]]'s attention is "scaled dot-product" + "softmax-cross-entropy NLL," the reader needs MML Ch 3 (inner products) + Ch 6 (NLL = exponential-family loss). When [[2001.08361-scaling-laws]] fits a power law to cross-entropy, the reader needs MML §8.3 (MLE) + Ch 6 ([[CrossEntropyLoss]] via Bernoulli/Categorical likelihoods).

**Connections to other corpora.**
- **Corpus I (algebrica.org):** MML Ch 2 + Ch 4 elaborate the algebrica.org [[vector-spaces]] / [[matrices]] / [[matrix-diagonalization]] / [[eigenvalues-and-eigenvectors]] / [[inverse-matrix]] / [[rank-of-a-matrix]] block into the mappings-and-bases view ML uses. MML Ch 3 ([[AnalyticGeometry]]) generalizes algebrica.org's geometric blocks ([[unit-circle]], [[pythagorean-theorem]]) to inner-product spaces. MML Ch 5 ([[VectorCalculus]]) is the multivariate extension of algebrica.org's [[remarkable-limits]] / [[indefinite-integrals]] stub block.
- **Corpus II (LLM research):** Ch 5 backprop + Ch 7 optimization + Ch 8 MLE are the load-bearing prerequisites for [[1706.03762-attention-is-all-you-need]] / [[1810.04805-bert]] / [[1910.10683-t5]] / [[2001.08361-scaling-laws]] / [[2205.14135-flashattention]] training-stack derivations. [[NoFreeLunchTheorem]] + [[Abduction]] + [[ModelSelection]] frame the [[StructuredRealWorldDistribution]] argument of [[2605.12966-agentic-ai-to-agi]].
- **Corpus III (Molnar):** MML Ch 4 (SVD) + Ch 5 (gradients) + Ch 12 (kernels) are the math underneath [[imlbook-shap]] / [[imlbook-shapley]] / [[imlbook-counterfactual]] / [[imlbook-pdp]] / [[imlbook-ale]].
- **Corpus IV (McKinney) + V (Mohandas):** MML supplies the *why* layer for the *what to compute* in [[pydata-modeling]] / [[madewithml-foundations-pytorch]] / [[madewithml-mlops-training]]. Use MML to understand the math, the applied corpora to ship it.
- **Corpus VI (Murphy):** MML and Murphy *cover overlapping ground with different framings*. MML is math-first, four-ML-examples-deep; Murphy is probability-first, encyclopedic. They pair on every overlapping chapter — see the [[mml-book]] source page for the chapter-by-chapter pairing table.
- **Corpus VII (ISLR):** MML and ISLR overlap on regression, classification, PCA. MML develops them from first principles with explicit derivations; ISLR develops them with R-lab worked examples on real datasets. ISLR is for *hands-on intuition*; MML is for *mathematical understanding*; Murphy is for *probabilistic depth*.

**Live tensions.**
- **Row-vector vs column-vector gradient.** MML uses the row-vector convention (§5.40); Murphy / Goodfellow use the column-vector convention. Same math, transpose-different — flagged on [[Gradient]] / [[Jacobian]].
- **Bias term absent from linear regression.** MML Ch 9 (Eq. 9.4) defines $f(\mathbf{x})=\boldsymbol\theta^\top\mathbf{x}$ without an intercept, requiring the $x^{(0)}=1$ augmentation trick. The [[madewithml-foundations-linear-regression]] convention carries $\theta_0$ separately. Both conventions are correct; cross-referencing requires noting the augmentation.
- **Monolithic-scaling framing.** MML's Part II is structured around monolithic supervised learning — implicit acceptance of the scaling story that [[2605.12966-agentic-ai-to-agi]] argues is *structurally* bottlenecked by the [[AverageTrap]]. MML is compatible with the [[AgenticAI]] critique but doesn't take a position. (Murphy has the same neutrality.)
- **Self-supervised pretraining is absent.** MML's 2020 vintage predates the BERT-era explosion as the conceptual core of ML. The book is silent on self-supervised learning, MLM, autoregressive LM training — Corpora II + V take over here.

---

## IX. *Dive into Deep Learning* (Zhang / Lipton / Li / Smola, **complete — 26/26 chapters**)

> 2026-05-16 closing addendum: [[d2l-appendix-tools]] now ingested — D2L's eight-section operational appendix: [[Jupyter|Jupyter Notebook]] as the primary authoring environment (notedown plugin for markdown-source notebooks, ssh `-L` port-forwarding for remote kernels), [[AmazonSageMaker]] as the fastest path to a GPU notebook (`ml.p3.2xlarge` V100, repo cloned at provision time), [[AmazonEC2]] as the cheaper alternative (Ubuntu AMI → `apt-get install cuda` → [[Miniconda]] → `pip install d2l==1.0.3`), [[GoogleColab]] as the free-tier alternative, *Selecting Servers and GPUs* hardware guide with explicit 1/2/4/8-GPU recipes ([[NVIDIA]] dominates via [[CUDA]] support; FP16 / Tensor Cores; PCIe lanes; cooling), *Contributing* GitHub fork → PR walkthrough with `%%tab` multi-framework conventions, and the alphabetical [[D2LPackage|`d2l` package]] API ([[Module]] / [[DataModule]] / [[Trainer]] / [[MultiHeadAttention]] / [[TransformerEncoder]] / [[Seq2Seq]] / `try_gpu` / `bleu` / `corr2d`). With this ingest **the entire D2L corpus is complete at 26/26 chapters**, contributing 26 source pages to the wiki's 242-source total. D2L is the wiki's *applied-deep-learning textbook layer*: pedagogically interleaving theory and runnable PyTorch / JAX / MXNet / TensorFlow code, where [[mml-book|MML]] is math-first, [[pml1-murphy|Murphy]] is encyclopedic, [[islr-seventh-printing|ISLR]] uses R-labs, and Corpus II (LLM research papers) is research-frontier-first. The corpus now anchors the wiki's coverage of every architectural primitive ([[MultilayerPerceptron|MLP]] → [[CNN]] → [[RNN]] → [[LSTM]] / [[GRU]] → [[Attention]] → [[Transformer]] → [[VisionTransformer|ViT]] → [[GenerativeAdversarialNetwork|GAN]] / [[DCGAN]]), every optimizer ([[SGD]] / [[Momentum]] / [[Adagrad]] / [[RMSProp]] / [[Adam]] / [[CosineLRSchedule|cosine LR]] + [[Warmup]]), every systems primitive ([[DataParallelism]] / [[AllReduce]] / [[RingAllReduce]] / [[ParameterServer]] / [[NVLink]] / [[HBM]] / [[TensorCore]]), every application domain ([[ComputerVision]] / [[NLP]] / [[RecommenderSystems]] / [[reinforcementlearning|RL]] / [[GaussianProcess|GPs]] / [[HyperparameterOptimization|HPO]] / [[GAN|generative modeling]]), the math-foundation appendix, and now the operational toolchain. The full corpus is the canonical bridge from Corpus VI–VIII (Murphy / ISLR / MML mathematical foundations) into Corpus II (LLM research) — the curriculum that lets a reader actually *run* the rest of the wiki.

> 2026-05-16 addendum: [[d2l-appendix-mathematics]] now ingested — D2L's eleven-section *Mathematics for Deep Learning* appendix ([[BrentWerness|Werness]] & [[RachelHu|Hu]] (Amazon) + the D2L authors) re-derives [[LinearAlgebra]] / [[Eigendecomposition]] / single- and multi-variable / [[IntegralCalculus|integral]] calculus / [[RandomVariable|random variables]] / [[MaximumLikelihoodEstimation|MLE]] (with [[FisherInformation|Fisher information]] + asymptotic theory) / common [[ProbabilityDistribution|distributions]] ([[BernoulliDistribution|Bernoulli]] / [[PoissonDistribution|Poisson]] / [[GaussianDistribution|Normal]] / [[ExponentialDistribution|Exponential]] + [[CentralLimitTheorem|CLT]]) / [[NaiveBayes|naive Bayes]] (~84% on binarized [[MNIST]]) / [[Statistics|statistics]] ([[BiasVarianceDecomposition|bias-variance]] / [[HypothesisTesting|hypothesis tests]] / [[ConfidenceInterval|CIs]]) / [[InformationTheory|information theory]] ([[Entropy]] / [[KullbackLeiblerDivergence|KL]] / [[MutualInformation|MI]] / [[CrossEntropy|cross-entropy]] = $H(P)+D_{KL}(P\|Q)$ — the load-bearing identity behind every classification loss = MLE). The math reference shelf under every later D2L chapter; closes D2L's foundations layer.


[[d2l-preface|D2L]] is the wiki's **deep-learning-centric textbook** layer. **All 26 chapters ingested** as of 2026-05-16 (corpus complete): [[d2l-preface]] (pedagogical thesis: concepts / context / code, just-in-time; "one working example, one notebook"; multi-framework [[MXNet]] / [[PyTorch]] / [[TensorFlow]] / [[JAX]]); [[d2l-installation]] (operational [[Miniconda]] + framework + `d2l==1.0.3` setup); [[d2l-notation]] (cross-chapter typographic conventions); [[d2l-introduction]] (broad ML/DL survey); [[d2l-preliminaries]] (the seven "survival skills" sections: [[NDArray|tensors]] + [[Broadcasting]], [[pandas]] preprocessing, [[LinearAlgebra]] + [[Norm|norms]], [[Calculus]] + [[ChainRule|chain rule]], [[Autograd]] + [[ComputationalGraph]], [[probability]] / [[BayesTheorem]] / [[JointProbability|joint]] / [[ConditionalProbability|conditional]] / [[StatisticalIndependence|independence]], and API lookup); [[d2l-linear-regression]] (first modeling chapter: [[LinearRegression]] as single-layer NN, [[MeanSquaredError|MSE]] = Gaussian [[MaximumLikelihoodEstimation|MLE]], closed-form vs [[MinibatchSGD|minibatch SGD]], D2L's [[Module]] / [[DataModule]] / [[Trainer]] [[ObjectOrientedDesign|OO]] scaffold inspired by [[PyTorchLightning]], [[Generalization]] / IID / [[Overfitting]] / [[Underfitting]] / [[CrossValidation|$K$-fold CV]], [[WeightDecay]] as $\ell_2$ regularization integrated into the optimizer); and [[d2l-linear-classification]] (second modeling chapter: [[Softmax|softmax regression]] = one-hot labels + affine + softmax + [[CrossEntropyLoss|cross-entropy]]; [[FashionMNIST|Fashion-MNIST]] as the running benchmark replacing [[MNIST]]; the [[LogSumExp|LogSumExp trick]] for numerical stability; statistical-learning-theory cluster — [[HoeffdingsInequality|Hoeffding bounds]], [[UniformConvergence|uniform convergence]], [[VCDimension|VC dimension]], [[TestSetReuse|adaptive overfitting]]; and the canonical [[DistributionShift|distribution-shift]] taxonomy — [[CovariateShift|covariate]] / [[LabelShift|label]] / [[ConceptShift|concept shift]] with importance-weighted [[EmpiricalRiskMinimization|ERM]] correction); and [[d2l-multilayer-perceptrons]] (the first genuinely *deep* chapter: [[HiddenLayer|hidden layers]] + nonlinear [[ActivationFunction|activations]] ([[ReLU]] / [[Sigmoid]] / [[Tanh]]) unlock [[MultilayerPerceptron|MLPs]]; [[UniversalApproximationTheorem|universal approximation]]; [[ForwardPropagation|forward]] / [[Backpropagation|backward]] propagation on the [[ComputationalGraph]]; [[VanishingGradient]] / [[ExplodingGradient]] and [[XavierInitialization|Xavier]] / [[HeInitialization|He]] init; modern generalization — [[InterpolationRegime|interpolation regime]] + [[DoubleDescent|double descent]] + [[NeuralTangentKernel|NTK]]; [[Dropout]] regularization + [[EarlyStopping|early stopping]] + [[WeightDecay]]; [[Kaggle]] House Prices capstone); and [[d2l-builders-guide]] (the "power-user" operations chapter: the universal [[NeuralNetworkModule|module / block]] abstraction; `Sequential` as a thin daisy-chain subclass; [[ParameterAccess|parameter access]] / [[ParameterSharing|tied weights]] / [[ParameterInitialization|`net.apply(init_fn)`]]; [[LazyInitialization|lazy init]] (`nn.LazyLinear`) deferring shape inference; [[CustomLayer|custom layers]] via `nn.Parameter`; [[FileIO|file I/O]] + [[Checkpoint|checkpointing]] via [[StateDict|`state_dict`]] (architecture *not* serialized — code rebuilds); [[GPU]] device handles `torch.device('cuda:i')`, model `.to(device)`, same-device-operand rule, and the "transfers are slow" cost model that propagates from [[gpumemoryhierarchy|HBM↔SRAM]] up to host↔device); and [[d2l-convolutional-neural-networks]] (D2L's first vision-shaped chapter: derives the [[ConvolutionalLayer|convolutional layer]] from two priors — [[TranslationInvariance]] + [[Locality]] — imposed on an MLP, collapsing a $10^{12}$-parameter FC layer over a $1000\times1000$ image into $4\Delta^2$ shared weights; what frameworks call "convolution" is actually [[CrossCorrelation|cross-correlation]] (kernel-flip is invisible to learned models); operational stack — [[Padding]] / [[Stride]] output-shape arithmetic, multi-channel kernels ($c_o\times c_i\times k_h\times k_w$) summing over input channels and stacking over output channels, the [[OneByOneConvolution|$1\times1$ conv]] as per-pixel channel-mixing FC layer; [[MaxPooling|max-]] / [[AveragePooling|average-pooling]] as parameter-free per-channel aggregators providing local translation invariance; [[ReceptiveField|receptive fields]] grown multiplicatively by depth + stride + pooling, Hubel & Wiesel neurophysiology lineage; capstone is a from-scratch [[LeNet|LeNet-5]] ([[YannLeCun|LeCun]], [[LeonBottou|Bottou]], Bengio, Haffner 1998) trained on [[FashionMNIST]] — conv→sigmoid→AvgPool→conv→sigmoid→AvgPool→flatten→FC(120)→FC(84)→FC(10), the first CNN to match SVMs on real OCR and the deployed-to-ATMs proof-of-concept of pre-revival deep learning); and [[d2l-convolutional-modern]] (post-LeNet CNN tour, 8 sections: [[AlexNet]] 2012 ([[AlexKrizhevsky|Krizhevsky]] / [[IlyaSutskever|Sutskever]] / [[GeoffreyHinton|Hinton]]) breaks the hand-engineered-features paradigm on [[ImageNet]] with [[ReLU]] + [[Dropout]] on dual GTX 580s → [[VGG]] 2014 ([[KarenSimonyan|Simonyan]] & [[AndrewZisserman|Zisserman]], [[oxforduniversity|Oxford]]) introduces blocks of small $3\times3$ convs — "deep and narrow beats shallow" — and the *family* paradigm → [[NetworkInNetwork|NiN]] 2013 introduces $1\times1$ convs and [[GlobalAveragePooling|global average pooling]] (kills the FC head) → [[GoogLeNet]] / [[Inception]] 2014 ([[ChristianSzegedy|Szegedy]] et al.) multi-branch concatenation + stem/body/head template → [[BatchNormalization|batch normalization]] 2015 ([[SergeyIoffe|Ioffe]] & [[ChristianSzegedy|Szegedy]]): $\gamma\odot(\mathbf{x}-\hat\mu_\mathcal{B})/\hat\sigma_\mathcal{B}+\beta$ — internal-covariate-shift motivation now disputed but BN is "indispensable" → [[ResNet]] 2015 ([[KaimingHe|He]] et al., MSRA) makes the identity easy to learn via [[ResidualConnection|residual connections]] $f(\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{x}+g(\mathbf{x})$, solving the **degradation problem** and enabling 152-layer networks (and propagating into [[transformer|Transformers]] / RNNs / GNNs) → [[ResNeXt]] 2017 generalizes with [[GroupedConvolution|grouped convolutions]] → [[DenseNet]] 2017 ([[GaoHuang|Huang]] et al.) concatenates instead of adds for aggressive feature reuse → closes with [[RegNet]] 2020 ([[IliaRadosavovic|Radosavovic]] et al., [[fair|FAIR]]) which optimizes *distributions* of networks via empirical CDFs to discover transferable design principles (share bottleneck ratio, share group width, increase channels/depth across stages, linear width-vs-block-index) — and the [[VisionTransformer|ViT]] transition: "scalability trumps inductive biases."); and [[d2l-recurrent-neural-networks]] (D2L's pivot from fixed-length to *sequence* data: [[AutoregressiveModel|autoregressive]] sequence math + $k$-step extrapolation-error compounding → text-preprocessing pipeline + [[ZipfsLaw|Zipf]] on *The Time Machine* → [[LanguageModel|language models]] as $\prod_t P(x_t\mid x_{<t})$, [[NGram]] / [[LaplaceSmoothing]] baselines and their inadequacy at scale, [[Perplexity]] as the NLP-standard LM metric → the [[RNN]] recurrence $\mathbf{H}_t=\phi(\mathbf{X}_t\mathbf{W}_\textrm{xh}+\mathbf{H}_{t-1}\mathbf{W}_\textrm{hh}+\mathbf{b}_\textrm{h})$ with weight-tied parameters across time, [[HiddenState|hidden state]] as memory → from-scratch [[CharacterLevelLanguageModel|char-level RNN LM]] with [[TeacherForcing|teacher forcing]] + softmax/cross-entropy + [[GradientClipping|gradient clipping]] $\mathbf{g}\leftarrow\min(1,\theta/\|\mathbf{g}\|)\mathbf{g}$ ("a hack, but a useful hack") → closed-form [[BPTT]] (Werbos 1990) with $\partial L/\partial\mathbf{h}_t = \sum_i (\mathbf{W}_\textrm{hh}^\top)^{T-i}\mathbf{W}_\textrm{qh}^\top \partial L/\partial \mathbf{o}_{T+t-i}$ — eigenvalue powers manifest as [[VanishingGradient|vanishing]] / [[ExplodingGradient|exploding]] gradients; [[TruncatedBPTT|truncated BPTT]] (Jaeger 2002) is what frameworks implement, randomized truncation (Tallec & Ollivier 2017) is empirically no better. Closes pointing forward to [[LSTM]] / [[GRU]] and the [[transformer|Transformer]] regime change); and [[d2l-recurrent-modern]] (the 8-section *Modern RNN* sequel: [[LSTM]] ([[SeppHochreiter|Hochreiter]] & [[JurgenSchmidhuber|Schmidhuber]] 1997) with [[MemoryCell|memory cell]] + [[InputGate|input]] / [[ForgetGate|forget]] / [[OutputGate|output]] gates and weight-1 self-recurrence as the structural fix for vanishing gradients → [[GRU]] ([[KyunghyunCho|Cho]] et al. 2014) streamlines this to [[ResetGate|reset]] + [[UpdateGate|update]] gates → [[DeepRNN|deep RNNs]] stack $L$ layers, [[BidirectionalRNN|bi-RNNs]] ([[MikeSchuster|Schuster]] & [[KuldipPaliwal|Paliwal]] 1997) concatenate forward + backward (encoder-only — "no future at inference" blocks decoder use) → pivots to [[MachineTranslation|MT]] as the canonical [[SeqToSeq|seq2seq]] task: [[EncoderDecoder|encoder-decoder]] template ([[KyunghyunCho|Cho]] et al. 2014; [[1409.3215-seq2seq|Sutskever-Vinyals-Le 2014]]) with [[TeacherForcing|teacher-forced]] decoder and `valid_len`-masked cross-entropy → [[BLEU]] ([[KishorePapineni|Papineni]] et al. 2002) as modified-$n$-gram-precision with brevity penalty → [[BeamSearch|beam search]] with length-penalty $\frac{1}{L^\alpha}\sum\log P$ ($\alpha\!=\!0.75$) bridges [[GreedyDecoding|greedy]] $\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{Y}|T')$ and exhaustive $\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{Y}|^{T'})$ at $\mathcal{O}(k|\mathcal{Y}|T')$. The textbook bridge to the [[transformer|Transformer]]: every architectural primitive needed to read [[1409.3215-seq2seq]] is now established); and [[d2l-attention-and-transformers]] (the 9-section Transformer-regime chapter: [[Attention|attention]] formalized as a soft database query $\textrm{Attention}(\mathbf{q},\mathcal{D})=\sum_i\alpha(\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}_i)\mathbf{v}_i$ → [[NadarayaWatson|Nadaraya–Watson]] kernel regression as the half-century precursor → [[ScaledDotProductAttention|scaled dot-product]] $\mathbf{q}^\top\mathbf{k}/\sqrt{d}$ vs [[AdditiveAttention|additive]] $\mathbf{w}_v^\top\tanh(\mathbf{W}_q\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{W}_k\mathbf{k})$ [[AttentionScoringFunctions|scoring]] with [[MaskedSoftmax|masked softmax]] padding → [[BahdanauAttention|Bahdanau, Cho & Bengio 2014]] differentiable alignment ($\mathbf{c}_{t'}=\sum_t\alpha(\mathbf{s}_{t'-1},\mathbf{h}_t)\mathbf{h}_t$) as the structural fix for the fixed-vector bottleneck of [[1409.3215-seq2seq|Sutskever-Vinyals-Le]] → [[MultiHeadAttention|multi-head]] $h$-parallel pools over different representation subspaces → [[SelfAttention|self-attention]]'s structural trade-off ($O(n^2 d)$ compute, $O(1)$ sequential ops, $O(1)$ max path length — vs CNN $O(knd^2)/O(1)/O(n/k)$ and RNN $O(nd^2)/O(n)/O(n)$) + sinusoidal [[PositionalEncoding]] $p_{i,2j}=\sin(i/10000^{2j/d})$ with the $2\times 2$ relative-offset linear-projection identity → the [[Transformer]] block ([[1706.03762-attention-is-all-you-need|Vaswani et al. 2017]]): MHA + positionwise [[FeedForwardNetwork|FFN]] + [[ResidualConnection]] + [[LayerNormalization|LayerNorm]] post-norm, decoder adds causal-masked self-attention + encoder–decoder cross-attention → the three pretraining modes: encoder-only [[BERT]] ([[MaskedLanguageModeling|MLM]] over 250B tokens), encoder–decoder [[T5]] ([[SpanCorruption|span corruption]] over [[C4]]'s 1T tokens with unified text-to-text framing), decoder-only [[GPT]] / [[GPT-2]] / [[GPT-3]] (autoregressive LM with [[InContextLearning|in-context]] zero/one/few-shot) → [[2001.08361-scaling-laws|Kaplan et al. 2020]] power-law scaling in $N$/$D$/$C$ → [[ChainOfThought|CoT prompting]], [[RLHF]], [[InstructGPT]], [[ChatGPT]] → and the [[VisionTransformer|Vision Transformer]] ([[Dosovitskiy|Dosovitskiy]] et al. 2021): images as flattened patches (linear projection ≡ stride-$p$ conv) + learnable [[ClsToken|`<cls>` token]] + learned positional embeddings + [[PreNorm|pre-norm]] + [[GELU]] MLP — "scalability trumps inductive biases" when training larger models on larger datasets. Closes by recasting Transformers as the cross-modal backbone ([[CLIP]], [[DALLE2|DALL-E 2]], [[Imagen]], [[Parti]], [[Gato]] all serialize their modalities into tokens). The arc *MLP → CNN → RNN → gated RNN + seq2seq → attention + Transformer + pretrained foundation models + ViT* is now complete); and [[d2l-optimization]] (the 11-section theoretical chapter on *Optimization Algorithms* — D2L's deepest optimization-theory excursion: distinguishes optimization's goal (minimize training loss) from DL's (minimize generalization); catalogs nonconvex pathologies — local minima, [[SaddlePoint|saddle points]] (exponentially dominant in high $d$ since Hessian eigenvalue signs flip independently), [[VanishingGradient|vanishing gradients]]; a [[Convexity|convexity]] primer (sets / functions / [[JensensInequality|Jensen]] / Hessian-PSD / Lagrangian / penalty / projection — reinterpreting [[WeightDecay]] as the penalty approximation and [[GradientClipping]] as $\ell_2$-ball projection); a step-by-step algorithm tour — [[GradientDescent]] from Taylor expansion, [[NewtonsMethod]] (one-step on quadratics, fatal on nonconvex), [[Preconditioning|diagonal preconditioning]] as the cheap Newton proxy, [[LineSearch]] (too costly for DL); [[StochasticGradientDescent|SGD]] as $\mathcal{O}(1)$-per-step unbiased estimator with $\mathcal{O}(1/\sqrt{T})$ convex convergence rate (Nesterov-Vial 2000 proof) under vanishing $\eta$ schedules; [[MinibatchSGD|minibatch SGD]] as the vectorization-friendly compromise (variance ↓ $b^{-1/2}$); [[Momentum|momentum]] / leaky averaging $\mathbf{v}_t = \beta\mathbf{v}_{t-1}+\mathbf{g}_t$ ([[BorisPolyak|Polyak]] 1964) as both acceleration on ill-conditioned problems (eigendecomposition analysis on convex quadratics shows the stable range expands from $0<\eta\lambda<2$ to $0<\eta\lambda<2+2\beta$) and noise smoothing, with effective sample weight $\frac{1}{1-\beta}$; the [[Adagrad]] → [[RMSProp]] → [[Adadelta]] → [[Adam]] progression — [[Adagrad]] ([[JohnDuchi|Duchi]], Hazan & Singer 2011) introduces per-coordinate scaling via $\mathbf{s}_t = \mathbf{s}_{t-1}+\mathbf{g}_t^2$ as cheap diagonal-Hessian proxy (great for sparse features, decay too aggressive for DL); [[RMSProp]] ([[GeoffreyHinton|Tieleman & Hinton]] 2012) fixes this with leaky-average $\mathbf{s}_t = \gamma\mathbf{s}_{t-1}+(1-\gamma)\mathbf{g}_t^2$, decoupling rate-scheduling from coordinate-adaptivity; [[Adadelta]] ([[MatthewZeiler|Zeiler]] 2012) eliminates the global learning rate by using $\sqrt{\Delta\mathbf{x}_{t-1}}$ as the unit-matching numerator; [[Adam]] ([[DiederikKingma|Kingma]] & [[JimmyBa|Ba]] 2014) unifies momentum + RMSProp + **bias correction** $\hat{\mathbf{v}}_t = \mathbf{v}_t/(1-\beta_1^t)$, $\hat{\mathbf{s}}_t = \mathbf{s}_t/(1-\beta_2^t)$ with defaults $(\beta_1, \beta_2, \epsilon) = (0.9, 0.999, 10^{-6})$; [[Yogi]] (Zaheer, Reddi et al. 2018) is the divergence-fix variant for high-variance second moments. Closes with the [[LearningRateScheduler|learning-rate scheduler]] taxonomy on three axes — **magnitude / decay rate / initialization (warmup)** — and canonical policies: factor / multifactor / polynomial $\eta_0(t+1)^{-\alpha}$ (square-root default) / [[CosineLRSchedule|cosine annealing]] (Loshchilov & Hutter 2016) $\eta_T+\tfrac{\eta_0-\eta_T}{2}(1+\cos(\pi t/T))$ + [[Warmup|linear warmup]] (Gotmare, Keskar, Xiong et al. 2018) — establishing the **warmup + cosine** recipe that is now default in [[transformer|Transformer]] / LLM pretraining. The chapter is the wiki's canonical reference for *every* gradient-based optimizer beneath every neural net in D2L and Corpus II — and the theoretical answer to [[d2l-linear-regression]]'s assertion that "minibatch SGD is the universal DL optimizer."); and [[d2l-computational-performance]] (the 7-section *Computational Performance* chapter — D2L's systems-and-scaling layer that bridges single-GPU single-process training to the multi-GPU multi-machine regime every frontier workload assumes: [[ImperativeProgramming|imperative]] vs [[SymbolicProgramming|symbolic]] programming + the hybrid bridges ([[TorchScript|`torch.jit.script`]] / `tf.function` + [[XLA]] / MXNet `hybridize()`) that *write imperatively, compile symbolically* and produce Python-independent serializable graphs; [[AsyncComputation|asynchronous frontend/backend dispatch]] over the [[ComputationalGraph|dep-graph]] (PyTorch GPU ops async by default; reducing 10000 sequential `y=x+1` ops from $10000(t_1+t_2+t_3)$ to $t_1+10000\,t_2+t_3$) + the implicit-blocker pitfalls (`print` / `.item()` / `.numpy()` / `wait_to_read()` — *"copying small amounts of data frequently from MXNet's scope to NumPy and back can destroy performance"*); [[AutoParallelism|automatic parallelism]] across devices ($\max(t_0,t_1)$ not $t_0+t_1$ when the barrier is removed) + computation–communication overlap (`non_blocking=True` PCIe DMA in flight during backprop); the [[hardware]] + [[MemoryHierarchy|latency-table]] chapter (registers ≤1 cycle / L1 1.5 ns / L2 5 ns / L3 16 ns / DRAM 46 ns / GPU [[HBM]] 200 ns / 1 MB over [[NVLink]] 30 µs / 1 MB over [[PCIe]] 80 µs / 4 KB over 10 GbE 10 µs / cross-DC RTT 500 µs / CA↔NL 150 ms) — *"first DRAM read is 500× as expensive as subsequent burst reads"*, *"aim for a small number of large transfers rather than many small ones"*; CPU [[Vectorization|SIMD]] (NEON 128 / AVX2 256 / AVX-512 512-bit) and the GPU answer in [[TensorCore|tensor cores]] (specialized $4{\times}4$–$16{\times}16$ FMA per clock at FP16/BF16/FP8/FP4); interconnects ([[NVLink]] 100 GB/s aggregate per V100 > [[PCIe]] 4.0 ×16 32 GB/s > 100 GbE 10 GB/s > 1 GbE 100 MB/s); training-vs-inference accelerator distinction (T4 inference / V100 training); the three ways to parallelize ([[ModelParallelism|network partition]] / [[TensorParallelism|layerwise split]] — historical [[AlexNet]] on dual GTX 580 @ 2 GB / [[DataParallelism|data parallelism]]) — D2L's verdict: **"data parallelism is the most convenient way to proceed"** with full-model replicas + minibatch sharding + [[AllReduce]] + $k$-fold minibatch and LR scaling; from-scratch implementation of [[AllReduce]] on a custom LeNet + concise `nn.DataParallel(net, device_ids=devices)` on a ResNet-18 variant; [[ParameterServer|parameter server]] (Smola & Narayanamurthy 2010; Li, Andersen, Park et al. 2014) reframed as a [[KeyValueStore|key–value store]] with `push(key,value)` (accumulate) / `pull(key,value)` (retrieve) over a *commutative reduction* — sharding parameters across $n$ servers gives $\mathcal{O}(m/n)$ scaling, $m=n$ → constant; [[RingAllReduce|ring synchronization]]'s astonishing $O(1)$-in-$n$ result (160 MB across 8× V100 over NVLink in ~6 ms via two passes along the ring — better than PCIe even at 8 GPUs); [[NCCL]] / [[Horovod]] as production all-reduce backends with topology-aware path selection. The textbook foundation under [[DistributedTraining]] / [[DataParallelism]] / [[AllReduce]] / [[ParameterServer]] — and the chapter-level analogue of the [[2205.14135-flashattention]] / [[gpumemoryhierarchy]] within-GPU memory-aware-algorithm story, applied one tier higher at host↔device and inter-device scales); and [[d2l-nlp-pretraining]] (the 10-section *NLP Pretraining* chapter — D2L's pivot from architectures to **upstream text-representation pretraining**: [[Word2Vec|word2vec]] ([[SkipGram]] + [[CBOW]]) with [[NegativeSampling]] (NCE-style binary classification against $K$ noise words, $\mathcal{O}(K)$ per step) or [[HierarchicalSoftmax]] (binary tree, $\mathcal{O}(\log_2|\mathcal{V}|)$) replacing the $\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{V}|)$ softmax bottleneck → [[GloVe]] ([[JeffreyPennington|Pennington]], [[RichardSocher|Socher]] & [[ChrisManning|Manning]] 2014) as squared-loss factorization of the global co-occurrence matrix $\sum h(x_{ij})(\mathbf{u}_j^\top\mathbf{v}_i+b_i+c_j-\log x_{ij})^2$ justified by the ratio of co-occurrence probabilities → [[FastText]] ([[PiotrBojanowski|Bojanowski]] et al. 2017) as char-$n$-gram [[SubwordEmbedding]] $\mathbf{v}_w=\sum_g\mathbf{z}_g$ for OOV / morphology → [[BPE|Byte Pair Encoding]] ([[RicoSennrich|Sennrich]] et al. 2015) as the fixed-vocab variable-length subword tokenizer behind [[GPT2|GPT-2]] / [[RoBERTa]] → [[AnalogyTask]] recovering man:woman::son:daughter / beijing:china::tokyo:japan / bad:worst::big:biggest directly from 50-d GloVe → pivots from static / context-independent to [[ContextualEmbedding|context-sensitive]] embeddings ([[TagLM]] → CoVe → [[ELMo]] → [[GPT]] → [[BERT]]: "the best of both worlds" — bidirectional encoder + task-agnostic) → and re-builds the BERT pretraining pipeline ([[MaskedLanguageModel]] 15%-mask with 80/10/10 split + [[NextSentencePrediction]] binary classification on `[CLS]` + [[WordPiece]] 30K vocab + learnable positional embeddings) end-to-end on [[BookCorpus]] (800M words) + [[EnglishWikipedia]] (2.5B words) — the **3.3B-word self-supervised corpus** that defined the modern pretrain-then-finetune NLP paradigm. The textbook foundation under every modern LLM input layer; the canonical companion to [[1810.04805-bert]]); and [[d2l-computer-vision]] (the 14-section *Computer Vision* chapter — D2L's first **Part 3 application chapter** that leverages the [[CNN]] / [[ResNet]] / [[VGG]] backbones built in [[d2l-convolutional-neural-networks]] / [[d2l-convolutional-modern]] for the four canonical CV tasks beyond classification. [[DataAugmentation|Image augmentation]] (flip / crop / `ColorJitter` / `RandomResizedCrop` — "indispensable for [[AlexNet]]") + [[FineTuning]] (four-step ImageNet-pretrained → copy → replace-head → train-with-$10\times$-LR-on-head; ResNet-18 hot-dog binary classifier demo) → [[BoundingBox]] / [[AnchorBox|anchor boxes]] ($ws\sqrt{r}$, $hs/\sqrt{r}$ with the $n+m-1$ trick) / [[IntersectionOverUnion|IoU = Jaccard]] / [[NonMaxSuppression|NMS]] (greedy; Soft-NMS flagged as a softer alternative) / greedy anchor↔ground-truth matching with $\sigma_{xy}=0.1$, $\sigma_{wh}=0.2$ offset parameterization → [[MultiscaleObjectDetection|multiscale detection]] via CNN feature pyramids (smaller maps + larger receptive fields → larger objects) → [[SSD|Single-Shot Multibox Detection]] ([[WeiLiu|Liu]], Anguelov, Erhan et al. 2016) as the canonical single-stage detector (truncated VGG/ResNet base + multiscale feature blocks + per-pixel $a(q+1)$-channel class head + $4a$-channel bbox head, "simple, fast, and widely used") + the R-CNN two-stage lineage [[RCNN]] ([[RossGirshick|Girshick]], Donahue, Darrell & Malik 2014, [[SelectiveSearch|selective search]] → 2000 proposals → per-region CNN → per-class SVMs, ~10 s/image fatal cost) → [[FastRCNN]] (Girshick 2015, one whole-image CNN forward pass + [[ROIPooling|RoI pooling]] per proposal, end-to-end trainable, ~10× speedup) → [[FasterRCNN]] ([[ShaoqingRen|Ren]], [[KaimingHe|He]], Girshick et al. 2015, learned [[RegionProposalNetwork|Region Proposal Network]] replaces selective search, 5 FPS) → [[MaskRCNN]] ([[KaimingHe|He]], Gkioxari, Dollár & Girshick 2017, swap [[ROIPooling]]→[[ROIAlign]] with bilinear interpolation + per-RoI FCN mask head → [[InstanceSegmentation]]) → [[SemanticSegmentation]] vs [[InstanceSegmentation]] vs low-level image-segmentation distinction on [[PascalVOC2012]] (21 classes, fixed RGB colormap labels) → [[TransposedConvolution|transposed conv]] = fractionally-strided conv as the upsampling primitive that broadcasts via the kernel (for stride $s$, padding $s/2$, kernel $2s$ → exactly $s\times$ upsampling) with [[BilinearInterpolation|bilinear-interpolation]] kernel initialization → [[FCN|Fully Convolutional Network]] ([[JonathanLong|Long]], [[EvanShelhamer|Shelhamer]] & [[TrevorDarrell|Darrell]] 2015): ResNet-18 backbone (drop GAP+FC) + $1\times1$ conv to $K=21$ classes + stride-32 transposed conv → per-pixel softmax cross-entropy → [[StyleTransfer|Gatys neural style transfer]] ([[LeonGatys|Gatys]], [[AlexanderEcker|Ecker]] & [[MatthiasBethge|Bethge]] 2016) iteratively optimizes the *synthesized image itself* on a frozen [[VGG|VGG-19]] with three-component loss (content MSE at a deep layer + style MSE on [[GramMatrix|Gram matrices]] at multi-scale layers + total-variation noise penalty) → and two Kaggle capstones ([[CIFAR10]] image classification with `read_csv_labels` / `reorg_train_valid` directory-organization helpers + dog breed identification on a 120-breed ImageNet subset). The unifying theme: **layerwise / multiscale feature reuse on pretrained ImageNet CNN backbones** across every CV task — the paradigm that NLP would later mirror with [[BERT]] / [[GPT]] pretraining → fine-tuning); and [[d2l-nlp-applications]] (the 7-section *NLP Applications* chapter — D2L's **downstream** companion to [[d2l-nlp-pretraining]], applying [[GloVe]] / [[BERT]] to two representative tasks ([[SentimentAnalysis|sentiment]] on [[IMDb]], [[NaturalLanguageInference|NLI]] on [[SNLI]]) across four architectural choices for the same task: [[BidirectionalRNN]] + GloVe ([[d2l-nlp-applications]] §sentiment-rnn), [[TextCNN]] ([[YoonKim|Kim]] 2014, §sentiment-cnn) with [[MaxOverTimePooling]] + multi-width 1-D convs, the [[DecomposableAttention|decomposable attention]] model ([[AnkurParikh|Parikh]], Täckström, Das & Uszkoreit 2016, §nli-attention) — pre-Transformer **attention + MLPs, no recurrence/convolution**, with the linear-complexity *decomposition trick* $e_{ij}=f(\mathbf{a}_i)^\top f(\mathbf{b}_j)$ that prefigures [[SelfAttention]] — and fine-tuned [[BERT]] (§nli-bert). Closes with the [[FineTuningBert]] template — "minimal architecture changes" for sequence-level ([[SentimentAnalysis]] / [[CoLA]] single-text, [[NaturalLanguageInference|NLI]] / [[SemanticTextualSimilarity|STS]] text-pair) and token-level (POS tagging, [[QuestionAnswering|QA]] on [[SQuAD]]) applications, all sharing the [[ClsToken|`[CLS]`]] aggregate or per-token hidden states. Articulates the engineering trade-off ("when space or time is limited, those crafted models based on MLPs, CNNs, RNNs, and attention are more feasible") — BERT minimizes architecture but maximizes compute. The applied-NLP analogue of [[d2l-computer-vision]]: where the CV chapter established *pretrained CNN backbone + fine-tune* as the universal CV template, this chapter operationalizes the analogous *pretrained Transformer encoder + fine-tune* template at the downstream-task level, completing the **CV ↔ NLP symmetry** D2L has been building toward); and [[d2l-reinforcement-learning]] (the three-section *Reinforcement Learning* chapter — guest co-authored by [[PratikChaudhari|Chaudhari]] (Penn / [[Amazon]]), [[RasoolFakoor|Fakoor]] & [[KavoshAsadi|Asadi]] (Amazon) — D2L's compact RL introduction: [[MarkovDecisionProcess|MDP]] formalism $(\mathcal{S},\mathcal{A},T,r)$ with [[TransitionFunction|transition kernel]] / [[RewardFunction|reward]] / Markov property / state-expansion trick + [[DiscountFactor|$\gamma$ discount]] → [[PolicyFunction|policies]] / [[ValueFunction|value]] / [[ActionValueFunction|action-value]] functions, the [[BellmanEquation|Bellman]] one-step decomposition and [[RichardBellman|Bellman]]'s 1950s [[DynamicProgramming|DP]] principle ("the remainder of an optimal trajectory is also optimal"), [[ValueIteration|Value Iteration]] $V_{k+1}(s)=\max_a\{r(s,a)+\gamma\sum_{s'}P(s'\mid s,a)V_k(s')\}$ converging in ~10 sweeps on FrozenLake-v1 when the MDP is known → [[QLearning|Q-Learning]] ([[ChristopherWatkins|Watkins]] & Dayan 1992) as the [[ModelFreeLearning|model-free]] alternative — replaces the analytical expectation with a sampled [[TemporalDifferenceLearning|TD]] update, [[OffPolicyLearning|off-policy]] behavior-vs-target structure with [[EpsilonGreedy|$\epsilon$-greedy]] / softmax-temperature [[ExplorationExploitation|exploration]], self-correcting overestimation, ~250 episodes to converge on the same task — *"this happens because Value Iteration has access to the full MDP whereas Q-learning does not."* The chapter is RL distilled to first principles and the textbook foundation under [[reinforce|REINFORCE]] / [[grpo|GRPO]] / [[rlvr|RLVR]] / DQN — the modern LLM-era RL methods); and [[d2l-gaussian-processes]] ([[AndrewGordonWilson|Wilson]] ([[NewYorkUniversity|NYU]] / [[amazon|Amazon]])'s guest three-section chapter — D2L's only **function-space Bayesian** chapter: defines a [[GaussianProcess|GP]] as *"a collection of random variables, any finite number of which have a joint Gaussian distribution"*; prior specified by mean + [[KernelFunction|kernel]]; derives the [[RBFKernel|RBF kernel]] as the $J\to\infty$ Riemann-sum limit of $J$ Gaussian basis functions (a GP-with-RBF is a [[UniversalApproximator|universal approximator]] with a *finite-computation infinite-parameter* prior — Wilson's running counter-argument to "the fuss about overparametrized neural networks"); derives the [[NeuralNetworkKernel|NN kernel]] from a one-hidden-layer Bayesian NN at infinite width ([[RadfordNeal|Neal 1996]] — the historical precursor to [[NeuralTangentKernel|NTK]]); gives the **closed-form Gaussian posterior** $m_*=K(X_*,X)[K(X,X)+\sigma^2 I]^{-1}\mathbf{y}$ and $S_*=K(X_*,X_*)-K(X_*,X)[K(X,X)+\sigma^2 I]^{-1}K(X,X_*)$; introduces the [[MarginalLikelihood|marginal-likelihood]] objective $-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbf{y}^\top[K_\theta+\sigma^2 I]^{-1}\mathbf{y}-\tfrac{1}{2}\log|K_\theta+\sigma^2 I|+c$ that compartmentalizes into data-fit + complexity-penalty and **encodes [[OccamsRazor|Occam's razor]] automatically** — the single biggest ML contribution to GP research; clean operationalization of the [[EpistemicUncertainty|epistemic]] vs [[AleatoricUncertainty|aleatoric]] uncertainty split (*"without being precise about what the uncertainty represents, it is essentially meaningless"*); flags the $\mathcal{O}(n^3)$ [[CholeskyDecomposition|Cholesky]] / log-det bottleneck (~10k training points without scalable inference; "a reputation for 'being slow' that has been inaccurate now for almost a decade") and introduces [[GPyTorch]] ([[JacobGardner|Gardner]] et al. 2018) as the [[PyTorch]]-integrated production library (SKI / KISS-GP scaling to millions of points; full-batch [[Adam]] / L-BFGS since the marginal likelihood does not factorize over data). Wilson's chapter-level thesis: *"GPs and deep neural networks are not competing approaches, but highly complementary"* — closing the loop between [[d2l-multilayer-perceptrons]]'s [[NeuralTangentKernel|NTK]] paragraph and [[d2l-attention-and-transformers]]'s [[NadarayaWatson|Nadaraya–Watson]] kernel-regression precursor for attention); and [[d2l-hyperparameter-optimization]] ([[AaronKlein|Klein]], [[MatthiasSeeger|Seeger]] & [[CedricArchambeau|Archambeau]] ([[Amazon]])'s five-section guest chapter — D2L's only chapter cast entirely as **[[BlackBoxOptimization|black-box optimization]]** of the [[HyperparameterOptimization|HPO]] objective $f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{R}$ with no usable gradient, noisy evaluations, expensive function calls, and structured / conditional config spaces. Builds the searcher / scheduler / tuner API ([[d2l-hyperparameter-optimization]] §hyperopt-api — the same decomposition implemented by [[SyneTune]], [[RayTune]] and [[Optuna]]); implements [[RandomSearch]] from a SciPy `loguniform` config space and grounds it in the [[JamesBergstra|Bergstra]] & [[YoshuaBengio|Bengio]] 2012 *random-beats-grid* result; benchmarks it against [[BayesianOptimization]] ([[JasperSnoek|Snoek]] et al. 2012 — BO matches random search ≤1000 s, dominates afterwards via surrogate-model exploitation); distributes random search asynchronously via [[SyneTune]] § rs-async (linear $K$-worker speed-up because each config is independent of past observations — *unlike* model-based methods which need synchronization); introduces [[MultiFidelityOptimization|multi-fidelity HPO]] with the objective $f(\mathbf{x}, r)$, $r$ a resource budget (epochs / subset size / CV folds) and the assumption that low-$r$ rankings predict high-$r$ rankings; implements [[SuccessiveHalving]] ([[Jamieson|Jamieson & Talwalkar 2016]] / [[Karnin|Karnin et al. 2013]]) — $N=\eta^K$ configs at $r_\text{min}$, keep top $1/\eta$ at each rung, only one reaches $r_\text{max}$ — flagging the synchronization-barrier idle-worker pathology of synchronous SH on heterogeneous workloads; and finally pivots to **[[ASHA|asynchronous successive halving]]** ([[LishaLi|Li]] et al. 2018) — *"promote the moment $\eta$ observations land on a rung"* — the algorithm that eliminates SH's barrier at the cost of mildly-suboptimal early promotions, ubiquitous in production HPO ([[SyneTune]] / [[RayTune]] / [[Optuna]]). Closes by positioning [[NeuralArchitectureSearch|NAS]] and [[AutoML]] as natural HPO extensions. The chapter is the **non-learnable-parameter complement to [[d2l-optimization]]** — where the optimization chapter handles SGD/Adam search over learnable weights, this one handles black-box / multi-fidelity search over the hyperparameters that those optimizers themselves require); and [[d2l-generative-adversarial-networks]] (the two-section *Generative Adversarial Networks* chapter — D2L's **first dedicated generative-modeling chapter**, pivoting from discriminative learning into the regime where training's output is a *sampler*, not a classifier. §`gan` builds the [[generativeadversarialnetwork|GAN]] from first principles on a 2-D Gaussian toy: [[Generator|generator]] $G$ maps noise $\mathbf{z}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)$ to fakes; [[Discriminator|discriminator]] $D$ is a binary BCE classifier; the two play a [[MinMaxGame|minimax game]] $\min_D\max_G\{-E_x\log D(\mathbf{x})-E_z\log(1-D(G(\mathbf{z})))\}$ from [[IanGoodfellow|Goodfellow]], Pouget-Abadie, Mirza et al. 2014; the non-saturating $\min_G\{-\log D(G(\mathbf{z}))\}$ reformulation feeds fakes through $D$ with the *real* label $y=1$ to fix the vanishing-gradient pathology of the original $\max_G$ objective when $D(G(\mathbf{z}))$ is small early in training. §`dcgan` scales the scaffold to RGB-image synthesis on a Pokémon-sprite dataset via [[DCGAN]] ([[AlecRadford|Radford]], Metz & Chintala 2015): all-convolutional discriminator (4 strided convs + [[BatchNormalization|BN]] + [[LeakyReLU]] $\alpha=0.2$ — fixes the "dying ReLU" pathology that would otherwise cripple $D$ early in training) and mirror-image generator (4 [[TransposedConvolution|transposed convs]] + BN + [[ReLU]] inflating $1{\times}1{\times}100$ noise through $4{\times}4 \to 8{\times}8 \to 16{\times}16 \to 32{\times}32 \to 64{\times}64$ with channel widths $100\!\to\!512\!\to\!256\!\to\!128\!\to\!64\!\to\!3$ and a final $\tanh$ landing in $[-1,1]$). Practical recipe: same LR for both nets, weights init $\mathcal{N}(0, 0.02)$, **Adam $\beta_1 = 0.5$** (vs default $0.9$) to track the fast-changing adversarial gradients — the single most-cited hyperparameter tweak in the GAN literature. The chapter is operationally minimal but conceptually load-bearing: it grounds the [[generativeadversarialnetwork|GAN]] concept page (previously cited only at the framing level via [[d2l-introduction]]), introduces the [[TransposedConvolution]]-as-upsampling-primitive role beyond [[FCN]] (cf. [[d2l-computer-vision]]), and sets up the [[DiffusionModel|diffusion]] successor story D2L's Introduction already flagged — diffusion models have "started to replace GANs in production systems like DALL-E 2 and Imagen"); and [[d2l-recommender-systems]] ([[ShuaiZhang|Zhang]] ([[Amazon]]), [[AstonZhang|Zhang]] ([[Amazon]]) & [[YiTay|Tay]] ([[google|Google]])'s ten-section *Recommender Systems* chapter — D2L's only chapter on **personalized retrieval / ranking** as a learning problem distinct from classification and regression. Builds the recommender stack on [[MovieLens]] 100K (943 users × 1682 movies, 93.695% sparse [[InteractionMatrix|interaction matrix]]): [[CollaborativeFiltering|CF]] taxonomy ([[MemoryBasedCF|memory-based]] / [[ModelBasedCF|model-based]] / hybrid; [[ImplicitFeedback]] vs [[ExplicitFeedback]] per [[YifanHu|Hu]] / [[YehudaKoren|Koren]] / Volinsky 2008) → rating-prediction with [[MatrixFactorization]] ([[YehudaKoren|Koren]], Bell & Volinsky 2009; Funk 2006, [[NetflixPrize]] — \$1M won by BellKor's Pragmatic Chaos with MF as load-bearing component) and [[AutoRec]] ([[SuvashSedhain|Sedhain]] et al. 2015 — autoencoder reformulation of MF with gradient masking on unobserved entries, beating linear MF on MovieLens) → personalized ranking with [[BPR]] ([[SteffenRendle|Rendle]] et al. 2009, *UAI* — pairwise log-sigmoid $\textrm{BPR-OPT}=-\sum\ln\sigma(\hat{y}_{ui}-\hat{y}_{uj})+\lambda\|\Theta\|^2$ derived from MAP) and interchangeable [[HingeLossRanking|Hinge]] loss → [[NeuMF]] ([[XiangnanHe|He]] et al. 2017, *WWW*) fusing [[GMF]] (Hadamard-product + dense head — generalizes MF) and an MLP branch with non-shared embeddings, trained with BPR on implicit feedback, evaluated by [[HitRate|Hit@k]] / [[AUC]] not [[RMSE]] → [[CaserModel|Caser]] sequence-aware CNN ([[JiaxiTang|Tang]] & [[KeWang|Wang]] 2018, *WSDM*) treating the last $L$-item embedding matrix as an "image" + horizontal convolutions (union-level patterns: "milk + butter → flour") + vertical convolutions (point-level patterns) + a separate long-term-taste embedding ([[SequenceAwareRecommendation|Quadrana, Cremonesi & Jannach 2018]] survey context) → **feature-rich CTR** pivot to [[FactorizationMachines]] ([[SteffenRendle|Rendle]] 2010, *ICDM*) — general-purpose model unifying linear regression, MF, and polynomial-kernel SVMs, with the celebrated **$\mathcal{O}(kd)$ pairwise-interaction reformulation** $\frac{1}{2}\sum_l((\sum v_{i,l}x_i)^2 - \sum v_{i,l}^2 x_i^2)$ making FM tractable on sparse high-cardinality categorical CTR inputs — and [[DeepFM]] ([[HuifengGuo|Guo]], [[RuimingTang|Tang]], Ye & He 2017, *IJCAI*) parallelizing FM with a deep MLP component sharing the same embedding table, capturing both low- and high-order interactions while eliminating [[WideAndDeep|Wide & Deep]]'s manual feature-crossing. The textbook foundation under [[CTRPrediction|every monetized digital surface]] (search ads / display ads / social-feed ads / app-store ads), every modern e-commerce ranker, and every Netflix/Spotify/YouTube personalization layer — and the **third Part 3 application chapter** completing the [[ComputerVision]] / [[NLP]] / **personalization** triad on top of the [[d2l-attention-and-transformers|Transformer]]-era foundation built in Part 2).

The Introduction chapter is where D2L *substantively* opens — defining [[MachineLearning|machine learning]], surveying the four core components (data / model / objective / optimizer) and four paradigms ([[SupervisedLearning|supervised]] including [[Regression]], [[Classification]], tagging, [[RecommenderSystems|recommender]]; [[UnsupervisedLearning|unsupervised]]; [[SelfSupervisedLearning|self-supervised]]; [[reinforcementlearning|RL]] with [[MarkovDecisionProcess|MDP]] / contextual-bandit / [[MultiArmedBandits|MAB]] cases), tracing the **historical roots** (Bernoulli / Gauss / Fisher / Shannon / Turing / Hebb / Rosenblatt → 1995–2005 NN winter where [[KernelMethods|kernel methods]] dominated → [[ImageNet]]-driven revival in the 2010s), and defining [[DeepLearning|deep learning]] as multi-level [[RepresentationLearning|representation learning]] with [[EndToEndTraining|end-to-end training]] that replaces handcrafted [[FeatureEngineering|feature engineering]] (Canny / [[SIFT]]). The chapter's historical lens is wider than most ML textbooks (Bain 1873, Sherrington 1890, Köbel 1535 trimmed-mean foot estimation) and explicitly flags ethical-history caveats (Fisher's eugenicism).

**Why this corpus matters now.** D2L's strength relative to the other corpora is its **pedagogical pacing** — it interleaves theory and runnable PyTorch / JAX code, where [[mml-book|MML]] is math-first, [[pml1-murphy|Murphy]] is encyclopedic, and [[islr-seventh-printing|ISLR]] uses R-labs. D2L is the **applied-deep-learning textbook layer** that complements them. With [[d2l-attention-and-transformers]], [[d2l-optimization]], [[d2l-computational-performance]], and now [[d2l-computer-vision]] ingested, the *MLP → CNN → RNN → gated RNN + seq2seq → attention + Transformer + pretrained foundation models + ViT* span of Part 2 is **complete**, the optimization + systems-and-scaling layer beneath it — the "how to actually train these" half — is **also complete**, and **Part 3 application chapters are now opening up**: the [[ComputerVision]] application layer is in place — every modern CV pipeline (detection / segmentation / style transfer / classification) reduces to a pretrained-ImageNet-CNN-backbone + [[FineTuning]] + task-specific head, exactly the recipe D2L's CV chapter walks through end-to-end. Every 2026 LLM training run reduces to (a) some [[transformer|Transformer]] variant, (b) [[Adam|Adam-family]] optimization on [[MinibatchSGD|minibatch SGD]] with a [[CosineLRSchedule|cosine-warmup]] schedule, and (c) [[DataParallelism|data-parallel]] [[AllReduce|all-reduce]] (often [[RingAllReduce|ring-style]] via [[NCCL]]) on [[NVLink]]-connected GPUs with [[HBM]] memory — and D2L now covers all three, plus the [[ComputerVision|CV]] application layer that demonstrates the **layerwise feature reuse** template the same way [[BERT]] / [[GPT]] would later use it for NLP. With [[d2l-nlp-pretraining]] now ingested, **Part 3's NLP-pretraining backbone is also in place** — [[Word2Vec]] / [[GloVe]] / [[FastText]] for static [[WordEmbedding|word embeddings]], [[BPE]] / [[WordPiece]] for subword tokenization, and the full [[BERT]] [[MaskedLanguageModel|MLM]] + [[NextSentencePrediction|NSP]] pretraining recipe — completing the **[[NLP]] half of the symmetry**: where [[d2l-computer-vision]] established *pretrained CNN backbone + fine-tune* as the universal CV template, [[d2l-nlp-pretraining]] establishes the analogous *pretrained Transformer encoder + fine-tune* template that every modern decoder LLM (GPT / Claude / Gemini / Llama) inherits at its input layer. D2L is now the corpus that bridges Corpus II (LLM research) into a deployable curriculum.

**Connections to other corpora.**
- **Corpus II (LLM research):** D2L's [[scalinglaws|scaling]] paragraph cites [[2001.08361-scaling-laws|Kaplan et al.]] directly; its Transformer paragraph cites [[1706.03762-attention-is-all-you-need|Vaswani et al.]] and ChatGPT; its self-supervised section cites [[1810.04805-bert|BERT]]-style MLM.
- **Corpus VI–VIII (Murphy / ISLR / MML):** D2L's four-components framing is consonant with Murphy's [[EmpiricalRiskMinimization|ERM]] view and ISLR's prediction / inference distinction; MML supplies the math beneath every D2L derivation.
- **Corpus V (Made With ML):** D2L is *also* runnable code, but emphasizes architectures over MLOps; Corpus V supplies the productionization layer.

**Live tensions.**
- *Mild* tension with [[2605.12966-agentic-ai-to-agi]]'s [[AverageTrap]] critique — D2L's Introduction implicitly endorses monolithic supervised learning at scale. Same neutrality as Murphy / MML / ISLR.
- The chapter dates Hebbian learning to 1949 and biologically-inspired neural networks to Bain (1873) / Sherrington (1890), a wider historical lens than most ML textbooks. No internal conflict; flagged on [[d2l-introduction]].

## X. *The Embedded Rust Book* ([[RustEmbeddedWorkingGroup]] — **complete, 44/44 chapters**)

> 2026-05-16 closing addendum: [[rust-embedded-book-appendix-glossary]] now ingested — **Appendix A: Glossary** and the **44th and final file** of *[[TheEmbeddedRustBook]]*. Pure cross-walk: one-paragraph definitions of the vendor-acronym alphabet the preceding 43 chapters use freely — **[[BSP]]** (Board Support Crate, depends on a [[HALCrate|HAL]]), **[[FPU]]** (Floating-Point Unit), **[[HALCrate|HAL]]** (Hardware Abstraction Layer, sits on top of a [[PeripheralAccessCrate|PAC]], may impl [[EmbeddedHalCrate|`embedded-hal`]] traits), **[[I2C]]** (Inter-IC), **[[PeripheralAccessCrate|PAC]]** (generated from an [[SVDFile|SVD]] via [[Svd2Rust|`svd2rust`]]), **[[SPI]]** (Serial Peripheral Interface), **[[SVDFile|SVD]]** (System View Description XML), **[[UART]]** (asynchronous-only [[USART]] subset), **[[USART]]** (Universal Synchronous/Asynchronous Receiver-Transmitter). Introduces 3 new concept pages — [[BSP]] / [[FPU]] / [[UART]] — every other glossary term already had a page from prior chapters. **Closes the Embedded Rust corpus at 44/44.**

*The Embedded Rust Book* is the wiki's **first systems-programming / [[EmbeddedSystems|embedded-systems]]** corpus — fully orthogonal to the nine ML/data-science corpora above. It teaches [[RustLanguage|Rust]] for [[BareMetalProgramming|bare-metal]] [[Microcontroller|microcontroller]] development on [[ARMCortexM|ARM Cortex-M]] via the [[STM32F3DISCOVERY]] dev board, coordinated by the [[RustEmbeddedWorkingGroup]]'s resources team. **All 44 chapters ingested** between 2026-05-16 (file 1, [[rust-embedded-book-intro-index|Introduction]]) and 2026-05-16 (this file, [[rust-embedded-book-appendix-glossary|Appendix A: Glossary]]).

**Corpus map (the 11-part outline plus the closing appendix).**

| Part | Files | Anchor concepts introduced |
|---|---|---|
| **Introduction** (intro/) | 9 files (1–9) | [[NoStd]], [[BareMetalProgramming]], [[HostedEnvironment]], [[RustCoreLibrary]] vs [[RustStandardLibrary]], [[HeapAllocation]], [[RustRuntime]], [[CrossCompilation]], [[CargoBinutils]], [[CargoGenerate]], [[OnChipDebugging]], [[JTAG]] / [[SWD]], [[RustTarget]], [[UdevRules]] |
| **Getting Started** (start/) | 8 files (10–17) | [[CortexMQuickstartTemplate]], [[ARMSemihosting]], [[VectorTable]], [[LinkerScript]], [[OpenOCDConfig]], [[GDBLoad]], the four-layer crate stack ([[MicroArchitectureCrate]] → [[PeripheralAccessCrate]] → [[HALCrate]] → [[BoardCrate]]), [[SVDFile]], [[TypeStateProgramming]], [[SysTick]], [[PanicHandlerAttribute]], [[PanicInfo]], [[ExceptionAttribute]], [[DefaultHandler]], [[HardFault]], [[ExceptionFrame]], [[NVIC]], [[InterruptAttribute]], [[InterruptPriority]] |
| **Peripherals** (peripherals/) | 4 files (18–21) | [[Peripheral]], [[VolatileMemoryAccess]], [[RawPointer]], [[ReprC]], [[BorrowChecker]], [[Singleton]], [[PeripheralsTake]] |
| **Static Guarantees** (static-guarantees/) | 5 files (22–26) | [[StaticGuarantee]], [[BuilderPattern]], [[FiniteStateMachine]], [[DesignByContract]], [[ZeroCostAbstraction]], [[ZeroSizedType]] |
| **Portability** (portability/) | 1 file (27) | [[Portability]], [[DriverCrate]], [[HardwareAbstractionLayer]] — resolves the long-standing forward references to [[EmbeddedHalCrate|`embedded-hal`]] |
| **Concurrency** (concurrency/) | 1 file (28) | [[Send]], [[Sync]], [[CriticalSection]], [[Atomic]], [[Mutex]], [[CellRust]], [[RefCell]], [[UnsafeCell]], [[InteriorMutability]], [[DataRace]], [[Deadlock]], plus the [[RTIC]] / [[Embassy]] / [[BareMetalCrate]] framework triad |
| **Collections** (collections/) | 1 file (29) | [[GlobalAllocator]], [[GlobalAlloc]], [[OutOfMemory]], [[AllocErrorHandlerAttribute]], [[BumpPointerAllocator]], [[FixedCapacityCollection]], [[MemoryFragmentation]], [[WorstCaseExecutionTime]], [[AllocCrate]] vs [[HeaplessCrate]] |
| **Design Patterns** (design-patterns/) | 7 files (30–36) | The HAL-design-pattern alphabet (`C-CRATE-NAME` / `C-FREE` / `C-REEXPORT-PAC` / `C-HAL-TRAITS` / `C-CTOR` / `C-INLINE` / `C-ZST-PIN` / `C-ERASED-PIN` / `C-PIN-STATE`); [[ErasedPin]], [[PinTypeState]] |
| **Tips for embedded C developers** (c-tips/) | 1 file (37) | [[CargoFeatures]] (the `#ifdef` analog), [[RustMacro]] (the `#define` analog) |
| **Interoperability** (interoperability/) | 3 files (38–40) | [[BuildRs]], [[ExternC]], [[NoMangle]], [[CrateType]]; tool entities [[Bindgen]] / [[Cbindgen]] / [[CcCrate]] |
