| **Unsorted topics** (unsorted/) | 3 files (41–43) | [[OptLevel]] (`opt-level` regime table + `profile-overrides` recipe); tool entity [[LibmCrate]] |
| **Appendix A: Glossary** (appendix/) | 1 file (**44**) | [[BSP]], [[FPU]], [[UART]] — pure cross-walk into the glossary alphabet |

**Total contribution to the wiki.** 44 source pages; the [[TheEmbeddedRustBook|book entity]] page + [[RustEmbeddedWorkingGroup]] + [[EmbeddedHalCrate]] entities + tool entities ([[Bindgen]], [[Cbindgen]], [[CcCrate]], [[LibmCrate]]); ~90 net new concept pages across the embedded-Rust crate stack, peripheral / interrupt / FFI / build / concurrency / collections / design-pattern / optimization vocabularies. Most arrive concentrated in the Getting Started + Static Guarantees + Concurrency + Collections clusters; the appendix's three contributions ([[BSP]], [[FPU]], [[UART]]) are the **acronym-disambiguator closure** of the corpus — every term the book uses now has an explicit wiki anchor.

**Why this corpus matters in this wiki.** This is the wiki's first **non-ML** corpus. It demonstrates that the `[[wikilink]]` graph + source-page + concept-page architecture generalizes to *any* technical domain with terminology, layered dependencies, and design tradeoffs. The Rust-specific patterns ([[TypeStateProgramming]], [[ZeroCostAbstraction]], [[Singleton]]) and the hardware-specific patterns ([[MemoryMappedIO]], [[VolatileMemoryAccess]], [[Interrupt]], [[NVIC]], [[ExceptionFrame]]) are now first-class citizens of the wiki and reachable from anywhere via the standard query workflow.

**Connections to other corpora.** Largely orthogonal — but a few sympathetic resonances: [[TypeStateProgramming]] and [[ZeroCostAbstraction]] echo the *correctness-by-construction* impulse the ML corpora pursue via [[biconditionalcorrectness]] ([[2605.00424-skills-as-verifiable-artifacts]]); the [[Microcontroller|MCU]] hardware regime is the smaller-scale sibling of the [[GPU]] memory-hierarchy story in [[d2l-computational-performance]] / [[gpumemoryhierarchy]] / [[2205.14135-flashattention]]; [[NoStd|`#![no_std]`]] crates that compile to a [[Microcontroller|MCU]] are the workload-engineering analog of quantized / distilled models targeting edge inference.

---

## XI. *Embedded Controllers Using C and Arduino* ([[JamesFiore]] — single 166-page book, ingested 2026-05-17)

The wiki's **second embedded-systems corpus** and the load-bearing **counter-example** to the Rust-first pedagogy of corpus X. Fiore's [[embedded-controllers-fiore|*Embedded Controllers Using C and Arduino*]] (version 2.1.11, 15 Oct 2024, CC BY-NC-SA — see [[OER]]) is the textbook for a one-semester embedded-controllers course in [[MohawkValleyCommunityCollege|MVCC]]'s AAS Electrical Engineering Technology program. Two halves:

- **Chs. 1–14: [[CLanguage|C language]] tutorial** for students arriving from Python / BASIC / Pascal — memory model, types, functions, libraries, [[BitwiseOperations|bitwise ops]], `#define`, pointers, arrays, structures. The last four chapters (linked lists, malloc/free, file I/O, command-line args) are marked optional because real small-MCU code rarely needs them.
- **Chs. 15–29: "Bits & Pieces"** — embedded chapters that drop down to the [[ATmega328P]] [[AVR]] microcontroller on the [[ArduinoUno|Arduino Uno]] and read the [[Arduino]] runtime library source code chapter by chapter (`pinMode`, `digitalWrite`, `digitalRead`, `analogRead`, `analogReference`, `analogWrite`, `delay`, `ISR(...)`). Each chapter opens with the library's public docs, dissects the implementation in [[wiring_digital_c|`wiring_digital.c`]] / [[wiring_analog_c|`wiring_analog.c`]], then shows the equivalent direct-register C (`PORTB |= 0x01;`, `DDRB |= 0x3F;`, `sbi(ADCSRA, ADSC);`) that runs faster and works on any AVR — not just an Arduino board.

**Anchor concepts introduced.** [[Arduino]] / [[ArduinoUno]] / [[ATmega328P]] / [[AVR]] / [[Atmel]] / [[ArduinoCC]] / [[JamesFiore]] / [[MohawkValleyCommunityCollege]]; the [[HarvardArchitecture|Harvard]]-vs-[[VonNeumannArchitecture|Von-Neumann]] and [[RISC|RISC]]-vs-[[CISC]] taxonomies; the [[DataDirectionRegister|DDR]] / [[PortRegister|PORT]] / [[PinRegister|PIN]] register triple that underlies AVR [[GPIO]]; [[ADC|10-bit ADC]] with `ADCSRA` / `ADMUX` programming; [[PulseWidthModulation|PWM]] via three [[TimerCounter|timer/counters]]; [[InterruptServiceRoutine|ISR]] mechanics with the 26-vector table and the `ISR(VECTOR_vect)` macro; [[StatusRegister|SREG]]'s `I T H S V N Z C` bits and the manual-save-on-ISR discipline; [[CrossCompiler|cross-compilation]] as the embedded toolchain shape; [[FlashMemory]] / [[SRAM]] / [[EEPROM]] memory-tier comparison; [[BitwiseOperations|bitwise idioms]] and the Arduino `bitSet` / `bitClear` macro family; [[PullUpResistor]] mechanics and the pin-13-LED caveat; [[OER]] as the publishing model.

**The tension with corpus X.** Same hardware abstraction at the bottom (GPIO pin = DDR-bit + PORT-bit + PIN-bit + pull-up MOSFET + Schmitt trigger; ADC = successive-approximation 10-bit + multiplexer; timer = up-counter + compare-match + waveform gen). **Opposite design philosophy at the top:**

| Axis | [[TheEmbeddedRustBook]] (Rust on Cortex-M) | [[embedded-controllers-fiore]] (C on AVR) |
|---|---|---|
| Substrate | 32-bit [[ARMCortexM\|Cortex-M4]] / [[STM32F303VCT6]] / 256 k Flash / 48 k SRAM | 8-bit [[AVR]] / [[ATmega328P]] / 32 k Flash / 2 k SRAM |
| Language | [[RustLanguage\|Rust]] with `#![no_std]` | [[CLanguage\|C]] |
| Abstraction layer | [[HALCrate\|HAL]] over [[PeripheralAccessCrate\|PAC]] over [[MicroArchitectureCrate\|µarch]] crate | Arduino Wiring library; bypass via direct register writes when needed |
| Safety stance | [[TypeStateProgramming\|Typestate]] + [[StaticGuarantee\|static guarantees]] + [[ZeroCostAbstraction\|zero-cost abstractions]] — misuse becomes a compile-time type error | Hardware understanding first; safety comes from knowing the registers, not from the type system |
| Build system | [[Cargo]] + [[Rustc]] + linker scripts | Arduino IDE wrapping `avr-gcc` + `avrdude` |
| Concurrency | [[Send]] + [[Sync]] + [[Mutex]] + [[CriticalSection]] + [[RTIC]] | `volatile` + `sei()` / `cli()` brackets |
| Audience | Production embedded engineers writing portable safe firmware | First-time embedded undergraduates learning what a register is |

Neither corpus is wrong. The Rust book argues that production firmware needs the type-system payoff because mistakes are common and expensive; Fiore argues that you cannot use the type-system payoff without first understanding what's underneath, and the OER-textbook audience hasn't paid for that intuition yet. A student going from this corpus to corpus X gets the safety machinery added to their existing hardware mental model; a student going the other direction gets the hardware intuition that Rust's typestate API otherwise hides. **The wiki should serve both trajectories.**

---

## XII. *Introduction to Information Retrieval* ([[ChristopherManning]], [[PrabhakarRaghavan]] & [[HinrichSchutze]] — Cambridge University Press 2008, ingested 2026-05-23)

The wiki's **first classical-IR textbook** — the canonical Stanford IR course-text whose HTML edition lives at <https://nlp.stanford.edu/IR-book/>. All 21 chapters ingested in a single fan-out (one opus 4.7 subagent per chapter, fetching the chapter's top-level page plus every subsection in parallel). Source pages live at `wiki/sources/iir-ch01-…` through `wiki/sources/iir-ch21-…` (~60K words total). `source_file:` in each frontmatter points at the public URL — the book HTML is not checked into `raw/`.

**Pedagogical arc — pre-neural IR end-to-end.** The book is the canonical pre-LLM map of search:

| Phase | Chapters | Core technique introduced |
|---|---|---|
| **Indexing primitives** | 1–5 | [[BooleanRetrieval]] → [[InvertedIndex]] → [[Tokenization]] / [[Stemming]] / [[SkipPointer]] / [[PositionalIndex]] → BSBI/SPIMI/MapReduce construction → Heaps/Zipf statistics + γ/VByte compression |
| **Scoring & ranking** | 6–9 | [[TfIdf]] + [[VectorSpaceModel]] + cosine similarity + COSINESCORE → efficient top-K with champion lists / cluster pruning / tiered indexes → MAP/NDCG/kappa evaluation → [[RocchioAlgorithm]] feedback + query expansion |
| **Models beyond VSM** | 10–12 | XML structured retrieval + INEX → Probability Ranking Principle + Binary Independence Model + Okapi [[BM25]] → 2008-era statistical [[QueryLikelihoodModel]] LMs with JM/Dirichlet smoothing |
| **Learning on documents** | 13–15 | [[MultinomialNaiveBayes]] / [[BernoulliNaiveBayes]] + [[MutualInformation]] / chi-square feature selection → [[RocchioClassification]] / [[KNearestNeighbor]] / bias-variance → [[SupportVectorMachine]] + soft margin + kernels + [[RankingSVM]] + learning-to-rank |
| **Latent structure + clustering** | 16–18 | K-means + EM model-based clustering → HAC with single/complete/group-average/centroid linkage → [[SingularValueDecomposition]] + truncated SVD + [[LatentSemanticIndexing]] (Eckart-Young) |
| **Web-scale IR** | 19–21 | Bow-tie web graph + adversarial spam + [[Shingling]]/[[MinHash]] near-duplicate detection → Mercator-style crawlers + URL frontier + term-vs-document-partitioned distributed indexes → [[PageRank]] (teleporting Markov chain) + topic-sensitive PR + Kleinberg's [[HITS]] |

**Anchor concepts introduced.** ~120 new IR-specific concept wikilinks land across the chapters — many still unresolved, to be stubbed in a follow-up consolidation pass. High-value targets to create first: [[VectorSpaceModel]], [[TfIdf]], [[PageRank]], [[HITS]], [[OkapiBM25]], [[LatentSemanticIndexing]], [[Shingling]], [[MinHash]], [[BinaryIndependenceModel]], [[QueryLikelihoodModel]], [[RocchioAlgorithm]], [[Permuterm]], [[KGramIndex]], [[BSBI]], [[SPIMI]], [[HeapsLaw]], [[FrontCoding]], [[GammaCode]], [[VariableByteCode]], [[ChampionLists]], [[ClusterPruning]], [[TieredIndex]], [[NDCG]], [[MeanAveragePrecision]], [[CranfieldParadigm]], [[PseudoRelevanceFeedback]], [[QueryExpansion]], [[XMLRetrieval]], [[XPath]], [[INEX]], [[NEXI]], [[JelinekMercerSmoothing]], [[DirichletSmoothing]], [[Multinomial]], [[LaplaceSmoothing]], [[FeatureSelection]], [[MicroAveraging]], [[MacroAveraging]], [[VoronoiTessellation]], [[BiasVarianceTradeoff]], [[KernelTrick]], [[RBFKernel]], [[LearningToRank]], [[ClusterHypothesis]], [[EMAlgorithm]], [[Purity]], [[NormalizedMutualInformation]], [[RandIndex]], [[HAC]], [[SingleLinkClustering]], [[CompleteLinkClustering]], [[GroupAverageClustering]], [[EckartYoungTheorem]], [[FrobeniusNorm]], [[Synonymy]], [[Polysemy]], [[WebGraph]], [[BowTie]], [[WebSpam]], [[QueryIntent]], [[NearDuplicateDetection]], [[WebCrawler]], [[URLFrontier]], [[RobotsExclusionProtocol]], [[Mercator]], [[ConnectivityServer]], [[TermPartitioning]], [[DocumentPartitioning]], [[MarkovChain]], [[StationaryDistribution]], [[Teleportation]], [[PowerIteration]], [[AnchorText]], [[Ergodicity]].

**Anchor entities introduced.** The "founding fathers" of IR — [[ChristopherManning]] / [[PrabhakarRaghavan]] / [[HinrichSchutze]] (authors) + [[GerardSalton]] (vector space model, SMART) + [[MartinPorter]] (Porter stemmer) + [[VannevarBush]] / [[CalvinMooers]] / [[HansPeterLuhn]] (proto-IR pioneers) + [[StephenRobertson]] / [[KarenSparckJones]] (Probability Ranking Principle, BM25, idf) + [[JayPonte]] / [[BruceCroft]] (statistical LMs in IR) + [[ScottDeerwester]] / [[SusanDumais]] (LSI) + [[VladimirVapnik]] / [[ThorstenJoachims]] (SVMs, structural / RankingSVM) + [[AndreiBroder]] (bow-tie web graph) + [[LarryPage]] / [[SergeyBrin]] (PageRank) + [[JonKleinberg]] (HITS) + [[ThomasBayes]]. Corporate / dataset entities: [[CambridgeUniversityPress]], [[StanfordNLPGroup]], [[ReutersRCV1]], [[Reuters21578]], [[ShakespeareCorpus]], [[Westlaw]], [[W3C]], [[INEX]], [[Yahoo]], [[Altavista]], [[Heritrix]], [[InternetArchive]], [[TRECCollection]].

**Why this corpus matters in this wiki.** Up to now the wiki's retrieval discussion has been **modern-neural-first** — [[DenseRetrieval]], [[EmbeddingBasedRetrieval]], [[ContextualRetrieval]], [[rag]]. IIR fills in the **pre-neural foundation** those papers build on or supersede. [[BM25]] (Ch 11) and [[PageRank]] (Ch 21) are still load-bearing in production search a decade later; [[TfIdf]] and cosine similarity are the default "hello world" of any retrieval system; the evaluation vocabulary (MAP, NDCG, precision@K, kappa) is unchanged. Anyone reading the wiki's RAG / dense-retrieval line should be able to follow citations *backward* to this corpus to learn what was being replaced and why.

**The "language model" disambiguation.** Chapter 12's *language model* is the **2008-era statistical** sense — multinomial-unigram + Jelinek-Mercer / Dirichlet smoothing, used as a ranker via query likelihood P(q | M_d). It is NOT the neural-LM sense the rest of the wiki uses (autoregressive transformer decoders). The shared term but separate intellectual lineage is the single most important contradiction-class to surface in the [[LanguageModel]] concept page when consolidation happens.

**Connections to other corpora.** Strong forward connections: Ch 13 ([[NaiveBayes]]) connects to the existing [[NaiveBayes]] page (text-classification depth); Ch 15 ([[SupportVectorMachine]]) connects to [[islr-seventh-printing|ISLR]]'s SVM chapter; Ch 16–17 ([[KMeansClustering]] / [[HierarchicalClustering]]) connect to existing concept pages; Ch 18 ([[LatentSemanticIndexing]]) is the conceptual ancestor of modern embedding-based retrieval and of [[ContextualEmbedding]]; Ch 21 ([[PageRank]]) is the spiritual ancestor of [[google]]'s search ranking. Backward connections: the [[InvertedIndex]] arrives explicitly here (Ch 1–4) and is now a properly motivated concept rather than a bare term.

**Pending consolidation.** Chapter agents wrote ONLY their source pages — they did NOT create concept or entity stubs, did NOT update the main `LanguageModel` / `NaiveBayes` / `KMeansClustering` / `HierarchicalClustering` pages, and did NOT resolve case/spelling aliases (e.g. agent-introduced `[[ChiSquareTest]]` vs existing `[[ChiSquaredTest]]`, `[[MarkovChain]]` vs `[[MarkovModel]]`, `[[PowerIteration]]` vs `[[PowerMethod]]`). A follow-up curation pass should: (1) stub the ~70 highest-value unresolved concepts, (2) merge alias variants, (3) cross-link existing concept pages to the IIR chapter that grounds them, (4) extend the [[LanguageModel]] page with the statistical-vs-neural disambiguation.

## Rosetta Code programming-task corpus (added 2026-05-30 → 05-31)

A bulk ingest added **1350 source pages** — the complete canonical task set of [Rosetta Code's `Category:Programming Tasks`](https://rosettacode.org/wiki/Category:Programming_Tasks) (slug prefix `rc-`, grouped under the **Rosetta Code Tasks** section of [index](index.md)). It began as the 686 directly-listed pages of [`Category:Solutions by Programming Task`](https://rosettacode.org/wiki/Category:Solutions_by_Programming_Task) (680 ingested; 6 excluded — 1 `Talk:` page + 5 "NKT/NKTg Law" spam entries), then expanded to cover the **670 further tasks** that live only in that category's 62 topic subcategories or the master task list (classics like *Quicksort*, *Ackermann function*, *A\* search*, *Amicable pairs*). The **405 *Draft* Programming Tasks were intentionally excluded** (lower-maturity/incomplete). Each page captures one task's specification, its concrete requirements, the breadth of its cross-language solution coverage (tasks are implemented in tens to hundreds of languages — e.g. *100 doors* has 378), and `[[wikilinks]]` to the CS/math concepts it exercises. Pages were fetched via the MediaWiki API (`action=parse`, section-0 wikitext + section headings) because WebFetch is 403-blocked on the site. Ingest mechanics: **one Opus 4.8 subagent per page, run strictly sequentially** by a deterministic, self-correcting workflow loop (skip-if-exists + pending-list regeneration made it resumable across several Anthropic usage-limit cutoffs).

**How this corpus relates to the rest of the wiki.** It is largely a **reference / breadth corpus** orthogonal to the ML/IR core: an algorithmic-and-CS-fundamentals index spanning number theory, string processing, data structures, graphics, parsing/compilers, recursion, and esoteric puzzles. Its strongest natural bridges to existing content are foundational CS concepts that also underpin the ML corpus — e.g. [[EditDistance]] / [[LevenshteinDistance]], [[MarkovChain]], [[DynamicProgramming]], string-search adjacent to the [[InvertedIndex]] line, and the general [[Simulation]] / [[NumberTheory]] / [[Recursion]] vocabulary. Most concept links are intentionally **dangling**: per the bulk-ingest design each subagent wrote ONLY its one source page (no concept/entity-page minting, no `overview` rewrites, bookkeeping batched into a reindex of `index.md` + `log.md`), so a future curation/graph pass can decide which recurring task concepts ([[PerfectSquares]], cipher/crypto, [[CellularAutomaton]], etc.) deserve their own pages and how to cross-link them to the ML-side concepts.

## Stanford CS324 "Large Language Models" course corpus (added 2026-06-04)

A full ingest of the **14 lectures of [Stanford CS324, Winter 2022](https://stanford-cs324.github.io/winter2022/lectures/)** ("Large Language Models", Liang / Hashimoto / Tatsunori et al.) — slug prefix `cs324-`, raw provenance under `raw/cs324/`, one dedicated Opus 4.8 subagent per lecture run **strictly sequentially**, with the shared layer (index / log / this overview / entity+concept pages) consolidated by the orchestrator afterward to avoid write races. The arc: **Introduction** (LMs as token distributions; Shannon → n-grams → neural → [[Transformer]]; [[InContextLearning]], [[EmergentAbilities]], [[FoundationModel]]) → **Capabilities** (what [[GPT-3]] can do, all from next-word prediction) → **Harms I/II** ([[PerformanceDisparities]], [[SocialBias]], [[Toxicity]], [[Disinformation]]) → **Data / Security / Legality** ([[WebText]]/[[C4]]/[[ThePile]] + [[Datasheets]]; [[DataPoisoning]]/[[DifferentialPrivacy]]; [[Copyright]]/[[FairUse]]/[[FairLearning]]) → **Modeling / Training / Parallelism / Scaling Laws** ([[Tokenization]]/[[Attention]]; [[AutoregressiveLanguageModeling]] vs [[MaskedLanguageModeling]], [[Adam]]/[[Adafactor]]; [[DataParallelism]]/[[ModelParallelism]]/[[PipelineParallelism]] → [[3DParallelism]] via [[Megatron]]/[[GPipe]]/[[ZeRO]]; power-law [[ScalingLaws]]) → **Selective Architectures** ([[MixtureOfExperts]]: [[SwitchTransformer]]/[[GLaM]]/[[GShard]]; retrieval: [[RAG]]/[[RETRO]]) → **Adaptation** ([[Probing]]/[[FineTuning]]/[[PromptTuning]]/[[PrefixTuning]]/[[Adapters]]/[[LoRA]]) → **Environmental Impact** ([[LifeCycleAssessment]], [[CarbonEmissions]], [[StrubellEtAl]] vs [[PattersonEtAl]]).

**How this corpus relates to the rest of the wiki.** CS324 is the **pedagogical scaffolding** around the same foundations that anchor the *AI / LLM research corpus* (§II): its Modeling/Training lectures are the lecture-hall version of [[1706.03762-attention-is-all-you-need|Attention Is All You Need]], [[1810.04805-bert|BERT]], and [[1910.10683-t5|T5]]; its Scaling Laws lecture directly extends the [[2001.08361-scaling-laws|Kaplan et al. 2020]] paper already in the wiki (and pre-dates the Chinchilla revision flagged there); its Parallelism lecture is the systems backdrop to [[2205.14135-flashattention|FlashAttention]]. Unlike the Rosetta and IIR bulk ingests (which left concept links dangling), this ingest **mints curated entity/concept pages** for the genuinely new, recurring nodes (models/systems like [[Gopher]], [[GPipe]], [[SwitchTransformer]], [[RETRO]]; harms/legal/env concepts like [[Toxicity]], [[Copyright]], [[CarbonEmissions]]) while reusing the many that already existed ([[Transformer]], [[GPT-3]], [[ScalingLaws]], [[MixtureOfExperts]], [[RAG]], [[FineTuning]], …). Author-year citation links (e.g. `[[VaswaniEtAl2017]]`, `[[Koenecke2020]]`) are intentionally left as dangling provenance forward-refs rather than minted as stubs.

## *Introduction to Machine Learning Systems* (mlsysbook Vol 1) book corpus (added 2026-06-05)

A full ingest of all **16 chapters of [*Introduction to Machine Learning Systems*, Volume 1](https://mlsysbook.ai/vol1/)** ([[VijayJanapaReddi|Vijay Janapa Reddi]], [[Harvard]]; open-access, 2026) — slug prefix `mlsysbook-`, raw `.qmd` provenance under `raw/mlsysbook-vol1/`, **one dedicated Opus 4.8 subagent per chapter run strictly sequentially**, with the shared layer (index / log / this overview / ~300 concept + 43 entity pages) consolidated by the orchestrator afterward to avoid write races. The book is organized in four parts: **Foundations** ([[mlsysbook-ch01-introduction|Introduction]] → [[mlsysbook-ch02-ml-systems|ML Systems]] → [[mlsysbook-ch03-ml-workflow|ML Workflow]] → [[mlsysbook-ch04-data-engineering|Data Engineering]]) → **Build** ([[mlsysbook-ch05-neural-computation|Neural Computation]] → [[mlsysbook-ch06-network-architectures|Network Architectures]] → [[mlsysbook-ch07-ml-frameworks|ML Frameworks]] → [[mlsysbook-ch08-model-training|Model Training]]) → **Optimize** ([[mlsysbook-ch09-data-selection|Data Selection]] → [[mlsysbook-ch10-model-compression|Model Compression]] → [[mlsysbook-ch11-hardware-acceleration|Hardware Acceleration]] → [[mlsysbook-ch12-benchmarking|Benchmarking]]) → **Deploy** ([[mlsysbook-ch13-model-serving|Model Serving]] → [[mlsysbook-ch14-ml-operations|ML Operations]] → [[mlsysbook-ch15-responsible-engineering|Responsible Engineering]] → [[mlsysbook-ch16-conclusion|Conclusion]]).

**The unifying thesis: ML systems have a *physics*.** Reddi's distinctive contribution to the wiki is a quantitative, systems-engineering worldview anchored by a handful of recurring invariants the book applies to every topic: the **[[IronLawOfMLSystems|iron law]]** ($T = D_{vol}/BW + O/(R_{peak}\cdot\eta_{hw}) + L_{lat}$), the **[[DAMTaxonomy|D·A·M taxonomy]]** (every bottleneck lives on the Data, Algorithm, or Machine axis), the **[[RooflineModel|roofline]] / [[ArithmeticIntensity|arithmetic-intensity]]** lens, the **energy-movement invariant** (a DRAM access costs ~100–10,000× a MAC), and — in the [[mlsysbook-ch16-conclusion|Conclusion]] — the **[[ConservationOfComplexity|conservation of complexity]]** meta-principle (complexity moves between D/A/M, never disappears) and the **[[ThirteenQuantitativeInvariants|thirteen invariants]]**. Five **[[LighthouseModel|Lighthouse Models]]** (ResNet-50, GPT-2/3, [[DLRM]], MobileNet, [[KeywordSpotting|keyword spotting]]) recur as worked bottleneck exemplars, and the [[DeploymentSpectrum|deployment spectrum]] (cloud → edge → mobile → [[TinyML]]) spans ~9 orders of magnitude in power/memory.

**How this corpus relates to the rest of the wiki.** mlsysbook is the **systems-physics complement** to the wiki's practitioner-oriented production-ML texts: it covers much of the same surface as [[dmls-ch07-model-deployment|Chip Huyen's *Designing Machine Learning Systems*]] (data engineering, deployment, monitoring, compression, MLOps) and the *AI Engineering* successor, but motivates every decision from energy, bandwidth, and the iron law rather than from serving recipes. Its Build chapters re-ground the wiki's existing [[Backpropagation]] / [[Transformer]] / [[GEMM]] / [[MixedPrecisionTraining]] pages in hardware terms; its Optimize chapters deepen [[Quantization]] / [[Pruning]] / [[knowledgedistillation|distillation]] / [[NeuralArchitectureSearch|NAS]] with the conservation-of-complexity and theory-vs-practice-gap framings; its Deploy chapters add a first-principles **[[QueuingTheory|queuing-theory]]** foundation for serving and a quantitative **[[MLOps]]** treatment (retraining economics, drift-detection physics, the [[OuraRing|Oura]]/[[ClinAIOps]] case studies); and [[mlsysbook-ch15-responsible-engineering|Responsible Engineering]] reframes [[ResponsibleAI]] as measurable D·A·M constraints ([[Fairness]], [[CarbonFootprint|carbon]], [[EuropeanAIAct|EU AI Act]]). Authored entirely by [[VijayJanapaReddi]], who also leads [[MLPerf]]/[[MLCommons]] — making the [[mlsysbook-ch12-benchmarking|Benchmarking]] chapter the wiki's authoritative MLPerf source.

---

- Run `python tools/health.py` first every session — structural integrity is cheap to check.
- `python tools/lint.py` finds orphans, contradictions, stub-promotion candidates.
- `python tools/build_graph.py` rebuilds `graph/graph.html` from the current `[[wikilinks]]`.
- For new sources: drop into `raw/` (any of 20+ formats — see [[2605.03808-agentic-imodels]]'s autoresearch ethos for inspiration) and ask Claude to ingest.

## *The Fuzzing Book* — Software Test Generation ([[AndreasZeller|Zeller]], [[RahulGopinath|Gopinath]], [[MarcelBohme|Böhme]], [[GordonFraser|Fraser]] & [[ChristianHoller|Holler]] — [[CISPA]] 2024; **complete, 30/30 content chapters**, added 2026-06-06)

The wiki's first dedicated **software-test-generation / [[Fuzzing|fuzzing]]** corpus: all 30 content chapters of the open-source textbook at fuzzingbook.org, ingested one source page per chapter (`fuzzingbook-01`…`fuzzingbook-30`). It is the runnable-engineering counterpart to the [[Hypothesis]] [[PropertyBasedTesting|property-based-testing]] cluster already in the wiki, developing every technique from Python on a common `Fuzzer`/`Runner` class architecture ([[fuzzingbook-03-fuzzer|Ch 3]]).

- **Part I — Whetting the appetite** ([[fuzzingbook-01-tours|Tours]], [[fuzzingbook-02-intro-testing|Intro to Testing]]): the book's reading-paths map plus testing fundamentals — [[TestCase]], [[TestOracle]], [[Assertion]], [[Precondition]]/[[Postcondition]].
- **Part II — Lexical fuzzing** (Chs 3–8): blackbox [[RandomFuzzer|random fuzzing]] → [[Coverage]] feedback → [[MutationBasedFuzzing|mutation]] → [[GreyboxFuzzing|greybox]] ([[AFL]]/[[AFLFast]] [[PowerSchedule|power schedules]]) → [[SearchBasedTesting|search-based]] ([[GeneticAlgorithm]]/[[EvoSuite]]) → [[MutationAnalysis|mutation analysis]] (grading the *tests*, not the input).
- **Part III — Syntactic fuzzing** (Chs 9–16): [[ContextFreeGrammar|context-free grammars]] → efficient [[DerivationTree|derivation-tree]] [[GrammarFuzzer|fuzzing]] → [[GrammarCoverage|grammar coverage]] → [[Parser|parsing]] ([[EarleyParser|Earley]]/[[PackratParsing|PEG]]) → [[ProbabilisticGrammar|probabilistic]] and [[GeneratorGrammar|generator]] grammars → [[GrammarAwareGreyboxFuzzing|grammar-aware greybox]] ([[LangFuzz]]/[[AFLSmart]]) → [[InputReduction|delta-debugging]] reduction.
- **Part IV — Semantic fuzzing** (Chs 17–22): declarative [[ConstraintBasedFuzzing|constraints]] ([[ISLa]] + [[SMTSolver]]) → [[GrammarInference|grammar mining]] → [[DynamicTaintAnalysis|information-flow tracking]] → [[ConcolicExecution|concolic]] and [[SymbolicExecution|symbolic]] execution ([[Z3Prover]], [[KLEE]]/[[SAGE]]) → [[DynamicInvariant|dynamic-invariant]] / [[SpecificationMining|specification]] mining ([[Daikon]]).
- **Part V — Domain-specific fuzzing** (Chs 23–28): [[ConfigurationFuzzing|configurations]] + [[CombinatorialTesting|combinatorial testing]] → [[APIFuzzing|API]] fuzzing → [[TestCarving|unit-test carving]] → [[CompilerTesting|compiler]] fuzzing ([[DifferentialTesting]]) → [[WebApplicationFuzzing|web apps]] ([[SQLInjection]]/[[CrossSiteScripting|XSS]]) → [[GUIFuzzing|GUIs]] ([[Selenium]]/[[ModelBasedTesting]]).
- **Part VI — Managing fuzzing** (Chs 29–30): [[FuzzingAtScale|fuzzing at scale]] — [[CrashDeduplication|crash bucketing]]/[[CrashTriage|triage]] ([[FuzzManager]]) — and the statistical *when to stop* ([[GoodTuringEstimator|Good–Turing]]/[[SpeciesDiscovery|STADS]], [[ResidualRisk|residual risk]]).

Net: **+30 source pages, +157 concept pages, +35 entity pages**; [[Fuzzing]], [[Coverage]], [[Testing]] and the [[Hypothesis]]/[[PropertyBasedTesting]] cluster updated in place. No contradictions with existing wiki content.

## *Agentic Design Patterns: A Hands-On Guide to Building Intelligent Systems* ([[AntonioGulli]], Google 2025; **complete — 21/21 chapters + front matter + Appendices A–G & Conclusion**, added 2026-06-07)

The wiki's first dedicated **agent-engineering pattern catalog**: [[AgenticDesignPatterns|Gulli's book]] extracts 21 reusable, framework-agnostic patterns for building [[AgenticAI|agentic systems]], each ingested as its own source page (`agentic-design-patterns-ch01`…`ch21`) with hands-on examples in [[LangChain]]/[[langgraph|LangGraph]], [[crewai|CrewAI]], and [[GoogleADK|Google ADK]]. It is the applied-engineering complement to the wiki's [[ReasoningTechniques|reasoning]], [[rag|RAG]], and [[PromptEngineering|prompting]] clusters, framed around the book's [[AgentComplexitySpectrum|Level 0–3 complexity spectrum]] and [[ContextEngineering|context engineering]]. The Conclusion groups the 21 into four families:

- **Core execution & task decomposition**: [[PromptChaining]] (1), [[Routing]] (2), [[Parallelization]] (3), [[Planning]] (6), [[Prioritization]] (20) — sequencing, branching, concurrency, and goal-decomposition of work.
- **Interaction with the external environment**: [[ToolUse]]/[[FunctionCalling]] (5), [[ModelContextProtocol|MCP]] (10), [[RAG|Knowledge Retrieval]] (14), [[InterAgentCommunication|Inter-Agent Communication / A2A]] (15) — reaching beyond the model via tools, the [[A2AProtocol|A2A]] (horizontal) vs MCP (vertical) protocols, and grounded retrieval.
- **State, learning & self-improvement**: [[Reflection]] (4), [[MemoryManagement]] (8), [[LearningAndAdaptation]] (9), [[GoalSettingAndMonitoring]] (11), [[ExceptionHandlingAndRecovery]] (12), [[ResourceAwareOptimization]] (16), [[ReasoningTechniques]] (17), [[EvaluationAndMonitoring]] (19), [[ExplorationAndDiscovery]] (21) — self-correction, [[SemanticMemory|semantic]]/[[EpisodicMemory|episodic]]/[[ProceduralMemory|procedural]] memory, fault tolerance, cost-aware [[DynamicModelSelection|model selection]], and autonomous discovery ([[GoogleCoScientist]], [[AgentLaboratory]]).
- **Collaboration & human oversight**: [[MultiAgentCollaboration]] (7), [[HumanInTheLoop]] (13) / [[HumanOnTheLoop]], [[Guardrail|Guardrails]] (18) — agent ensembles, human approval/escalation, and layered safety.

Appendix A is a full [[PromptEngineering|prompting]] survey ([[StepBackPrompting|step-back]], [[AutomaticPromptEngineering|APE]]/[[DSPy]], [[RolePrompting]]); Appendices B–G cover [[ComputerUse|computer-use/GUI agents]], the framework landscape ([[autogen|AutoGen]], [[LlamaIndex]], [[Haystack]], [[SemanticKernel]], [[awsstrands|AWS Strands]]), [[Agentspace|Google AgentSpace]], CLI coding agents ([[claudecode|Claude Code]], [[GeminiCLI]], [[Aider]], [[copilotcli|Copilot CLI]]), and the [[CodingAgent]] team pattern.

Net: **+24 source pages, +58 concept pages, ~16 new entity pages**; dozens of existing pages augmented in place ([[ToolUse]], [[Planning]], [[Reflection]], [[ModelContextProtocol]], [[rag|RAG]], [[PPO]]/[[DPO]], the prompting cluster, framework entities). Resolved many pre-existing dangling forward-links (e.g. creating [[InterAgentCommunication]] and [[EvaluationAndMonitoring]] fixed ~30 inbound links). Only framing tensions (not contradictions) recorded — Gulli's constructive stance on LLM [[Planning|planning]]/[[TreeOfThoughts]] vs the wiki's Kambhampati/LLM-Modulo critique.
